  - `ui/home.lua` takes `init({ hosted = true })` for this, and then leaves alone what belongs to its host: it does not compile the tree or announce, and on its way out it does not reset the events, shut the card log, clear the chunk cache or drop the global table. It also exports `requestClose()`.
  - A widget call is stopped at the instruction limit where a tool script is yielded, so a page that builds a lot in one call is stopped part-way and finished over the following passes. The pages and the tool's interface are dropped from the module cache when it closes; the rest stays, because the dashboard's own modules share that cache.
  - A theme that binds its own controls opens the tool with the `openTool` action, through `ctx.action` or a `ctx.keys` binding, and `bin/themes/validate.lua` accepts it as one of the widget's actions.
  - Once the tool is closed the theme shows again, or the quick menu over a theme that does not take full screen. A view of the theme's whose condition held when the tool was opened and still holds is not opened again, as after any other press that finishes with the menu.
- **Urban draws its own full screen pages (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`, `docs/dashboard/README.md`)**:
  - On a widget with theme views, Urban draws the quick menu and the battery picker in its own look, and adds two pages of its own: *Profile & Tuning*, opened by a tap on the profile row, with the in-flight tuning surface and the battery profiles, and the *ELRS* link page, opened by a tap on the link bars or held open by a switch chosen on the *Top Bar* page. What each entry offers and what a press does stay the widget's.
  - A fourth settings page, *Keys*, chooses what the page keys, MDL, SYS and TELE do in full screen, the suite's tool among the choices; a key set to open a page closes it again. Left alone, SYS opens the tool, TELE the quick menu, and the page keys and MDL do what the widget does without a theme binding.
  - The top bar carries a second button beside the menu button, which opens the suite's tool. The tool opens only while the model is disarmed; while it is armed the button is drawn grey and a press, or a key set to the tool, does nothing.
  - The pages look like the screen they open over: no grey strip, a title in the largest face up to 1.4 times the top bar's lettering that fits beside the close box, with a 1 px line under it, buttons drawn as a 2 px outline with the choice in force in the ok colour, and a close box twice the earlier size (72 px above a screen height of 350 px, 36 px below) drawn as an outlined X rather than a red box. The text takes the flight view's face from the same rule, so a 480x320 screen no longer gets the 480x272 sizes, and rows grow toward the close box's size where the screen has room.
  - A switch on the *Look* page, *Frames on tap areas*, covers the frame the radio draws around every button a script creates -- light blue in its default theme -- on the full screen and its pages; it is on by default, which draws what was drawn before. The radio offers no way to switch that frame off or recolour it, so the buttons get square corners and lines in the area's colour over the frame.
  - The battery picker draws its packs in two columns from two packs up, the pack name one face above the flight view's, with *NO BATTERY* in its own row at the foot. Where the packs need more rows than the room above it holds, the pack grid scrolls -- by a swipe, or by the rotary encoder moving from pack to pack -- with the cells at the same size, so every pack can be picked. The link page draws its names as labels and its figures large, and sizes the name column against every name, so *TPWR* is no longer cut to *TPW*.
- **Setup > Telemetry opens on its sensor groups, and counts the 40 slots while you choose (`app/pages/setup/telemetry/page.lua`)**:
  - The page lists the sensor groups -- Battery, Voltage, Current, ESC 1 and the rest -- each with how many of its sensors are on, and a group opens on its own sensors only; Back returns to the list. It used to build every sensor of every group at once, about ninety switches on one scrolling page.
  - A line on both levels reads *Sensors: n / 40*: the slots a save would fill, counting the sensors the page does not list but keeps in their slots. Above 40 it turns to the warning colour and says that more than 40 cannot be saved, as a sensor is switched on rather than only when Save is pressed. The line and the save check are the same count.
  - Saving is unchanged: one write of all 40 slots, whichever group is open, and switches changed in several groups are saved together. The group titles are translated; the sensor names are not changed.
- **Urban's Keys page says whose each choice is (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/configure.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`)**:
  - The list a key is set from mixes what the dashboard does on every theme -- the quick menu, the main menu, leaving full screen -- with the two pages Urban draws itself, and nothing told them apart. Each choice but *Nothing* now opens with *Suite:* or *Theme:*. The stored choices are unchanged.
- **Flight Tuning > Advanced > PID Controller offers Error Decay Stick Gain on MSP API 12.10 (`app/pages/flight_tuning/advanced/pid_controller/page.lua`, `tasks/msp/api/pid_profile.lua`)**:
  - The setting firmware 4.7 adds to the PID profile (`error_decay_gain_cyclic`, rotorflight/rotorflight-firmware#457): the further the cyclic stick is deflected, the faster the roll and pitch I-term decays, so it does not wind up while the stick is held against a helicopter that cannot follow. 0 to 250, 0 is off. The row and its help line appear only on a flight controller that reports MSP API 12.10 or later.
  - The PID profile read takes the new last byte whenever the reply carries it, and the write sends it only to MSP API 12.10 or later, so a flight controller on 12.09 receives the same 43 bytes as before. The documentation page for PID Controller is new.
- **Setup > Ports offers SRXL2 ESC on MSP API 12.10 (`app/pages/setup/ports/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/ports.md`)**:
  - A serial port can be set to *SRXL2 ESC*, the Spektrum SRXL2 link that drives the motor and returns the ESC telemetry over one wire. It is offered from MSP API 12.10 (Rotorflight 4.7) on, like the firmware's own list; on an older flight controller the list is unchanged, and a port already set to it is still shown as *Custom (2097152)* and kept on save.
- **The CRSF sensor input of firmware 4.7 can be set up from the radio (`app/pages/setup/ports/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/sources/`, `docs/pages/setup/ports.md`, `docs/pages/setup/power/sources.md`)**:
  - *Setup > Ports* offers *CRSF Sensors* as a port function from MSP API 12.10. Before, a port the flight controller had set to it showed as *Custom (4194304)*.
  - *Setup > Power > Sources* offers *CRSF* as the voltage and the current source from MSP API 12.10. Before, a source set to it showed as *Unknown (4)*. The help and the new documentation page say that the source needs a port set to *CRSF Sensors*.
- **Flight Tuning > Tune Advisor reads what the flight controller measured in flight and suggests the next change (`app/pages/flight_tuning/tune_advisor/`, `tasks/msp/api/tune_advisor.lua`, `docs/pages/flight_tuning/tune_advisor.md`)**:
  - Firmware 4.7 measures, while the heli flies in rate mode, how it answers the sticks on each axis, and reports it with `MSP2_GET_TUNE_ADVISOR` (0x5F10). The page reads one axis at a time every 2 seconds and shows the rate flight measured, the response against the rate asked, the bounce-back after a stop, up to three suggested changes and the reason for each. It changes nothing by itself.
  - The rules and their wording are the Ethos suite's Tune Advisor page. Each change is named the way the page it points at shows it -- *PIDs > Roll > FF*, *Rates > Roll > Max Rate*, *Advanced > PID Controller > Cut-off point > Roll* -- with the values in that page's units.
  - The star button clears the measurements on the flight controller (`MSP2_CLEAR_TUNE_ADVISOR`, 0x5F11) after asking.
  - The tile needs MSP API 12.10 and is greyed out below it. A flight controller that answers the read with an error -- a 12.10 firmware from before the command, or a board built without it -- shows *Needs newer firmware*, and the page stops asking until Reload. The read completes on that error reply rather than retrying it like a lost link, which is what lets the page tell the two apart.
- **Diagnostics > CRSF Sensors shows what the flight controller hears from a CRSF sensor accessory (`app/pages/tools/diagnostics/crsf_sensors/`, `tasks/msp/api/crsf_sensors_status.lua`, `app/manifest.lua`, `app/pages/init.lua`, `docs/pages/tools/diagnostics/crsf_sensors.md`)**:
  - A read-only page for flight controllers with MSP API 12.10, which reads `MSP2_GET_CRSF_SENSORS_STATUS` (0x5F0B) about once a second: whether a serial port has the CRSF Sensors function, the port's received bytes, sync bytes, frames with a good and with a bad checksum, the last frame's type and length, and the latest GPS, battery, barometer, cell voltage and RPM values the accessory sent.
  - The values change in place, so the page keeps its scroll position; it is rebuilt only when a group starts or stops reporting or the number of cells or RPM values changes. A read that goes unanswered replaces the values with *No reply from the flight controller* and the page keeps asking. On an older flight controller the entry is greyed out and the message is never sent; a firmware that reports API 12.10 without the message answers it with an error, and the page says the firmware does not support it instead of waiting for a reply. A record in a payload version other than 1 gets the same answer, rather than numbers decoded from a layout the page does not know.
- **Cell count and cell voltages per battery profile, on firmware that keeps them per profile (`tasks/msp/api/battery_config.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/battery/`, `tasks/events/telemetry_bg/smart.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/power/battery.md`)**:
  - Rotorflight firmware 4.7 keeps the cell count and the min, max, full and warning cell voltages once per battery profile and appends them to `MSP_BATTERY_CONFIG` behind the six capacities; its single fields then carry the active profile only. The suite read and wrote only the single fields, so on such a board the Battery page edited the active profile's cells while showing them as if they applied to every profile.
  - The read takes the five per-profile blocks where the reply carries them, and a save writes them back behind the capacities. They are recognised by the reply's length, not by the MSP API version, which the firmware reported as 12.10 for weeks before the blocks existed; a board without them is read and written exactly as before.
  - On the Battery page a new *Edit Battery* selector, shown only on such firmware, chooses which profile the cell count and the four cell voltages show and edit. It opens on the active profile and is not sent to the flight controller, so editing another profile's cells never makes it active; *Selected Battery* still alone switches the profile. Within a profile each voltage stops at its neighbours -- Min below Max, and Min <= Warn <= Full <= Max -- because the flight controller changes a profile that is out of that order itself when it stores it.
  - The suite's own readers of the cell count and cell voltages -- the local SmartFuel estimate, the voltage callouts, the flight record's sag detector, the dashboard -- follow the `BatP` sensor: when it names another profile, the configuration's single fields are set from that profile's values, which is what the board then reports for them. The sensor reports the profile the board is running, so a change made while armed is followed at disarm, when the board applies it.
  - The help texts and the documentation no longer call *Max cell voltage* a high-voltage alarm level: the flight controller has no such alarm, and uses the value to work out the cell count and to scale the charge level when no capacity is set.
- **Urban's battery picker shows each pack's cycle count, and gives the packs the room NO BATTERY took (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/battpick.lua`, `pickview.lua`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`)**:
  - The line under a pack's name reads capacity, cycles and battery profile, e.g. *1100 mAh - 7 cycles - P2*; the profile is left out where no board profile matches the pack. The cycle count is the registry's, counted up by the pack's first flight.
  - *NO BATTERY* stays in its own row at the foot, but holds one line: it is as tall as the close box, drawn as an outline, and marked in the ok colour where it is the answer in force rather than filled. The height it no longer takes goes to the pack cells.
- **Settings > Audio > Events: an *On Connect* page, *Fuel* becomes *SmartFuel*, and a `?` on every row (`app/pages/settings/audio/events/`, `ui/controls.lua`, `docs/audio/events.md`)**:
  - A new *On Connect* page holds everything said once when a model connects: *Model Name*, *Pack Not Full* with its *Margin*, *Battery Capacity*, and *SmartFuel* (the level spoken once after connecting). The *Battery* and *Other* pages are gone, and *Pack Not Full* has left the *Voltage* page; the categories go from ten to nine. The settings keep their keys in the preferences file, so nothing a pilot has set changes.
  - The *Fuel* page and its master switch are called *SmartFuel*, the name of the feature on *Setup > Power* that produces the value it announces.
  - Every row on the nine pages has a `?` button with a short explanation of that row; the page help says what the page is for. `Controls.appendRadioSwitch` takes the same optional `helpText` / `helpTitle` / `onHelp` as the number and choice controls; a switch without them is drawn as before.
- **Urban gets a telemetry page with up to twelve tiles (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`)**:
  - In full screen a tap on the value rows opens *Telemetry*: up to twelve readings as tiles, three to a row, each with its name, its figure and, where the flight record keeps them, the least and the most the flight reached (`min .. max`, a side the record does not keep as `-`). A key can open it as well, chosen on the *Keys* page.
  - The tiles are chosen on a fifth settings page, *Telemetry*, from the catalogue the five value rows choose from, so a tile shows a reading as its row does -- name, figure, colour and the *Units beside the values* setting. Nine are on by default (voltage, cell voltage, current, capacity used, fuel, headspeed, ESC and MCU temperature, BEC voltage); a tile set to nothing is left out and the others close up.
  - The range is the flight record's, read as the widget's boxes read it: the flight in progress where it has a value, the last flight otherwise. It is empty until the model has been armed once and covers the armed time only. A tile whose reading the host derives only on request (ESC load, ESC status, air rate, rate floor) is declared with the flight view's sources. `bin/accounting/measure.lua` reports `theme.urban` 19 instructions higher (worst pass 14032 to 14051, the sweep unchanged) and every other row unchanged; the 19 are the value panel's build, which now asks whether it is drawn in full screen and draws its separators through one branch for either case.
- **A fullscreen view may name the readings it needs, and they are read only while it is open (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`, `docs/developer/dashboard-views.md`)**:
  - A theme's view module may have `sources(zone, state)`, a list of sources like a phase module's. While the view is on top in fullscreen, the derived snapshot resolves that list after the theme's own; on every other surface it does not. Until now a reading only a view showed had to be declared by the phase module, and was then read on every telemetry pass of the phase, with the view closed.
  - The combined list is made once per view module and theme load, so a standing view costs two comparisons a pass; a theme whose views name nothing is unchanged.
- **Urban's rows and tiles can show the radio's telemetry sensors the widget has no reading of its own for (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`)**:
  - Twenty-six readings join the list the value rows and the *Telemetry* tiles choose from, each by the name the suite's decoder gives the sensor: BEC temperature, tail speed, vario, TQ, transmitter power, the ESC's BEC temperature, BEC current, the ESC's voltage, current, capacity, RPM, PWM and load, GPS satellites, speed, altitude and distance, pitch, roll and yaw, the flight controller's CPU, system and real-time load, bus and MCU voltage, and RSNR.
  - A sensor is declared to the host only while a row or a tile shows it. On a tile its range is the radio's own least and most of the sensor (its `-` and `+` forms), since the flight record keeps none of them. The defaults show none of them, so `bin/accounting/measure.lua` reports every row unchanged.
- **Urban's telemetry page names what its tiles read itself (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`)**:
  - The tiles' readings move from the flight view's `sources` to the telemetry view's own `sources(zone, state)`, so on a widget with view sources a tile's sensor and its range are read only while the page is open, not for the whole flight. The flight view's list names only what the flight view draws again. On a widget without view sources a tile whose reading is not a fixed state field reads `-`. `bin/accounting/measure.lua` reports every row unchanged.
- **The pilot chooses what the quick menu shows, and in which order (`app/pages/settings/dashboard/quick_menu/`, `widgets/dashboard/quick_menu_order.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/fullscreen_menu.lua`, `lib/preferences.lua`, `docs/pages/settings/dashboard/quick_menu.md`, `docs/dashboard/quick-menu.md`)**:
  - A new page, *Settings* → *Dashboard* → *Quick Settings*, has one row per place in the dashboard widget's quick menu, from the top, each offering every entry the menu has and a dash for an empty place. Saved, the widget draws the menu in that order with its next reload of the settings.
  - An entry chosen twice counts at its first place. A chosen entry still hides where it does not apply -- in-flight tuning with its preview off, the battery prompt without a pack, the tool while armed -- because the menu asks each entry's condition on top of the choice.
  - Until the page is saved with another order the menu is exactly what it was. The choice is one setting, `dashboard.quick_menu`: absent for the default, the ids in order otherwise, and empty for a menu with every entry taken out.
  - A theme that draws the quick menu itself with `ctx.list("quick")` -- Urban does -- follows the pilot's order. `ctx.entry(id)` and a list handed to `ctx.menu` still reach every entry the menu has, whether the pilot has put it in the quick menu or not.
- **The quick menu and Urban's keys can open the Flight Log page directly (`widgets/dashboard/fullscreen_menu.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/quick_menu_order.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/views.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/tool_host.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/`, `bin/themes/validate.lua`, `docs/dashboard/quick-menu.md`, `docs/pages/tools/flight_log.md`)**:
  - A new quick menu entry, **FLIGHT LOG**, opens the suite's tool inside the widget on its *Flight Log* page rather than on its menu, and the back key on that page closes the tool again, because the pilot came from the dashboard. It is not in the menu as it comes: a pilot adds it on *Settings* → *Dashboard* → *Quick Settings*. It is listed only while the *Flight Log* preview is on and the model is disarmed, the two conditions under which the tool offers the page itself.
  - Urban's *Keys* page offers *Suite: Flight log* as a choice for every key. A key set to it does nothing while the preview is off or the model is armed.
  - The action behind both is `openTool:<menuId>`, which `ctx.action` and `ctx.keys` take as well. It opens only a page `views.lua` lists, while that page's condition holds, and does nothing otherwise; `ui/home.lua` takes `init({ hosted = true, landing = <menuId> })` and opens the page through its root entry and its menu, under the menu's own conditions, falling back to the menu where a step fails. `openTool` without a page is unchanged.
- **Urban opens a battery page from a tap on the battery gauge (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/battview.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/layout.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/configure.lua`, `bin/themes/validate.lua`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`)**:
  - In full screen, a tap on the battery gauge opens a *Battery* page: the cell voltage as a bar on a scale marked at the flight controller's minimum, warning and full cell voltage, with the three written out under it; the pack as a battery laid on its side with the fuel figure, the cell count and the used capacity; and a foot line with the pack voltage, the lowest cell voltage of the flight and the reserve. The limits and the reserve are the flight controller's, read on connecting, and the page rebuilds once when they arrive or change; until then they read `-`.
  - A key can open the page too: *Theme: Battery* is a new choice for every key on Urban's *Keys* settings page, and the key closes the page again while it is showing. No key is set to it by default.
  - The gauge on the flight screen is drawn where and as it was. In full screen it is built as the children of the button that opens the page, because a rectangle hands a press on to the object it was built into; the widget zone, and a widget without theme views, build it as before.
  - `bin/themes/validate.lua` no longer reports a rectangle among a button's own children as drawn over that button's press; a rectangle drawn over a button as a separate node is still reported.
- **The dashboard keeps a short history of what changed on the craft, for a theme to show (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`)**:
  - `state.eventLog` lists the last thirty transitions, oldest first, each with the radio's clock as `HH:MM:SS`: arming and disarming, the flight controller connecting and the connection ending, every change of the governor state, and every change of the speed controller's verdict (`esc_status_live`). An entry carries a kind and the value it is about rather than words, so a theme names it in the pilot's language; the speed controller's entry carries the controller's own verdict, which is already translated.
  - Nothing detects a transition for the history alone: each kind is noted where the widget already acts on it -- the arm edge, the connect and disconnect edges -- or after the telemetry read that brought it. The speed controller's entries come from the verdict the active theme already declares, so a theme that does not show it gets none, and nothing reads the two sensors for the history. The list is kept across connections and dropped with the widget.
  - `bin/accounting/measure.lua` reports `pass.state` 16 instructions higher (7825 to 7841) and the rows built on it by the same 16 (`pass.state.armed`, `pass.tuning.state`, `pass.tuning.prime` and the worst pass of every theme but Urban); the 16 are the two comparisons a pass that read the telemetry makes. Nothing is drawn by it: no shipped theme shows the history in this change.
- **Urban gets a status page with an event log (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`)**:
  - In full screen a tap on the governor row or the status line of the left panel opens *Status*: the arm state in large letters with the reasons that block arming under it, or *Ready to arm*, beside the governor, the throttle and the speed controller's live verdict, and under both the *Event log* -- the widget's history of what changed on the craft (`state.eventLog`), newest first, each entry with the radio's time. A key can open it as well, chosen on the *Keys* page (*Theme: Status*).
  - Every figure on the page is a reading the theme already had: the page declares no sources of its own and is not rebuilt for a reading or for a new log entry.
  - `bin/accounting/measure.lua` reports `theme.urban` 11 instructions higher (worst pass 14071 to 14082, the sweep unchanged) and every other row unchanged; the 11 are the left panel's build, which now asks whether it is drawn in full screen and draws the separator inside the new tap area as a line. The page itself is not a row there: counted the same way on its own, building it takes about 2 400 instructions and its closures about 900 a frame at 800x480, the same with an empty log and with thirty entries.
- **Urban's ELRS page shows both directions of the link (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/`, `lib/link_rates.lua`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`)**:
  - The page opened from the link bars now has a row each for RQ, TQ, 1RSS, 2RSS (once a second antenna has been seen), TRSS, SNR and TPWR, every one a bar with its figure, the air rate with its modulation beside the title (`500Hz (LORA)`) and the flight's least link quality at the foot (`RQ min`, from the flight record). The skipped-frame row and the rate floor line are gone; the floor still sets where the signal bars stand.
  - TRSS is measured against the same sensitivity floor as the receiver's antennas. SNR shows the receiver's and the transmitter's figure (`9 / 8dB`), its bar from -10 dB to +10 dB with steps at 4 dB and 0 dB, and reads `-` on an FLRC or FSK rate, which reports no signal-to-noise ratio. TPWR's bar is the power's share of a new *TX power limit* setting on the *Top Bar* page (25 mW to 2000 mW, default 100 mW), amber from 60 % and red from 85 % of it, because dynamic power raises the power as the link weakens.
  - The air rate, TRSS, RSNR and TSNR are the page's own `sources`, so on a widget with view sources they are read only while the page is open in full screen. `lib/link_rates.lua` gains each rate's modulation and `LinkRates.modulationOf(rate)`.
- **Back on a page with unsaved changes asks before leaving (`ui/home.lua`, `ui/loading_overlay.lua`, the configuration pages' `page.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`)** (#223):
  - Back on a page with a change that is not saved no longer discards it without a word. A box, *Unsaved changes*, offers **Stay**, **Save** and **Discard**. Stay -- and Back on the box -- keeps the page with its changes; Save saves it exactly as the header's Save does and the page stays open; Discard leaves without writing. *Confirm on Save* does not ask a second time after Save in the box.
  - The question is put only where the header offers Save, never while the model is armed or a save of the page is still on its way, and not when the page goes without a Back on it (the link was lost, or the tool hosted by the dashboard closed). Trims, Swash Geometry and both Servos pages, which send a change live under their override before Save, do not ask.
  - A page takes part through an optional `hasUnsavedChanges()` hook; 60 configuration pages answer it from the state that draws their *Unsaved changes* line. `docs/reference/saving.md` lists them. `ui/loading_overlay.lua` draws a box with several buttons (`actions`), side by side or, where a label does not fit, one under the other.
- **The Flyrotor ESC page has a help sheet behind the `?` (`app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/flrtr/`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/flrtr.md`)**:
  - One line per row, in the order the page shows them, for the *Section* on screen: *Basic*, *Advanced* and *Governor* each have a sheet of their own, because the seventeen rows together are more than a 480x320 sheet holds. The page passes the section it shows to the sheet (`M.onHelp`), as the Rates page passes its rates type.
  - The page declares `help = true` itself, so `?` is live on the first visit and not only after a `?` press elsewhere has created the help registry.
  - What the lines say about the electrical angle, the starting torque, the ESC mode and the governor gains follows Rotorflight's own *Flyrotor Governor setup* page; the documentation page carries the same facts and now links that page.

### Bug Fixes & Improvements
- **No battery profile 1 is announced before telemetry answers (`ui/home.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`) (fixes #684)**: The telemetry state defaulted the battery profile to 1, so a reconnect announced profile 1 before the real profile arrived. It now stays unset until the `battery_profile` sensor answers.
- **ELRS to Rotorflight sync stops reading parameters during its write (`app/pages/tools/diagnostics/elrs_link/elrslink_task.lua`) (fixes #669)**: The state stayed `read` while the telemetry write was in flight, so late parameter replies re-ran finalize and queued duplicate writes. The state is now `write_rf` for the write, and the arming refusal covers it.
- **Cell voltages from 1.00 V to 5.00 V are decoded (`lib/audio.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/battview.lua`) (fixes #668)**: Three decoders read 100 centivolts as 10.0 V, and the audio range started at 2.0 V. 100 centivolts now decode to 1.00 V, 1000 is read as millivolts because centivolt cells are at most 500, and audio accepts 1.00 to 5.00 V.
- **SmartFuel config rejects truncated replies (`tasks/msp/api/smartfuel_config.lua`) (fixes #647)**: A reply shorter than four bytes was read as a table of zeros, which could be shown and written back. The parser now returns nil, which the readers already treat as a failed read.
- **Cross Coupling Ratio stops at the firmware's 200 (`app/pages/setup/controls/adjustments/page.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/inflight/functions.lua`) (fixes #662)**: The flight controller clamps the ratio to 200 (`src/main/fc/rc_adjustments.c:216`), so a range above 200 was saved and silently clamped. The adjustment range now ends at 200.
- **Motorized tail trim and limits show their percent sign (`app/pages/setup/mixer/tail/page.lua`) (fixes #652)**: The `%` suffix was part of the `string.format` template, which failed on a trailing `%` and fell back to the raw unscaled value. The suffix is now appended after formatting.
- **ERASE BLACKBOX on the dashboard's quick menu asks before it erases (`widgets/dashboard/fullscreen_menu.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/views.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/confirm_menu.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/dashboard/quick-menu.md`, `docs/developer/dashboard-views.md`, `bin/dashboard/verify_erase_confirm.lua`) (fixes #108)**:
  - Erasing the flight controller's blackbox cannot be undone, and the row sat one tap from the flight line with nothing in front of it: a mis-tap on a touchscreen took the logs before the pilot could look. The row now carries a `confirm`, and `M.run` holds its press behind it, so tapping the row raises a question and queues nothing; ERASE performs the erase and its follow-up and leaves full screen, and CANCEL -- or a short press on RTN, through the view's `back` -- performs nothing and puts the menu back. A question that cannot be raised leaves the press unperformed rather than erasing unguarded.
  - The question is a view of its own (`confirm_menu.lua`) on the same stack as the menu and the picker, so the menu stands under it and declining returns to it as it was. It names what is about to be lost where the flight controller has reported the storage summary, and it is the shared vocabulary the entry list was always meant to carry: `confirm` is resolved in `views.lua` for any entry, the menu's own or one a theme draws.
  - The row is unchanged otherwise -- a press still queues the erase and the summary read and then does `done` -- and the confirmation adds no MSP traffic of its own. `bin/accounting/measure.lua` reports every row unchanged, and `bin/dashboard/verify_erase_confirm.lua` checks the whole path offline, its `--self-test` proving the checks go red for a row that erases without asking.
- **`rfsuite.batteryPick.open()` called while the dashboard widget is in its zone now opens the picker on the next entry into fullscreen (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`, `docs/developer/dashboard-views.md`) (fixes #453)**:
  - The documentation said the call puts the picker back the next time fullscreen is entered. The call opened it on the view stack, which is fullscreen state, and the next pass without an event -- every pass while the widget is not fullscreen -- dropped it again, so a call from a theme, which runs in the zone, only forced a rebuild. The call now also leaves a request of its own on `state.batteryPick`, and the next fullscreen pass opens the picker from it; the per-pass drop of the stack is unchanged.
  - The request lapses where the model is armed by then or has disarmed since the call, and a reconnect drops it with the rest of the prompt's state, so a picker asked for before a flight does not appear after it.
  - Its own share of the cost, counted offline with the accounting stubs against the tree without it: +4 instructions on every fullscreen pass while no request is waiting, the first one after a theme change included, and nothing on a zone pass. With the fullscreen theme entry above, a legacy fullscreen pass costs +11 with the menu up and +15 with the picker up, and +143 on the first fullscreen pass after a theme change.
- **The tool announced a lost connection again every two minutes, and reset its sensors five times a second while it waited for one (`ui/home.lua`, `lib/audio.lua`, `docs/audio/events.md`) (fixes #425)**:
  - The tool's home loop runs its audio block at 5 Hz and tested the connection with a reading rather than a latch. The branch for "not ready" had no edge test of its own, so it announced the loss, reset the audio state, called `Sensors.reset()` and cleared the telemetry fields **five times a second** for as long as the condition held -- no model powered up, the connect chain still running, or the link up and no battery reading arrived. The dashboard widget has gated the same call on the edge of its link for some time; the tool now does the same, and the readings that decide the edge stay per tick.
  - What it cost: `Sensors.reset()` empties every matched path, every miss record, every back-off, the `getFieldInfo` cache and the first-search budget, so each of those ticks threw away what the tick before had learned and sent the next one back to the top of each source's list. The back-off for a source this radio does not carry had no chance to act in between -- it was cleared before the next tick could read it.
  - What the pilot heard: `announceConnectionLost()` latches on `connectionLostPending`, but `resetConnectionState()` takes that latch away again once the 120 s recovery window has passed, and the branch called the two in that order on every tick. Driving the shipped `lib/audio.lua` under both call patterns over 400 s, with the model armed and the link up: **four** announcements on the old pattern, **one** on the edge. The page in `docs/audio/events.md` had described the intended behaviour all along -- once for the loss, again when it answers -- so the repetition was a departure from the documentation rather than a documented feature.
  - **The master volume keeps following the setting while the tool waits.** Gating the whole pass on the edge left the global variable written once per loss and then not again, so a pilot who set a global variable as the master volume sat at the alert level until the next connect. While the tool stayed in that state the level also **pulsed**: the next tick re-armed the pending loss and the write after it took the level back down, on the same 120 s cycle as the announcement. `Audio.refreshConnectionVolume()` is a third door into `lib/audio.lua` for exactly this, and the tool's waiting branch calls it.
  - The 18-sensor poll moved inside the same gate. While the tool is not connected those sensors answer nothing, and polling them at 5 Hz only to write `roundInt(nil, fallback)` defaults over the fields the reset had just cleared was the same waste seen from the other side. `state.telemetryState` now stays clear until real values arrive.
  - One consequence in the tool's favour: the carried-over fuel check that holds back the first fuel callout of a new session now works there as it always did in the widget, where the previous value used to be overwritten by the clear on the very next tick.
- **Main Power Lost, its recovery and Pack Not Full say their own words (`src/rfsuite/audio/`, `bin/sound-generator/json/`, `docs/audio/events.md`)**:
  - `lib/audio.lua` plays `stat/alerts/mainpower.wav`, `mainpowerok.wav` and `notfull.wav`, and neither pack carried them, so each spoke a neighbouring file instead: `batteryempty.wav` or `lowvoltage.wav`, `evt/battery.wav`, and `voltage.wav`.
  - The two new phrases are declared in both word lists and all three files are built for `en` and `de`, with the voice each language already uses.
  - `stat/alerts/batteryempty.wav` shipped in the English pack only and in no word list. It says "Battery Empty", the same phrase as `lowbat.wav`, and is now declared in the English word list. The German pack does not gain it: `mainpower.wav` now comes ahead of it in both packs, so it could never be reached, and a German pack that predates this change goes on falling back to `lowvoltage.wav`.
- **A build that could not go red now can (`.vscode/scripts/resolve_i18n_tags.py`, `bin/package/build_package.py`, `.github/workflows/checks.yml`, `.github/workflows/pr.yml`) (fixes #286)**:
  - Four gaps composed into one outcome: a package could be built wrong, the step that would have noticed could not report it, and the workflows that publish did not run the checks that existed. Nothing below is visible on a radio; all of it decides whether a wrong artefact reaches one.
  - **An unknown `--lang` is refused instead of warned about.** A locale that is not a bundle skipped the whole i18n stage and still wrote a ZIP at exit 0, so a run against a mistyped locale shipped every marker standing. `--lang fr` now exits 2 and writes nothing, and the guard around the i18n stage refuses for the same reason rather than skipping silently.
  - **The resolver has an exit status.** `main()` returned `None` on every path, so `sys.exit(main())` was `sys.exit(None)` — exit 0, whatever happened. The replacement count was doing two jobs as well: a file that could not be written reported `0` replacements, the same number a file needing no change reports, so a run in which every write failed was indistinguishable from a clean tree. `process_file` now returns its write failures and `main()` exits 1 on unresolved keys or failed writes, which is what the packager's `check=True` was already reading as a check.
  - **The packager takes a post-condition.** After the i18n stage it counts the markers in the staged tree against the count before it, and refuses when the stage ran, markers were there, and none were resolved. The invariant is *nothing was resolved while something had to be*, not *zero markers*: three markers survive every legitimate build, all three of them guards that look for the marker itself. A zero-marker post-condition would have failed every correct build.
  - **The gates run where things are published.** Three `pr.yml` checks move into `checks.yml` as a reusable workflow, and `push.yml`, `release.yml` and `snapshot.yml` run it as a `needs:` of the job that builds or publishes. A red check now stops the release instead of annotating it. The **documentation rule is deliberately the one that stays out**: it can be satisfied by a `Documentation:` line in the pull request body, and a push or a tag carries no body, so from there it falls back to demanding a file under `docs/`. Measured over the last fifteen commits on master that is seven of them, and all three of `snapshot/0.1.4`, `0.1.5` and `0.1.6` are of that shape -- so the merge that added the job would have stopped a release tag on any of them. It remains in `pr.yml`, the only caller with a body to offer.
  - `pr.yml` gains one job: the resolver's own self-test. It had only ever run in `checks.yml`, so a self-test that failed on `ubuntu-latest` reached master unbroken — its read-only case restored the file with `os.chmod(target, stat.S_IWRITE)`, which on POSIX leaves it `--w-------` with every read bit cleared, so the self-test's own read-back raised. The same call passed on Windows, where it only toggles the read-only attribute, and the job would first have run on the push that merged the change. The restore now puts back the mode the file had, and the case fails by name if the file is not usable afterwards rather than raising and taking the remaining cases with it.
  - `deploy-rfsuite-demo.ps1` called the resolver four times and checked no exit status, then reported success onto a radio. Those calls now throw on a non-zero status.
- **Twenty-two strings a pilot reads now reach a German install, in the two `?` help sheets, SmartFuel, the motor-override warning and the async load notice (`app/pages/settings/dashboard/inflight/help.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/inflight/help.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/smartfuel/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/motor_override/help.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/motor_override/page.lua`, `app/pages/lib/async_load_ui.lua`, `docs/developer/i18n.md`)**:
  - All twenty-two keys were already written and already translated. What was missing was the step that turns a key into text on an installed card, because **a packaged card carries no locale bundle**: the packager copies `src/rfsuite/` without `i18n/` and puts back `i18n/init.lua` alone, so on the card a runtime `i18n.t(key)` answers with the key and the pilot gets the **fallback** the precompiler baked into the marker. A string the precompiler did not rewrite was therefore English in every locale, on a build that had the German sitting in `de.lua`.
  - Three call shapes had to change, and none of them is visible on a desktop: a wrapper **named** `t` or `pageText`, because the precompiler's pattern knows those two names; a **prefix to derive**, from a `pageT` call, a `keyPrefix` local or a `buildSimplePage` key, or the whole rewrite is skipped for the file; and a **fallback that is one string literal**, because the pattern reads it out of the call and a `..` chain is not a string it can take.
  - Both help sheets drop their hand-rolled `tr` for the house `Common.pageT("...")` form every other help sheet already uses, and their namespaces are the ones the translations were already in -- no key moved. The SmartFuel page's five help texts go through `pageText` directly, which is all its `pageHelpText` wrapper ever did.
  - The motor-override warning reads the same either way now: the name and the prefix were right, and a fallback joined up over three lines was the only thing in the way.
  - The async load notice is a shared module, and the block its key lives in is `diagnostics_info`. All four of its callers hand it a translator bound to their **own** page, and all four of those blocks carry the key in both bundles, so a source run always found it. A **packaged** card is the case that was broken: it carries no bundle, so all four callers took the inline English fallback. Naming the block fixes all four, and names the block the translation is really in rather than the caller's.
  - **The rules are written down** in `docs/developer/i18n.md`, which the developer index has been listing as *to write* since it was planned: the three shapes, why a packaged install has nothing to look in, what the translation gate does and does not read, and how to check a string by building the locale and reading the ZIP.
  - Verified by precompiling the tree before and after: **exactly 22 new markers**, in the same distribution the report gives them, no existing marker lost, and every key present in both locale bundles. Read back out of the built German package, all twenty-two are German. Reported as #284.
- **Localization no longer pins an automatically resolved language, and offers *Automatic* as the choice it stands for (`app/pages/settings/localization/page.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `app/pages/settings/localization/help.lua`, `docs/pages/settings/localization.md`)**:
  - `language` is an *optional* key, and it is its **absence** that means "resolve it": the resolution then reads the card's own baked locale on a packaged build, or the radio's language setting on a source or simulator run. The page gave that key a hard `"en"` default and wrote every key of its schema on save, so on a build that resolved to German the combo already read *English* before anything was pressed, and saving the page for any other reason -- a temperature unit, an altitude unit -- wrote that English into the settings file and made the displayed lie true.
  - **What the setting steers on a packaged card is the voice, not the screen.** The screen text of a packaged card is a literal by the time it is installed -- the packager resolves the labels into the chosen language, and the card carries no locale bundle to look in -- so a language saved here does not move a single label there; it chooses the language of the **announcements**, from the `SOUNDS/rf/<language>` folder. The tool's own language follows on a source or simulator run, at the next start of the tool. And even the voice is settled per Lua state rather than per save: an announcement resolves its folder once and keeps it, so one that has already spoken stays in the old language until that state is created again.
  - A card on which the old page already wrote `language = "en"` cannot be told apart from a chosen one, and this change does not remove it: **if you never chose a language, set it to *Automatic* once and save.**
  - The combo now carries **Automatic** as its first entry, and the page maps an absent key onto it rather than onto a default. The combo is therefore a statement about the file, and about nothing else where the file says nothing.
  - Saving writes the key only when a language was chosen, and choosing *Automatic* clears it again -- so the state the file documents is reachable from the page, which it was not before.
  - The control is the same save on another page: `Settings > General` never wrote under `[localizations]`, and a language that *is* stored stays stored through a save of this page.
  - The page's documentation is written with it, and names what the second unit setting actually does today. Reported as #275.
- **A theme's `t()` resolves the language once per settings change instead of on every call (`widgets/dashboard/themes/default/common.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/themes/@aerc/common.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/objects/common.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `lib/system_locale.lua`) (fixes #253)**:
  - Both theme commons asked `lib/system_locale.lua` for the language on every call of `t()`, and `t()` is reachable from a label text getter that is handed to `lvgl.build()`. A function in the firmware's reactive sweep -- on whatever instruction budget `refresh()` left over, outside the widget's own `pcall` -- was therefore reading a settings module. The rule that says a closure in that position reads precomputed state and its own compiled config, and makes no file probe, named these two files.
  - The language is now memoised in both theme commons and keyed on a generation counter that `lib/system_locale.lua` bumps when the preferences are reloaded. The two-second window that module already keeps bounds how stale an answer can be; the counter is what says an answer is *wrong*, which a window cannot. `bin/accounting/measure.lua` reports `sweep.text/blackbox` down from 340 to 308 instructions against a target of 500, and every call after the first is 32 instructions cheaper.
  - The object layer's own locale memo is in the same change because it is where the missing half was: nothing in the tree ever cleared it, so a language changed under *Settings › Localization* did not reach the dashboard until the widget's Lua state was rebuilt. It is keyed on the same counter, and so is the widget's own i18n context, which `runtime.lua` rebuilds in the preferences reload -- the one place that already knows a setting changed, and the place that reaches it through the `reload.req` sequence. Without that the theme would have spoken the new language while the runtime's labels and the menus kept the old one.
  - Which language a user ends up with is unchanged: the resolution was correct, only repeated. A `system_locale` without the counter -- a card carrying files from two releases -- is not memoised at all and keeps the old per-call behaviour.
- **Dashboard: the governor box no longer reports "throttle off" over a hovering helicopter (`widgets/dashboard/objects/text/governor.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`)**:
  - The flight controller runs a governor state machine in modes DIRECT, ELECTRIC and NITRO only. In OFF and LIMIT it never enters one, so the governor state sensor stays at its initial value -- throttle off -- from power-up to landing, and the box showed that for the whole flight with nothing on screen to say why.
  - In those two modes the box now shows the mode instead, *Gov. Off* and *Gov. Limit*, under the names the mode is set by. It reads the `gov_mode` the connect chain already fetches once over MSP, so nothing new is asked of the flight controller; until that has answered, the box behaves exactly as it did.
  - The arming-disabled reason and the disarmed label still come first, and the state sensor is still what is shown in DIRECT, ELECTRIC and NITRO. A box carrying a `thresholds` list keyed on governor state names will not match in these two modes, because the text is no longer a state name.
- **MIN RPM and MIN CURRENT are the floor of the flight, not of the spool-up (`tasks/events/telemetry/flight_record.lua`, `docs/reference/flight-statistics.md`) (fixes #221)**:
  - The two minima were tracked across the whole armed window. A pilot arms first and spools up afterwards, so the first readings inside that window are the lowest ones it will ever carry and they stayed the minimum for the rest of the flight: `MIN RPM` on a postflight tile showed a spool-up or spool-down sample, and `MIN CURRENT` showed the current at arming, which is in practice zero. The maxima beside them were right and are unchanged — nothing about a ramp can raise them.
  - They are now taken only while the rotor is under power, which the flight controller's own governor state decides: `ACTIVE`, where the governor is holding the requested headspeed, and `BYPASS`, where the throttle curve drives the head directly. Spool-up, recovery, throttle idle, throttle hold, autorotation and bailout are ramps or are not driving the head. `FALLBACK` is excluded as well, because the flight controller enters it when the motor rpm signal has failed and a headspeed sampled there is not a headspeed.
  - The gate has to hold for 2 s before a minimum is taken. `ACTIVE` is entered at 99 % of the requested headspeed, so the readings behind the transition are already flight readings and the wait only removes a sample taken on the edge of one.
  - With the governor off or limiting the throttle only, the flight controller never moves the governor state, and a receiver may not be sending that sensor at all. A gate on the state alone would take no minimum at all on those models, so the state is trusted only once a value inside its own range has been seen in the flight; until then the gate is headspeed at or above 100 rpm together with throttle at or above 25 % or current at or above 1.5 A. The throttle-or-current half is the rule this suite already applies to the same question when it draws a logged flight; the headspeed term is added here. Its limit is documented: without a governor state nothing says the head has settled, so a slow spool-up on such a model can still reach the minimum.
  - Without a governor state the gate is also held on its way out: a reading is taken only once the gate has held for another 2 s after it. The head can slow before the current falls — the start of a spool-down, a head bogging under load — and while current is still drawn such a reading passes the gate, so the last 2 s of a driven stretch never count. A driven stretch shorter than 4 s therefore leaves no minimum on such a model. The governor state needs no such wait: the flight controller leaves `ACTIVE` and `BYPASS` on the throttle input, before the headspeed falls.
- **A reconnect no longer carries the last session's readings into the new one (`tasks/events/telemetry/flight_record.lua`, `docs/reference/flight-statistics.md`)**:
  - The flight record keeps each reading from one sample to the next, so a sensor that answers nothing in a pass leaves its previous value standing. When a link came up, the record's statistics were cleared but those readings were not, so a model connected without a sensor the previous session had — ESC temperature, BEC voltage, altitude, link quality — inherited that session's last value for it, and the value went into the new flight's maxima and minima as if it had been read.
  - Every sampled reading now goes back to the value a fresh start has when a link comes up. Within a session a reading is still left standing, as before.
  - The inherited readings would also have decided the powered gate above: an inherited governor state such as throttle hold would have kept it shut for the whole session, so no headspeed or current minimum would have been taken at all, and an inherited headspeed and throttle would have opened it on a head nothing was driving.
- **Five contracts that looked load-bearing and were wired to nothing (`tasks/msp/runtime.lua`, `tasks/msp/save_pipeline.lua`, `tasks/msp/api/rpm_filter_v2.lua`, `tasks/msp/api/`, `app/pages/`, `docs/reference/saving.md`) (#306)**:
  - **An open page reads again when a different flight controller answers.** Eighteen setup pages compare `rfsuite.session.signature` to decide whether what they read still belongs to the connected board, and nothing ever wrote it. The MSP runtime now writes it from the API version and the MCU id. It changes only when a board identifies itself differently, and keeps its value while no board is identified, so a link that drops and comes back to the same board does not discard edits that have not been saved.
  - **A dismissed save reports its outcome when its page is opened again.** A save that reboots the flight controller can be dismissed once its settings are stored, and finishes without a screen. The ten pages that start one claimed the held outcome from `onActivate`, which the host never calls, so it was never shown. They claim it when they load now, and the outcome appears in the same box a watched save reports in.
  - **The RPM filter v2 module builds a write the firmware accepts.** `MSP_SET_RPM_FILTER_V2` takes the axis first and then sixteen notches, 65 bytes; the module sent 64 and every write would have been refused. Its simulator reply now has the four bytes per notch its parser reads. No shipped page loads this module.
  - Removed without a change in behaviour: `M.onLoad` and `M.onActivate` on 53 pages and `M.getModuleTitle` on 12, none of which anything calls, and the line on 28 pages that copied a `syncHeaderTitle` the page context never carries. The 27 API modules no shipped page loads now say so in their header.
- **The SmartFuel value is handed over in state rather than through the radio's sensor table (`tasks/events/telemetry_bg/smart.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry/flight_record.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `lib/audio.lua`, `docs/reference/telemetry-sensors.md`) (#227)**:
  - The suite worked the remaining fuel out, published it as the `SmFt` telemetry sensor, and then read that sensor straight back in the same Lua script that had computed it. The dashboard's telemetry read, the per-flight statistics and the spoken announcements all run in the state that computes the value, so they now take it directly, under `rfsuite.session.smartfuel`.
  - **`SmFt` and `SmCp` are published exactly as before.** They are the export for what the suite cannot hand a value to: logical switches, special functions, the radio's own telemetry screens and the telemetry log. A warning a pilot has built on `SmFt` is untouched, and `Bat%` is not a substitute for it, because the reserve is applied on the way to `SmFt` and not on the way to `Bat%`.
  - **The dashboard no longer shows a different quantity where the sensor cannot be created.** A model that does not carry `SmFt` made the write land nowhere, and the read behind it fell through the search path to `Bat%`, which is a different quantity because the reserve is not applied on the way to it — with nothing saying so.
  - **The percentage keeps the precision it was computed with.** A telemetry sensor here carries no decimals, so the trip through `SmFt` rounded the value to whole percent before its own dashboard saw it again. A reading can therefore now differ from `SmFt` by one. The estimated consumption is still handed over as a whole mAh, which is what the sensor publishes and what every consumer of it asks for.
  - **Two smaller consequences of that, both of them a pilot can see.** A gauge or readout whose theme floors the value can read one percent lower than before; one that does not say how to round it is unchanged, because the default rounds. And the empty-fuel alert, which fires at 0 %, now fires when the estimate actually reaches 0 rather than when it rounds to it, so it comes up to half a percent later.
  - **`SmFt` and `SmCp` can now be switched off, per flight controller (`app/pages/setup/power/preferences/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/power/preferences.md`).** *Configuration* → *Setup* → *Power* → *Preferences* gains *Publish SmFt / SmCp*. **It is on by default, which is what the suite has always done**, so nothing a pilot has built on those sensors changes; what is new is that a machine whose sensors nothing reads can stop spending two telemetry slots and a model write per update on them. Until the hand-over above existed the publication was the only way the value reached the dashboard and could not be switched off at all.
  - The spoken fuel percentage is rounded where it is spoken. `playNumber` takes an integer and raises on anything else, so without that the alert tone would have played with no number behind it.
- **The Flight Log page says where the pilot is, and the battery editor keeps the focus (`app/pages/tools/flight_log/page.lua`, `lib/flight_log.lua`, `docs/pages/tools/flight_log.md`) (fixes #371)**:
  - **Creating a battery no longer loses the form to the first keypress.** The tab row was the first child of every build, so a rebuild put the focus ring on the *Flights* tab while the screen showed the new-battery form, and one ENTER there discarded the form and left for the empty flight list. The row is now appended after the body — drawn in the same place, reached in the right order — so the ring lands on the first control of whatever the page is showing.
  - **The battery editor is the whole page while it is open.** The tabs are not drawn over it: a press on one of them throws a half-typed pack away, and the editor's own *Save* and *Cancel* plus Back are the ways out of it.
  - **The current tab is drawn as the active one**, and the two views that are not tabs — a single flight and a pack's detail — carry a heading naming the flight's date and time and the pack's name. Until now four identical buttons and a column of value rows were all the page said about where the pilot had got to.
  - **A pack's model list can hold more than one model again.** The *Models* field was sanitised by the rule for a single model name, which strips commas, so `Goblin,Raw` was stored as one unmatchable name and the pack silently stopped being offered for either craft. The field now keeps `,` and `*`, trims each name and drops an empty one.
  - **Renaming or deleting a pack takes the *battery for the next flight* with it.** The choice is stored as an id, so the old one named no pack: the list offered it as unknown and refused to re-select it, while the flight was still logged against an id the registry no longer had.
  - **Reload on a single flight's detail no longer reports an empty log.** It cleared the flight it was showing and stayed on the view, which then drew "No flights recorded yet" on a radio whose log is full.
  - Pressing the tab that is already active rebuilds nothing, and a tab press now clears the model filter as well as the page number, so the tab leads to its own list rather than to the list somebody had filtered earlier.
  - Removed a page-edited flag that nothing read. The page has one place to write to and writes it on *Save*, so the flag could not affect anything.
- **One press of RTN steps one level, and closing the on-screen keyboard no longer abandons the form under it (`ui/home.lua`) (fixes #371)**:
  - A back key reaches a tool script twice for one press: once as a raw event when it goes down, and once when it comes up, either through the page element's own `back` callback or -- on a screen holding nothing focusable -- as a raw break event. The tool acted on both, so a single press ran the back handler twice. Only the branch that leaves a page was protected against that, which is the one branch where a second run is harmless; every branch above it ran unguarded, and a page's own back hook is the one that owns a ladder of levels. So one press stepped two: a pack's detail view in the flight log went to the flight list instead of the pack list, and an open pack editor was abandoned *and* its tab changed under the pilot.
  - Only the release edge and the long press are acted on now, and the press that was accepted is timestamped where it is accepted rather than only where it ends up, so a radio or a screen that still produces both routes has the second one dropped by the guard that already existed. Long-press behaviour is unchanged, and which edge an event carries is read from its flag bits instead of from a hard-coded event number, so it no longer depends on which `EVT_*` names a given radio exports.
  - **The same press edge was the one that fired while the on-screen keyboard was open.** The release goes to the keyboard, which closes itself and forwards nothing, so the press edge was the only thing reaching the page -- and a pilot who typed into a text field and pressed RTN to put the keyboard away lost the form underneath it. That press now closes the keyboard and nothing else.
- **Both ESC protocol lists are built from the MSP API version the board reports (`app/pages/setup/esc_motors/throttle/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/telemetry/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/throttle.md`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/telemetry.md`) (fixes #296)**:
  - The Throttle page's protocol combo writes the position in its list, so the list has to be the flight controller's own enum. `SRXL2` -- added to the firmware in front of `DISABLED` and available from API 12.10 -- was not offered at all, so on a 12.10 board the page showed `DISABLED` for a board running SRXL2, picking `DISABLED` wrote SRXL2 instead of switching the motor output off, and the board's own `DISABLED` could not be selected. The tail of the list is appended entry by entry now, so `DISABLED` keeps whatever number the board gives it. The `CASTLE` gate also moves from API 12.07 to 12.08, which is where the firmware and the Configurator put it; that half changes nothing on any supported board, because the oldest API this package supports is 12.08.
  - The ESC Telemetry page's protocol list had no API gate at all and wrote `RECORD` as a constant 14. `XDFLY`, `FrSky F.BUS` and `SRXL2` were each added to the firmware in front of `RECORD`, so that number is RECORD on one firmware generation, SRXL2 on the next and undefined on the one before. On an API 12.08 board `FrSky F.BUS` selected RECORD and `RECORD` selected a protocol the board does not define; on a 12.10 board `RECORD` selected SRXL2 and the board's own RECORD was unreachable. Each optional entry now carries the API version it arrived in, and `RECORD` takes the number that follows them.
  - The Update frequency and the three throttle endpoint values are offered for `SRXL2` as well, which is what the Configurator offers for it.
  - Both pages now have a documentation page; neither had one.
- **AM32 refuses a parameter block from another ESC family (`tasks/msp/api/esc_parameters_am32.lua`) (part of #291)**:
  - The AM32, BLHeli_S and Bluejay tiles are lit by one and the same ESC telemetry protocol, so whichever of the three a pilot opens is the one that reads. The AM32 module guarded only the length of the reply (`#buf < 50`), and a 66-byte BLHeli_S block passes that test: the page then drew that ESC's EEPROM under AM32 names and a Save wrote the AM32 meanings back into those bytes.
  - The block does carry the family in its first byte — the module already read it as `esc_signature` — so the module now declares `mspSignature = 0xC2` (`ESC_SIG_AM32` in the firmware) and refuses a block that does not carry it, which is the guard the other nine modules were given. A reply the module refuses is `nil`, which the page already treats as "not read", so Save stays blocked rather than sending a guess.
  - `docs/reference/saving.md` lists the pages that refuse a foreign block; AM32 joins that list, and so does Flyrotor, which already carried the guard and was missing from it. With AM32 fixed the list is all ten pages, and the page says so.

- **Copy Profiles asks what it is about to overwrite, says why it refused, and offers only the profiles the board has (`app/pages/tools/copy_profiles/page.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `docs/pages/tools/copy_profiles.md`) (fixes #299)**:
  - A copy replaces a whole PID or rate profile on the flight controller and nothing anywhere holds what was there before. The only thing standing in front of it was the generic *Do you want to save the changes?*, which says nothing about what is lost and which a pilot can switch off under *Settings* > *General*. The question now names the profile that is about to be overwritten and the one it is copied from, and it is asked whether or not that preference is on.
  - A page may now supply the words of its save confirmation, and say that they are not optional: `M.getSaveConfirm(ctx)` returns `{ title, message, always }` and `ui/home.lua` puts that text in the dialog it already raises. A page that needs no question keeps the generic one, and nothing changes for it. Where the arming state could not be read, that warning is not replaced -- the page's question is appended to it, so the pilot reads both.
  - The confirmation stays in the host on purpose: the save dispatcher re-checks the page, its read state and the armed state at dispatch, after the answer and before anything is written. A page that raised its own dialog and wrote from the callback would sit outside all three, and the firmware copies a profile while armed -- only the switch to it is armed-gated.
  - Where the page requires the question and no dialog can be shown, nothing is written and the pilot is told, rather than the save proceeding on the assumption that the answer would have been yes.
  - SAVE is greyed out while source and destination are the same profile, which is the state the page opens in since both lists start at profile 1; tapping it as the page loaded used to do nothing and say nothing. The page's refusals -- a missing flight-controller link, and the same-profile copy should it be asked for anyway -- are reported through the save notice the host already passes to every page instead of being dropped.
  - The two profile lists are built from the counts the flight controller reports in its status reply instead of always offering six. A board with 256 kB of flash or less has fewer -- three or two PID profiles, and as few as three rate profiles -- and its copy handler refuses an index beyond them silently, so picking profile 4, 5 or 6 on such a board produced a save that reported success and copied nothing. PID and rate profiles are counted separately, so changing the type rebuilds the two lists and brings the selection back into range.
  - The page's help text and a documentation page now say that the destination keeps nothing of its own settings and that there is no undo, and `docs/reference/saving.md` gains a section on what overrides the save confirmation.
- **The two Diagnostics pages that report telemetry now answer for what is arriving, not for what was once received (`app/pages/tools/diagnostics/validate_sensors/page.lua`, `app/pages/tools/diagnostics/info/page.lua`, `docs/pages/tools/diagnostics/validate_sensors.md`, `docs/pages/tools/diagnostics/info.md`) (#288)**:
  - **Validate Sensors judged a row with `getValue`, which cannot tell a live sensor from a stopped one.** Once the radio stops receiving a sensor, `getValue` answers integer `0` — a number — so a row nothing was sending read `OK`, which is exactly the answer the page exists to refute. The test is now `getSourceValue`, whose second return value says whether the sensor is still current: immediately false when the link drops, and false after that sensor's own twenty-second timeout when only it falls silent.
  - **A working Battery Cell Voltages row read `INVALID`.** `Cels` is registered with `UNIT_CELLS`, for which EdgeTX answers a table rather than a number, so the page's "is it a number" test rejected a sensor that was working perfectly. A table is a value and is accepted as one.
  - **The page had three states and two words for them.** The row colour already separated "the radio has no such sensor" (red) from "the radio has it and nothing is arriving" (orange), but both printed `INVALID`. The second state now reads `NO DATA`, and the in-app help says what each of the three words means.
  - **Info reported a link mode with no link.** `RFMD` reads `0` both when the model has no such sensor and when the radio has stopped receiving it, and the mode table has an entry for `0`, so RF Mode and RF Band read `25Hz` / `900Mhz` on a page a pilot typically opens because something is wrong. Both rows now read `-` unless the sensor is there and current.
  - **Info kept the previous helicopter's board for up to 45 seconds after a model change.** Variant, Board Info and Build Info are read over MSP and cached for the refresh interval, and leaving the page cleared neither the values nor the timestamp — so re-opening it inside that window issued no read at all and drew the last flight controller's answers with nothing on screen saying so. Leaving the page now drops both, so the next visit reads afresh.
  - **A row that needs two sensors read `NO DATA` when the radio had only one of them.** *Adjustment Function* is `AdjF` and `AdjV` together, and the middle state was taken from either one being present. `NO DATA` tells the pilot the radio already has the sensor and there is nothing to do but wait for frames, which is the wrong instruction for a sensor the radio does not have: the row now reads `NO DATA` only when the radio has both, and `INVALID` as soon as one is missing, which sends the pilot to *Discover new* instead.
  - Both pages have a documentation page for the first time, and the page index counts them.
- **XDFly, OMP and ZTW send the governor gains and the motor poles in the ESC's own encoding (`tasks/msp/api/esc_parameters_xdfly.lua`, `tasks/msp/api/esc_parameters_omp.lua`, `tasks/msp/api/esc_parameters_ztw.lua`) (fixes #295)**:
  - The parameter block these three share stores `gov_p`, `gov_i` and `motor_poles` one below the number Rotorflight's other configuration tools show for them. All three were declared as bare `U16` and neither `Api.parse` nor `Api.buildWritePayload` touched them, so the number on the page was the number on the wire: the page read each of the three one low, a save stored a word one high, Governor P Gain and Governor I Gain could not be set to their lowest step at all, and the highest step each page offers was one past the end of the range those tools present.
  - The shift is applied at the MSP boundary, so the pages are unchanged and go on working in the numbers a pilot reads. A save that edited nothing still puts every word of the block back exactly as it was read, including a word outside the range the page can reach.
  - `capacity_correction` is the fourth biased word of the same block and was already handled, on the page, in the display function of its own control. It is left there.
  - The three documentation pages say that the same ESC now reads one higher than it did on an earlier release.

- **ESC #2 temperature is decoded, so the sensors behind it in a frame are no longer dropped (`lib/rf2tlm_sensors.lua`, `docs/reference/telemetry-sensors.md`) (fixes #272)**:
  - `Es2T` (custom telemetry id `0x1057`) was the only row of the decoder table with no decoder. The frame walk cannot skip a sensor whose width it does not know, so it stopped at that point and discarded everything the flight controller had packed behind it in the same frame -- on every frame, for as long as the sensor was scheduled. Depending on where the scheduler placed it that could be the headspeed, which the dashboard, the governor tile and the flight record all read. The flight controller sends the value as one unsigned byte, and the row now says so.
  - The same row would also have raised `attempt to call a nil value` on the file's second entry point, the one that takes a list of requested rows and pre-creates each sensor by calling its decoder. Nothing in this tree loads the file that way — both loaders call it bare — so that path is unreachable here; it is reached in the sibling Lua scripts this file comes from. The row fix closes it either way.
  - *ESC2 Temp* is offered on the Telemetry page, so the sensor that cost the rest of the frame was one the suite itself invited the pilot to select.
  - **The custom telemetry sensors now have a reference page**, which they had none of: that the Telemetry page's catalogue and the decoder are kept in step, that a sensor the suite does not recognise stops the frame walk and costs everything the flight controller packed behind it in that frame — the shape this fix is an instance of, and the one a pilot on a newer firmware can hit again — how to work around it until the suite is updated, and what the `*Cnt` and `*Skp` counters mean. The Telemetry page links it.
- **The ELRS Link page refuses both of its write buttons while the model is armed (`lib/armed.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `app/pages/tools/diagnostics/elrs_link/page.lua`, `docs/pages/tools/diagnostics/elrs_link.md`) (fixes #335)**:
  - `lockedWhileArmed` answers whether a menu entry may be *entered*, and the header's Save and Reload answer for themselves. A button a page draws is covered by neither, and a page opened before the arming edge stays open across it with its buttons live -- so the two syncs on this page were reachable while the craft was in the air. *RF -> ELRS* is the one that got there: it writes the transmitter module through `crossfireTelemetryPush`, which is not the MSP queue and so is not covered by the pause the MSP runtime applies to that queue while armed either.
  - Neither sync writes while the model is armed. Pressing one puts no question up and sends nothing, the Status row reads *Unavailable while armed*, and the arming state is read again when the confirmation is answered, so arming the model while the question stands cancels the write rather than letting the answer carry it through. A guard that cannot load its own predicate refuses rather than waving the write through. *Probe* only reads and is unchanged.
  - **A press is not the write, so the refusal is in the task as well.** The module is pinged, then read parameter by parameter, and the `CRSF_FRAMETYPE_PARAMETER_WRITE` frames follow over the next several seconds one at a time -- so a check taken at the button covers the button and not the window after it, during which the buttons are greyed out and the pilot cannot intervene either. The task now abandons a transfer the moment the model is armed, at the one point every one of its callers passes through.
  - **That also covers the setup assistant's Link step, which had no armed check at all.** It drives the same task from three places -- both *use this* buttons and each picker's own `set`, the last of them with no confirmation in front of it -- and relied on its menu entry being locked while armed, which only governs whether the step may be entered. A step left open across the arming edge kept all three live. The comment that recorded that reasoning is corrected in the same change.
  - The two predicates the tool already used for this -- armed, and armed state unreadable -- were file-locals in `ui/home.lua` that nothing under `app/pages/` could reach. They are `lib/armed.lua` now, so a page-local write asks the same question the header's Save asks instead of growing a second, weaker test of its own.
  - Where the arming state cannot be read at all, the confirmation says so before it is answered, under the same key the header's Save uses, and does not block -- a save elsewhere in the suite asks the same question for the same reason.
- **A YGE ESC keeps the flag bits nobody edited (`app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/yge/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/yge.md`) (fixes #346)**:
  - The page took the ESC's flags byte apart into the four bits it sets -- *Direction*, *F3C Auto*, *Keep mAh*, and the 12 V BEC flag, which follows *BEC Voltage* reaching 12.0 V -- and built it again from zero on every Save, so every other bit the ESC keeps in that byte was written back as zero, including by a Save that edited nothing. Every other field of the block was already carried through from the read; the flags byte was the one that was reconstructed.
  - The four bits are now written into the byte that was read. Verified over all 256 values of that byte, driven through the page's own read reply and its own controls: a Save with nothing edited now puts back the byte it read and leaves the rest of the payload identical to the parsed block, where the same check on the previous code changed 240 of the 256. An edit made through one of the page's own controls now follows that control's own rule for its bit and leaves every bit above 3 alone, over all 256 read values and in both directions, where the previous code lost a bit above 3 in 480 of those 512 cases.
- **What an ESC page read does not outlive the visit any more (`app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/scorp/page.lua`, `.../am32/page.lua`, `.../flrtr/page.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/`) (fixes #354)**:
  - Both pages refuse a Save until the ESC has been read, and both asked that question by testing whether `parsedCache` holds anything. The page module outlives its own close -- `registry.release` runs `onClose` and keeps the module in its cache -- so that field answers *has a read ever succeeded on this module*, not *did this visit read*. A pilot who read the ESC, left the page and came back therefore passed the guard on the earlier visit's block as soon as the new read did not arrive, and that block went to the ESC together with everything edited since, reported as a successful save.
