  - The block is now dropped where the next read is queued and again when the page is left, and with it the values, the ESC's model, version and firmware and the session copy, all of which come out of the same reply; the page's own initial values come back from a snapshot taken at module load. It is the shape the other seven manufacturer pages carry.
  - On the AM32 page the earlier visit's block need not even belong to the same ESC, because *ESC Target* is cleared when the page is left and starts again at 0. Changing the target within one visit restarts the four-way connect sequence, which takes several seconds before the parameter read itself is queued; the block is dropped at the start of that sequence rather than at its end, so the window in between cannot be saved from either.
  - The Scorpion page reports the refusal as the translated sentence the other pages show, instead of the reason code `esc_not_read`.
  - The FlyRotor page already gated its Save on a per-visit flag and keeps it; what it did not do is drop what a reply left on the screen. A second visit whose read is refused or does not arrive showed the previous ESC's values, name and firmware until something replaced them, and now shows the page's own initial ones. All ten ESC Configurator pages behave the same way at a close.
  - **Documentation**: the AM32 and BLHeli_S pages get the files they never had, which were the last two ESC Configurator pages without one. `docs/pages/README.md` is repaired while it is open: seven page files that already existed were still listed as *to write* and unlinked, and its Status line counted 19 written where the tables held 15. Every row now agrees with the files in the tree -- 24 written of 93 reachable -- and that was recomputed rather than adjusted.
- **A telemetry sensor the radio is not sending is no longer taken for a reading of zero (`lib/sensors.lua`, `docs/reference/sensor-selection.md`) (fixes #270)**:
  - `getValue()` answers integer `0` for a sensor row the model still carries but which has not been delivered since the model was loaded, so such a row reads as a successful measurement of nothing. The search loop has tested for that since it was written; the alias fallback below it retried the same name without the test, and for almost every value the alias **is** the first search path -- `Bat%` for fuel, `Vbat` for voltage, `Hspd` for headspeed, `Curr` for current, `RQly` for link. So the loop rejected the dead row and the line below it adopted the very same row, and the value then answered 0 for as long as the model stayed loaded. The alias fallback and the direct read now apply the same test the loop does.
  - **What changes for the pilot, and it is narrower than it sounds**: the fuel callout and the flight log stop treating a dead `Bat%` as a real 0 %. The announcement path turns a missing fuel reading into its own sentinel and gates on it, so nothing is spoken; the flight record only latches `fuelSeen` on a number, so the minimum-fuel columns stay empty instead of being filled with a zero nobody measured. **The dashboard is unchanged, every tile of it**: its `setField` ignores a nil outright and every telemetry field it keeps is initialised to 0, so a value with no sensor behind it reads `0` there before and after this change. Making the dashboard show `--` is a separate change to a separate gate and is not attempted here.
- **The setup assistant reads the slot it is about to take and the channel it is about to write (`app/pages/setup_wizard/proc_radio.lua`, `app/pages/setup_wizard/radio.lua`, `docs/pages/setup_wizard.md`) (fixes #303)**:
  - The Profile channel needs two adjustment slots on the flight controller. The slot enumeration recorded only the FIRST empty slot and the plan then allocated the second function to the one after it without testing that it was empty, so on a board whose adjustment table has a hole -- an adjustment configured in a slot that comes after an empty one -- the write replaced the pilot's own adjustment. The row showed as ready, the write succeeded and nothing was said. Every empty slot is recorded now, the plan takes the next unused one per function, and a board with no second empty slot blocks the row the way a missing mode slot already did. The mode-range side of the same plan has carried that guard since it was written.
  - The assistant tells the flight controller absolute microsecond windows -- 1700..2100 for a mode, 988..2012 for the adjustment travel -- and what a channel actually puts on the wire is the mixer value after its output stage, which nothing read. **A channel reverted by an earlier setup turned the position the pilot named as armed into the bottom of the travel and put the other position inside the arming window** -- and on the throttle channel, whose hold line pins the motor by forcing MAX to the bottom of the travel, **the same revert put full throttle in exactly the position the pilot named as the lock**. The direction is now one function, and every line all three channel writers emit is multiplied by it; the completion criterion and the read-back that recovers the pilot's picked position take the sign from the same place, so the stored weight and its inverse cannot drift apart. The reading that looks like the obvious fix is not one: `getValue("chN")` is the mixer output before the output stage, so it cannot see a reverted channel either.
  - End points, subtrim, centre offset and an output curve are not a sign and no weight compensates them: at end points of 40 % full deflection is about 1700 microseconds, exactly the edge of the arming window. Such a channel is refused on the Write screen rather than written with a window it can never reach, it is no longer reported as laid out, and the channel screen says what is wrong and where to put it right.
  - The Profile channel's switch picker and the Throttle channel's governor picker offered every source the radio has -- sticks, pots, trims, logical switches, channels, GVARs, telemetry sensors -- because `SRC_SWITCH` is registered inside EdgeTX's `lvgl` table rather than as a global, so the expression guarding it always fell through to the all-sources fallback. Picking a non-switch stored nothing, Next went inert, and the paragraph that would have explained it was itself gated on a non-empty pick. Both pickers take the filter the model template in this tree already spells correctly, and both paragraphs now draw on an unanswered pick.
  - The setup assistant now has a documentation page; it had none.
- **The ESC Target control is gone from the pages that cannot address a second ESC (`app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/`) (fixes #298)**:
  - Seven of the ten ESC manufacturer pages -- FlyRotor, Hobbywing V5, OMP, Scorpion, XDFly, YGE and ZTW -- drew an *ESC Target* ESC 1 / ESC 2 combo whenever the flight controller reported more than one motor, and also whenever that read had not answered yet -- which is every model on the first paint, and permanently on any model where the read never succeeded. Picking ESC 2 cleared the page and read again, so the control looked alive, but the value reached neither the read nor the write: both went to the same ESC as before. A pilot who chose ESC 2, changed a setting and saved wrote to ESC 1.
  - Those seven talk to the ESC over `MSP_ESC_PARAMETERS`, which carries no ESC index, and the flight controller answers it from the single serial ESC-telemetry port. The one command that selects an ESC, `MSP_SET_4WIF_ESC_FWD_PROG`, switches the board onto 4-way forward programming, which these protocols do not use. The control is removed rather than implemented, and the combo is no longer drawn on those pages.
  - The AM32, BLHeli_S and Bluejay pages do send that command before the parameter exchange, and are unchanged.
  - The motor-count read those seven pages made when they opened existed only to decide whether to draw the control, and goes with it.
- **YGE motor timing is translated between the list and the ESC's own encoding (`tasks/msp/api/esc_parameters_yge.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/yge.md`) (fixes #292)**:
  - The *Motor Timing* row offers four automatic modes and six fixed advance angles, and its position in that list went to the ESC as the parameter word with nothing in between. The ESC does not number them 0 to 9: it spells the automatic modes 16 to 19 and the fixed angles 1 to 6, and defines nothing between the two ranges. Nine of the ten entries therefore programmed a different timing than the one their label names -- picking *Auto Efficient* set a fixed 0 degrees, and picking *18 deg* sent a word the ESC does not define at all. The same gap ran the other way, so the row could read *Auto Power* for an ESC set to a fixed angle.
  - Both directions are translated now, in the parameter module rather than in the page, and the two tables are deliberately not each other's inverse: the ESC spells the first automatic mode both 0 and 16, and reading folds those together while writing spells it 0.
  - A row still standing on the entry the ESC's own word was decoded to writes that word back rather than the canonical spelling of the same entry, so a Save with nothing edited leaves the ESC's own word in place. That holds for every word the ESC defines. A word it does not define -- which no version of this page can produce, but an older one could, and which the page therefore draws as the first automatic mode -- is replaced by the word for the mode the row is showing, because nothing below the page can tell such a word left alone from that mode deliberately picked.
  - The YGE page now has a documentation page; it had none.
- **AM32 ESC preserves timing advance wire encoding on save (`tasks/msp/api/esc_parameters_am32.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/am32/page.lua`) (fixes #282)**:
  - Saving the AM32 page always encoded the timing advance byte using the new format (`10 + 8 * value`), which corrupted legacy-encoded ESC configurations (raw bytes 0..3 were rewritten as 10, 18, 26, or 34). The parse now tracks whether the timing advance was received in the legacy (0..3) or new (10..42) wire encoding and preserves this flag in the parsed data, allowing `buildWritePayload` to encode and write back the timing advance matching the ESC firmware's expected encoding.
  - A no-op save now preserves all 256 possible byte values in timing advance: the raw byte is kept when the index has not moved, so values that are not exactly one of the four fixed points (10, 18, 26, 34) in the new encoding are no longer snapped to the nearest bucket on a save that edited nothing.
  - The AM32 page now refuses to save before the first read has landed, the same way the FlyRotor page does: a save before a read would pack every field the page does not itself carry as zero and write those to the ESC.
- **RELOAD on the Session Logs page no longer erases the log (`app/pages/tools/diagnostics/session_logs/`, `docs/pages/tools/diagnostics/session_logs.md`) (fixes #301)**:
  - The page shows the log lines the running script is holding in memory, and the header's RELOAD button emptied that list instead of re-reading it. Unless *Log Session To Card* happened to be on, the list was the only copy of the session, so the record a pilot had opened the page to read was gone -- and the confirmation in front of the button asks about discarding unsaved changes, which is not what was being discarded. RELOAD now draws the page again and leaves the log alone, which is what it does on every other page. Clearing the log is not offered: a control that destroys data needs a label and a question of its own.
  - The page now has a `?` button and a documentation page; it had neither.
- **The Tail Rotor page guards governor profile writes against unread data (`app/pages/flight_tuning/advanced/tail_rotor/page.lua`, `tasks/msp/api/governor_profile.lua`) (fixes #285)**:
  - When editing Tail Torque Assist (TTA) on `Flight Tuning > Advanced > Tail Rotor`, saving previously issued a full `MSP_SET_GOVERNOR_PROFILE` even if the governor profile read step had timed out or failed, rewriting all other governor profile settings with compiled-in library defaults.
  - `MSP_SET_GOVERNOR_PROFILE` is now queued only when the governor profile read explicitly succeeded on this visit (`ui.runtime.governorReadComplete`). When the governor read fails, governor writes are skipped while PID profile saving proceeds normally with TTA controls inactive.
  - `GovernorProfileApi.buildWritePayload` now emits the 14-byte record when the profile was read from an older board (without `fallback_drop` and `flags`), and the 17-byte record when both fields are present. Compiled-in literals are never substituted for missing keys. This also fixes the Governor page on boards before 12.0.9, which could previously no longer write after the field validation was tightened.
- **Seven ESC Configurator pages refuse a parameter block that is not their ESC's, and refuse a save without one (`tasks/msp/api/esc_parameters_{blheli_s,bluejay,hw5,omp,xdfly,yge,ztw}.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/`, `docs/reference/saving.md`) (implements #291, fixes #352)**:
  - The ESC Tools grid offers AM32, BLHeli_S and Bluejay behind one condition, which is the ESC *telemetry* protocol and says nothing about which of the three firmwares is on the ESC. A page opened on the wrong one read the reply anyway: the flight controller sizes and labels the block, the page decodes it with its own field list, and every byte lands at an offset that means something else. The page then drew that as the ESC's settings and a Save wrote it back.
  - Each of these seven parse functions now tests the ESC family the flight controller put in the first byte of the block against the one the page is for, and refuses anything else, the way the Scorpion module already did. Six of them also refuse a reply shorter than the block the flight controller sends for that family, whose missing bytes used to read as zero and be written back as zero on the next save. OpenYGE has no such test because its block is sized from the parameter count the ESC itself reports.
  - BLHeli_S and Bluejay report the same family byte and the same block length, so the family test cannot separate them. The main revision does, and both modules now test it -- 16 for BLHeli_S, 0 for Bluejay -- which is where Rotorflight's earlier Lua suite tests it too. The two field lists share five header fields and then diverge: bytes 7, 8 and 9 are a BLHeli_S ESC's governor I gain, governor mode and low voltage limit, and the Bluejay page read them as the minimum startup power, the startup beep and the dithering -- which a Save then wrote back with those meanings.
  - Because a refused read leaves the page without a block, these seven pages now refuse a Save until a read has succeeded, as the Scorpion page already did. Without it the page would pack every field it does not itself carry as zero and write that to the ESC. The refusal is a translated message rather than the reason code the Scorpion page shows today.
  - Everything an earlier reply left on the page is dropped when the page is left, and the block itself again as the next read starts: the parsed block, the ESC's model, version and firmware, the active-field list and the page's edited values, which all come from the same reply. A page module outlives its own close, so a visit whose read fails -- a different ESC, a switched ESC target, an ESC that did not answer -- used to find the previous one's block still there, drew its values and its field list, and would have saved them to the new ESC.
  - The XDFly and ZTW simulator fixtures were 28 bytes where the flight controller sends 44, which the new length test would have refused. They are the sibling OMP fixture with sixteen zero bytes missing from three interior runs -- the non-zero bytes of the three blocks are identical apart from the signature -- so both are replaced by that block with their own signature byte. The last four bytes of the reply are the ESC's active-field mask, which the pages read directly, so this restores them to the fifteen active fields the short fixture happened to produce rather than moving them.
  - The two `isCompatibleEsc` functions, which encoded the main-revision test and which nothing called, are removed: the test they describe is now made at the read.
- **The ELRS Link page identifies the transmitter module properly, and both of its writes ask first (`app/pages/tools/diagnostics/elrs_link/`, `docs/pages/tools/diagnostics/elrs_link.md`) (fixes #300)**:
  - The page pings every CRSF device on the link and then decides which of the answers is the ExpressLRS module. One of the four tests it made was the source address `0xEE`, which is the CRSF transmitter address of the protocol and therefore says nothing about who is answering: any transmitter module in the bay passed it and became the target of the parameter writes. The identity now rests on the serial number ExpressLRS reports -- which is what its own module script decides on -- or, failing that, on the module's name, and the address answers the second question instead: whether that ExpressLRS device is the transmitter module, whose fields packet rate and telemetry ratio are.
  - *RF -> ELRS* and *ELRS -> RF* were one tap each with nothing in front of them, and both write to a live device: the first changes the transmitter module's packet rate and telemetry ratio on a radio that may be bound to a model at the time, the second writes the flight controller's telemetry configuration and commits it to permanent storage. Both now ask first, and the question quotes the two sides as the page's own rows show them. A declined question writes nothing and says so, and a radio that cannot put the question up writes nothing either.
  - The page now has a `?` button and a documentation page; it had neither.
- **The post-flight page keeps its statistics when the pack is unplugged (`tasks/events/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry/flight_record.lua`, `docs/reference/flight-statistics.md`) (fixes #323)**:
  - After a disarm the dashboard shows the post-flight page from the flight record under `rfsuite.session.flight`. The event runtime dropped that record, and zeroed the flight count, as soon as the link had been down for two seconds -- which is what unplugging the battery does -- so the tiles went to `--` and `0` while the pilot was reading them at the flight line.
  - The record is now dropped when the next session begins, that is when a link comes up again, and not when a link goes down. The post-flight values stay on the radio until a pack is plugged in again or the model is armed for the next flight. The flight log's pending entry is still released at the disconnect, as before.
- **FlyRotor ESC parameters payload size and ADV/OTHER parameters preservation (`tasks/msp/api/esc_parameters_flyrotor.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/flrtr/page.lua`) (fixes #281)**:
  - Extended FlyRotor `FIELD_SPEC` to 56 bytes to cover all ADV and OTHER parameter page fields (`active_freewheel`, `drive_freq`, `max_motor_erpm`, `throttle_protocol`, `telemetry_protocol`, `led_color`, `led_rgb`, `motor_temp_sensor`, `motor_temp`, `capacity_cutoff`), matching the flight controller's expected buffer length and preventing uninitialised MSP buffer tail bytes from corrupting ADV and OTHER parameter pages upon saving.
  - Added length check (`#buf < PAYLOAD_LEN`) and signature check in `Api.parse`; a rejected read is now logged so the refused save can be understood.
  - Added read verification guard and payload length assertion in `queueFlyrotorWrite` on the FlyRotor tool page to refuse saving without a preceding read or when payload size is invalid. The guard is per page visit (`escReadComplete`): the page module outlives its close in the registry cache, so a block read on an earlier visit no longer counts as a read for the current save. The refusal is translated (both `esc_not_read` and `invalid_payload_length` are localized), and the payload length is published by the API module so the page check cannot drift from the field spec.
- **In-flight tuning: custom adjustment windows (`widgets/dashboard/inflight/drive.lua`, `prime.lua`, `functions.lua`, `screen.lua`) (preview)**: Plus, minus and trim inputs now use the selected bank and row's actual increment and decrement windows from the flight controller. Non-standard or asymmetric windows no longer drive the standard row ladder. Custom steps are refused until their windows are read, and a missing or unreachable window does not fall back to another parameter's value. The Standard layout is unchanged. Unread layouts, unusable rows and an empty slot table give distinct on-screen reasons; a trim moved to an unusable row releases its hold and returns the channel to neutral. A value the flight controller would read as a second slot of the same bank, or a window it does not read at all, is refused rather than stepped. An empty slot read is shown as unavailable, with no stale parameter names.
- **Setup > Telemetry preserves unmanaged and native sensor slots on save (`app/pages/setup/telemetry/page.lua`, `ui/controls.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/telemetry.md`) (fixes #277)**:
  - Fixed a critical data loss defect where saving the Telemetry page wiped all sensor slots not present in its 88-entry custom sensor catalog. On flight controllers configured with native CRSF telemetry (`crsf_telemetry_mode = NATIVE`), saving overwritten slots 13..52 wiped four of the seven native telemetry sensors (`TELEM_BATTERY` [2], `TELEM_GPS` [72], `TELEM_RPM` [108], and `TELEM_TEMP` [109]), causing battery voltage/current, GPS, headspeed, and temperatures to stop streaming to the radio.
  - `buildWritePayload` now preserves any unmanaged sensor slot in place and writes back only the slots managed by the page catalog.
  - In Native mode, native sensors present on the flight controller (`Altitude`, `Attitude`, and `Flight Mode`) are dynamically detected and locked active/read-only, preventing them from being unselected or cleared via defaults. Conflicting sub-axes (e.g. individual attitude axes) are disabled when the combined parent sensor is locked.
  - `Controls.appendRadioSwitch` and `Controls.appendComboSelect` normalize `active` parameters to function getters, satisfying EdgeTX LVGL widget runtime (`lua_pcallk`) requirements.
  - `loadFromSession` now recognizes any non-zero sensor slot on the flight controller, preventing default custom sensors from being erroneously forced onto a configured native telemetry setup.
  - `onSave` accounts for unmanaged slots when verifying against the 40-slot limit (`#selected + unmanagedCount <= 40`), and `canSave` verifies that a full 52-byte telemetry buffer was received before saving.
  - The page now reads and displays the active CRSF telemetry mode (`CRSF Telemetry: Native` / `CRSF Telemetry: Custom`) with an advisory notice when Native mode is active, making the distinction between native and custom sensor lists transparent to the pilot.
- **Dashboard job step errors and CPU-limit kills clear `_job` and no longer pin the widget in the JOB branch (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `widgets/rfsuite/main.lua`) (fixes #280)**:
  - When an asynchronous job step (such as theme building or splash rendering) raised an error or hit an EdgeTX instruction-limit kill, `self._job` remained set because the step execution was unguarded. Every subsequent pass re-entered the JOB branch, re-executed the failing step, and raised again, permanently starving the STATE branch where `performBackgroundWork`, `MspRuntime.tick`, and `EventsRuntime.wakeup` execute.
  - Job steps in `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua` now run guarded under `pcall`. On error, `_job` is cleared, `built` is set to `false`, errors are logged to the card log sink via `logFault` and `widgetLog`, and CPU-limit errors are re-raised so that the outer handler in `widgets/rfsuite/main.lua` can invoke `letGoWhileBackedOff` and activate the 1.2 s back-off.
  - The outer fault handler in `widgets/rfsuite/main.lua` also defensively resets `widget._job = nil` and `widget.built = false` whenever `widget.refresh` fails.
- **The flight log keeps a flight whose capacity reading carries decimals, and its card guards refuse instead of assuming (`lib/flight_log.lua`, `tasks/events/ondisarm/tasks/flight_log.lua`, `docs/pages/tools/flight_log.md`) (fixes #309)**:
  - The `mah` column is written with `%d`, which raises for a number with no integer representation, and a consumption sensor the radio publishes with decimals delivers exactly that. The raise landed between the open and the close of `flights.csv`, so the handle was never closed and the flight was lost -- the flight record has been closed by that point and nothing tries a second time. The integer columns are now rounded down where the format table declares them integral, and the whole line is built before the file is opened, so nothing between the open and the close can raise at all.
  - `fstat` answers nothing both for a file that is not there and for a file it could not measure, and the guards resting on it read that as "carry on". An append to a log of unknown size could put a second header into the middle of it, which the parser then drops, and the verification afterwards reported a line as written when it had measured nothing. The append now tells the two apart -- opening for append creates the file, so a second look at a file that certainly exists is the card's own answer -- and refuses when the size cannot be established; the verification returns false rather than true when it could not verify.
  - A rewrite of the battery registry is refused when the read filled the 64 kB cap with no size to hold it against, which is the case in which the truncated text would have been written back over the pilot's own file. A registry below the cap is still stamped and still editable on a card whose `fstat` has stopped answering, and `markUsed` now goes through the same guarded read as the editing calls rather than guarding itself.
  - Each refusal is named in the log, instead of one sentence covering a full card, a missing card and a card that will not answer.
- **Telemetry dropout reconnect while armed reopens the flight record (`tasks/events/runtime.lua`) (fixes #278)**:
  - A telemetry dropout longer than 2 seconds triggers `publishConnected(false)`, which resets the flight record state. When telemetry reconnected while still armed, `state.lastArmed` remained `true`, preventing the arm edge detection (`armed ~= state.lastArmed`) and `Record.open()` from firing. As a result, flight time and statistics were lost for the remainder of the flight, and the subsequent disarm overwrote `flight.last` with an empty record.
  - Resetting `state.lastArmed = false` on disconnect and gating arm/disarm edge transitions on `state.linkStableUp` ensures that reconnecting while armed cleanly triggers the onarm edge and reopens the flight record, while dropouts without reconnect or returning disarmed do not trigger phantom records.
- **Accelerometer calibration (`setup/accelerometer`) (fixes #302):** Ask the pilot to hold the disarmed model level and still before starting. Wait for the flight controller's calibration status to become active and then clear, show a completion notice, and leave persistence to the firmware. Missing or invalid status samples leave monitoring active until its deadline, which starts at the command acknowledgement. Preflight retains the shared reply timeout; the shorter sampling timeout applies only after the start acknowledgement. Separate checking/starting displays and notices distinguish a refused start from an unconfirmed result; Save, Reload and another calibration are blocked during the operation.
- **The Battery page sends the battery profile only when the pilot selects one (`app/pages/setup/power/battery/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/power/battery.md`) (fixes #304)**:
  - Every save on `Setup > Power > Battery` sent `MSP_SET_BATTERY_PROFILE` with an index the page had resolved from the `BatP` telemetry sensor, and committed it to the board's own storage. `BATTERY_PROFILE` is part of the custom CRSF sensor set and does not exist on a native CRSF link, so on such a setup there was nothing to resolve and the resolution fell through to 0, which the flight controller reads as profile 1. A pilot editing a cell voltage on a board running profile 3 was switched to profile 1 by the save.
  - The write now goes out only when the pilot actually selects a profile in the combo on that page, mirroring the Consumption reserve, which has worked that way since #52. Selecting a profile still sends it; the six capacities, the cell count, the four cell voltages and the reserve are written exactly as before.
  - The sensor reading no longer accepts 0. The flight controller reports this sensor as index + 1, so a live reading is 1 to 6; 0 is what a sensor the radio has stopped receiving reads through EdgeTX, and it was being mapped onto profile 1. A telemetry dropout while the page is open therefore produced the same wrong write on a setup that otherwise resolves correctly.
  - Where the page has no source for the active profile it no longer records one either: the profile and the pack capacity it read off the flight controller are left as they were, rather than being replaced by the values of the profile the combo happens to be showing. The combo itself still shows the first profile, as it always has.
  - The page now has a documentation page, which names what the Save does and does not send.
  - The page's state is module-level and outlives a visit, so leaving the page without saving no longer
    carries a pending profile selection into the next one: the flag is cleared when the page closes, as
    the reserve's already is.
- **A Bluejay ESC keeps the settings nobody edited (`tasks/msp/api/esc_parameters_bluejay.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/bluejay.md`) (fixes #307)**:
  - Saving the Bluejay page wrote the whole parameter block back through the value transforms, and three of them are not reversible: the startup power and the two PWM-frequency thresholds round 256 raw bytes into a coarser range, and a raw PWM frequency of 0 was written back as 192. A Save with nothing edited therefore changed 130 of the 256 possible startup-power bytes, 155 of 256 on each threshold, and the one PWM-frequency value -- an EEPROM write to the ESC with a value the pilot never chose.
  - The parse now keeps the byte it read beside the value it shows, and the write sends that byte back for any of the five transformed settings whose displayed value still matches it. A setting the pilot did change is encoded exactly as before, including *Dynamic*, which is still written as 192. Verified over all 256 values of each of the five bytes: nothing changes on a save with nothing edited, where the same run on the previous code changes 130, 155, 155 and 1.
  - The module's built-in sample reply was 60 bytes where its own field list declares 66, so the parse rejected it -- and nothing said so: with no ESC attached the page came up from its own defaults, showing none of the block's values and neither of the two threshold rows, with no message anywhere that the reply had been refused. It had lost the two bytes belonging to the power rating and the forced EDT arming, which also moved both threshold bytes two places forward onto them; restored, it is the declared 66 bytes, the two thresholds read 67 % and 33 %, and the sample block is no longer a fixed point of every transform it passes through -- which is why a round trip of it had never shown any of this.
  - The Bluejay page now has a documentation page.
- **Rates cells are bounded by their rate type's own limits (`app/pages/flight_tuning/rates/page.lua`, `tasks/msp/api/rc_tuning.lua`, `docs/pages/flight_tuning/rates.md`) (fixes #283)**:
  - Every rate, shape and expo cell let the pilot wheel up to 1000, while the field behind each of them is a single byte on the wire. Above 255 the value was masked into an unrelated one, with nothing on screen marking the boundary: the page read 1500 deg/s and the flight controller was set to 220 deg/s. Each cell now carries the limits of its own rate type: the flight controller's own per-type limits for Roll, Pitch and Yaw, which it applies when it stores the settings, and the Configurator's where the board has none, which is the Collective row. Both are converted into the raw units the payload carries.
  - Roll, Pitch and Yaw were also allowed above what the flight controller keeps. A Rotorflight shape of 127 or a Betaflight super rate of 0.99 was accepted by the page and trimmed to 100 and 0.90 by the board when the settings were stored, so the page and the aircraft disagreed after a save that reported success. The cells now stop where the board does.
  - `MSP_SET_RC_TUNING` caps a value that does not fit its field instead of masking it, so a value arriving from anywhere out of range is held at the field's ceiling rather than turned into a different setting.
  - A value the flight controller is already running that lies outside the new limits is shown at the limit and still sent unchanged, so opening the page and saving it does not re-tune a board configured over the CLI.
- **The tool decodes the flight controller's telemetry while it is connecting, so it notices an arming (`tasks/events/runtime.lua`, `docs/reference/background-decoder.md`)**:
  - The custom telemetry frame is decoded by Lua, and while a tool is open the radio pauses the special-function script that would do it, so inside the tool the suite's own drain is the only decoder there is. That drain was deferred for the whole connect sequence, which is also the only source the `ARM` sensor has -- so a model armed during those seconds was invisible to the tool and the MSP queue kept sending, including the clock write, to a flight controller in the air. The tool now runs the drain during its connect sequence as well.
  - The deferral is unchanged for the dashboard and the service widget, where it is what keeps a connect pass inside the radio's per-cycle instruction budget. A tool call is not billed against that budget.
- **Arming during the connect sequence no longer leaves the link reported dead (`tasks/msp/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/common/runner.lua`, the twelve connect tasks under `tasks/events/`, `docs/troubleshooting/no-connection.md`) (fixes #297)**:
  - Configuration traffic is stopped while the model is armed by clearing the MSP queue, which is right; what was wrong was that everything the clear reported to was reading it as an answer. Arming within the first few seconds of a power-up therefore left the suite reporting no flight controller for the rest of the session, with `session.fblConnected` false, the start progress never complete and the model preferences never loaded, and it did not recover on landing.
  - The API version and unique-id reads now count a cleared request as still outstanding rather than as failed, so the handshake is retried on the first disarmed pass. This is done in the two reads themselves and not beside the armed gate, because every caller of `Queue:clear` has the same effect on them.
  - The twelve connect tasks -- status, telemetry configuration, battery and governor configuration, SmartFuel, ESC sensor configuration, model name, pilot configuration, clock, unique id, dataflash summary and flight statistics -- no longer mark themselves finished when their request was taken off the queue before it was sent. The task stays incomplete and asks again once the model is disarmed, instead of the session being left without the data while the log reported the task complete.
  - A task the runner re-queues after its own timeout now releases the task module with it, so the retry actually issues the request again rather than re-running a wakeup that returns on its first line while the log says *Re-queueing*.
  - Five of the tasks latched their request before a guard that can return without sending one, which produced a task that could neither complete nor retry if its MSP API module failed to load. They now latch after the guards and give up the way the tasks next to them already do.
- **Configuration stores survive a full card (`lib/config_store.lua`, `lib/log_sink.lua`, `docs/reference/configuration-files.md`) (fixes #276)**:
  - `Store:save` checks the return value of `io.write`. On a short write or full card, the incomplete temporary file is removed and the original store is left untouched, preventing settings from reverting to defaults on next load.
  - `LogSink.writeFile` checks the return value of `io.write` instead of assuming success once `io.open` succeeds, so a full card does not silently discard unwritten log lines from memory or reset the lost line counter.
- **Controls: `Controls.appendNumberField` anchors stepped quantization on `min` rather than zero (`ui/controls.lua`) (fixes #271)**:
  - Fixed an offset defect where number fields with a step size whose minimum is not a multiple of `step` (such as AM32 Motor KV with `min = 20, step = 40` and YGE Current Limit with `min = 1, step = 100`) mapped values onto `{0, step, 2*step, ...}` instead of `{min, min + step, min + 2*step, ...}`.
  - An ESC holding 1380 KV previously rendered as 1360 KV and stepping by one click wrote 1400 KV to the page and 1420 KV to the ESC.
  - The native `numberEdit` widget index range is now anchored at `0 .. ceil((max - min) / step)` with `min + index * step`. Both `display` and `set` apply identical clamping to `[min, max]`, and `stepVal` is guarded against non-positive values.
  - Separately from the minimum-anchoring, `get` now rounds to the nearest step grid point (with ties rounding upward via `floor(x + 0.5)`) rather than flooring unaligned values, affecting stepped fields (for example, an unaligned YGE stick position of 1785 us on a 10 us step now renders as 1790 rather than 1780).
- **Configuration saves require a completed read (ui/home.lua, app/pages/flight_tuning/, app/pages/setup/, docs/reference/saving.md) (fixes #273)**:
  - Thirty configuration pages refuse Save until their complete read sequence has succeeded. A pending read, a failed step or a rejected parser response cannot turn initial defaults into FC writes.
  - Reload invalidates the previous completion. The shared Save handler checks again after confirmation and before dispatch, reports why the save was refused, and queues no EEPROM commit for that refusal. Local radio settings keep their existing save behaviour.
  - A page change during confirmation reports that nothing was saved. Arming before dispatch uses the same configured notice or banner as the initial Save check.
- **Dashboard: bar gauge fill length and fill colour update reactively during flight (`widgets/dashboard/objects/gauge.lua`) (fixes #274)**:
  - Fixed an issue where horizontal and vertical bar gauges' filled rectangles and threshold colors were frozen at scene build time because their dimensions and colors were computed as static numbers instead of reactive getters.
  - Implemented reactive `size` and `color` getters for horizontal bar gauges, and `pos`, `size`, and `color` getters for vertical bar gauges (which grow upward from the bottom).
  - Both orientation paths share cached evaluation of derived telemetry readings, bound scaling, and threshold lookups between getters on each frame.
  - Removed the `if ratio > 0` condition guarding node insertion so bar gauge nodes are always emitted into the LVGL scene tree from the start, allowing bars starting at nil or 0 to render and update as soon as live telemetry arrives.
  - Updated offline instruction accounting budgets (`bin/accounting/budgets.lua`), passing all 51 rows and maintaining safety margins under EdgeTX widget instruction ceilings.
- **In-Flight Tuning (preview): the enable channel follows the selected bank at all times (`widgets/dashboard/inflight/drive.lua`, `docs/dashboard/inflight-tuning.md`)**:
  - The bank global variable was written only when the bank CHANGED -- by a chip, by the bank trim, or by a walk step that crossed a bank boundary. Nothing wrote it on the way into the air, and the interlock's own defensive clear takes it to zero while the selection stands, so between switching the interlock on and the first bank gesture the enable channel sat at its centre, which is inside none of the six windows the flight controller decodes. Every step in that window moved the value channel against no adjustment function and the board stepped nothing: a pilot who walks rows with a bank trim, and so never makes a bank change at all, got that for the whole flight.
  - The selected bank is now asserted once per pass for as long as the interlock is on, in all three phases the switch shows, so switching the interlock on, an arming, a row walk, a profile change, a landing and a recovery from an instruction-limit back-off all leave the enable channel where the screen says it is. It costs one comparison per pass once the variable holds the right code, and it is refused while a step is still on the wire -- moving the enable channel under a magnitude that sits in a step window is how one press ends up counted against another parameter -- with the write landing on the first pass after the pulse. The bank variable still goes to zero when the interlock is released, the fullscreen is closed, the widget is backgrounded or the link drops; what no longer clears it is the landing, since the surface is still up and still naming that bank. The value variable is unchanged in every respect: it is zero whenever no pulse is running and nothing is held, on every path out of the overlay.
  - The channel reading the surface shows is still the channel's own, so a missing mixer line stays visible as a bank that does not move; it is no longer adopted as the selection once the overlay is driving the variable itself, where a frame arriving one pass late could otherwise pull the selection back into the band it had just left.
- **In-Flight Tuning, the flight controller page: the read-back, its German text and its save (`widgets/dashboard/inflight/fcsetup.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/inflight/`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/controls/inflight.md`)**:
  - **A board set up with any step but the default read back as thirty-five differences.** The read-back held each slot against the standard set without naming the two step sizes the write had been built from, so it compared them against the module's own defaults of 5 and 50 instead. A model configured with a step of 1 was told *"Written, but the read-back does not match (35)"* about a board the same run had just written byte for byte, the head speed slot agreeing only because its own default happened to be what was written. The read-back now names both steps, as the write plan and the overlay's own comparison already did.
  - The read-back report also carries how many slots differ in the **step alone**, and the page says so where that is the whole of it, instead of reporting a count of differing slots. The step is the one field of a slot this page's own settings decide, so naming it names the remedy.
  - **Every string of the flight controller action was English on every radio.** The action is a module of its own so that the page does not pay for the writer on entry, and it took its text helper from the page -- which left it without a key prefix of its own, so the locale precompiler could not resolve any of its twenty-nine lookups and a packaged install, which ships no locale bundle, had nothing to resolve them against at runtime either. The confirmation dialog, the eight refusals, the three progress lines and every verdict the read-back reports were affected. The translations had been in the locale files all along.
  - **A failed save looked like a save that had worked.** The page reported its outcome through a callback the suite's save flow does not pass, so both branches were dead: a store write with no MCU id to key the model's settings by, or one the card refused, was swallowed. It now reports through the same callback every other page uses, and the save overlay says which it was.
- **In-flight tuning: the profile, the read and the undo (`widgets/dashboard/inflight/prime.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/inflight/screen.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/inflight/drive.lua`, `docs/dashboard/inflight-tuning.md`)**:
  - **Arming during a value read no longer leaves the overlay idle for the rest of the link.** The MSP runtime clears its whole queue on every armed tick, so a read the pilot arms into is given up -- which is right -- but everything after it asked that run's phase rather than asking whether the board had been read: no further read was started, the automatic undo refused as if nothing had ever been read, and the ground line said the values had never been read above an intact backup line. The read is taken again by itself on the ground, and the ground line reports the values it has with the time they were read at and a note that the attempt to refresh them was interrupted. A failed read is retried twice and then left with its error on the screen, because a board that answers an error answers it again.
  - **The automatic undo follows the PID profile.** It is one slot scoped to the profile it was copied from, and the request for it used to be raised only when the interlock went on -- so a pilot who changed profile on the ground kept the previous profile's copy as an undo for a profile he was no longer flying. A settled profile change on the ground now asks for a fresh copy, which replaces the old one, and the line under the buttons names the profile it came from.
  - **The undo names the profile that is being flown, even while the telemetry sensor is not answering.** A sensor the radio is not delivering reads 0 rather than nothing, so the reading fell past the last profile the widget had seen and onto the status reply of an earlier read -- which is the one thing that can be older than the profile change itself. Measured against a flight controller, that made one profile change on the ground produce two copies, the second of them naming the profile the pilot had just left. The value refresh no longer carries the previous read's status reply either, so the answer is the one that came back with the change or none at all, and none is refused by name.
  - **Releasing the interlock on the ground ends the tuning session.** The cached values, the backup line and the comparison against it belong to the session that has just been closed, so they go with it, and the next closing of the interlock reads the board again and takes a fresh backup of whichever profile is active then -- the nine value reads only, since a layout does not change because a switch moved. Releasing it in the air ends nothing: nothing can be sent to a flying machine, and the comparison the landing is about to show is measured against a snapshot that has to survive the switch.
  - **The comparison after the flight refuses to cross profiles, as the restore already did.** A backup of profile 1 held against the values of profile 2 listed everything the two profiles disagree about as something the flight had changed. The after surface now names the two profiles and makes no comparison, and the ground line says when the backup is not for the profile being flown.
  - **A restore with no record of which profile the backup holds is refused.** That record is kept in memory, so after a restart the spare profile holds a copy of something and nothing on the radio knows of what -- which was the one state in which a restore across profiles was allowed.
  - **The rate and PID profile rows are counted from 1**, like the header above them and the backup line under them, instead of showing the wire's own 0-based index.
  - **Backgrounding the widget ends whichever surface was up.** The postflight read-out used to survive it, so the next closing of the interlock was not an opening and asked for no undo; the pilot's log has two such cycles in a row. The overlay also writes one line when it is taken down, so a backgrounded widget is visible in the card log.
  - A value refresh now gets its own run rather than reusing the previous one, so a late reply of a read the queue had given up on cannot be counted into it and written to the cache as a value from before the profile changed. A backup asked for while a read is on the wire is refused by name instead of snapshotting half of one profile and half of another.
  - The gate that keeps a read off an expensive widget pass counts the whole wait rather than an unbroken streak: measured on the desktop probe, a widget alternating between a cheap pass and an expensive one used to count one skip in 400 passes and never reach the escape that takes a slice anyway.
- **In-Flight Tuning (preview): the board's answer to a step no longer rebuilds the surface (`widgets/dashboard/inflight/drive.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/inflight/screen.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/inflight/prime.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/dashboard/inflight-tuning.md`)**:
  - A step is a magnitude written to the value global variable and cleared a set number of milliseconds later, from a widget pass. The flight controller answers that step over its adjustment telemetry a few hundred milliseconds afterwards -- while the magnitude is still standing -- and that answer moved a counter the widget's render key was built from, so the live surface was torn down and built again inside the pulse window. EdgeTX calls a widget from its menus task in fixed slots and never catches up, so the slots a rebuild costs are slots the pass that clears the pulse does not get: on a radio where those slots are already tight the magnitude stayed on the wire past the flight controller's repeat window and the board counted a second step.
  - The values the board reports are now read from the surface's own snapshot by the same kind of per-frame closure the large value already used -- the six row values among them -- and the report moves a counter of its own that no render key follows. The rebuilds that change the LAYOUT are unchanged: a bank chip, a row selection, the trim walk finishing, a profile change and a phase change all still rebuild.
  - The widget also reports a hole in its own refresh cadence: at *DEBUG* it writes `pass gap 300 ms, 40 ms of it inside refresh, previous pass tuning_fs` whenever more than 150 ms has passed since the previous pass, which is the one thing no figure inside a pass can show. A pass that never reached the end of its refresh -- killed by the instruction limit -- reports -1 for the second figure rather than a guess. It costs 26 instructions on every pass against a per-pass budget of 12200, 9 of them the detection and 17 the second clock read that separates the two figures.
- **Flight statistics declared once (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/objects/common.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/objects/text/stats.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/objects/gauge.lua`) (fixes #248)**:
  - The per-flight extremes are one table with a row per statistic -- the state field it samples, which extremes it records, and the condition under which it records them -- and one loop over that table at each of the five places that used to spell the whole set out by hand. Adding a statistic is a row, not five edits.
  - Both objects that show the record resolve a box `source` through one shared mapping in `objects/common.lua`, so a source means the same statistic in a stats text box as in a gauge's maximum label. The `temp_esc` and `temp_mcu` spellings the gauge has always accepted are part of that mapping and now work on a stats box too.
  - Every recorded field keeps the name it had, including the older `lastMinVoltage`, `lastMinBecVoltage` and `lastMinLq` spellings, so a user theme reading the widget state directly is unaffected.
  - A stats text box with a threshold color no longer calls a resolver that does not exist.
- **Dashboard text box colors & threshold evaluation (`widgets/dashboard/objects/`, `docs/dashboard/user-themes.md`) (fixes #213)**:
  - Enabled dynamic threshold color evaluation (`thresholds`) on dashboard text boxes (`telemetry`, `stats`, `blackbox`, `governor`, `time`, and generic text), matching the reactive threshold behavior of gauge objects.
  - Added named color string resolution (`"orange"`, `"red"`, `"blue"`, etc.) for text box `textcolor` properties via `normalizeColor`.
  - Refactored `resolveThresholdColor` and temperature threshold conversions into `objects/common.lua` shared across gauge and text objects, with threshold compilation cached in a weak-keyed lookup.
  - Added support for governor state threshold matching against both translated and untranslated state names.
  - Documented dashboard box styling, named colors, and threshold formats in `docs/dashboard/user-themes.md`.
- **Initial fuel announcement: gated until valid telemetry arrives and sensor promotion added (`lib/audio.lua`, `lib/sensors.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `docs/audio/events.md`) (fixes #187)**:
  - Gated `initial_fuel` in `lib/audio.lua` with `self.state.fuelTelemetrySeen == true`. Previously, this check was missing while continuous `fuel_alerts` had it, causing startup audio to announce the pre-initialized seed value of `0` ("Battery 0%") before telemetry packets arrived.
  - Implemented dynamic deferral window based on `stabilize_delay` and detected carried-over readings from previous sessions (`previousSessionFuel`). If fresh, positive telemetry arrives within the window, the percentage is announced immediately; if the pack is genuinely empty (0%), the announcement proceeds once the ceiling expires.
  - Added inactive-sensor shielding (`telemetryValueIsLive()`) to `lib/sensors.lua` for both search path adoption and primary sensor promotion: an existing sensor on the model that reports `0` through `getValue()` before sending live telemetry is treated as a miss, preventing dead sensors like `SmFt` from locking out valid fallbacks.
  - Added periodic (3.0 s) promotion of the primary telemetry sensor (`SmFt`) when a fallback sensor (`Bat%`) is initially cached, and added `Sensors.reset()` to flush caches and field info on disconnect/reconnect edges.
- **The tool reports a pack that is gone as gone (`ui/home.lua`)**:
  - The tool's telemetry state kept the last positive pack voltage instead of storing the reading, so a pack disconnected while the flight controller stayed alive on its BEC still read as present. The widget stores the reading; the two now describe the same machine, which is what that shared state exists for. Every alert reading the field already requires a voltage above zero, so nothing else changes.
- **Preference watcher: a file that cannot be read is no longer reported as a file that changed (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`)**:
  - `preferencesStamp` dropped the per-model half of its stamp, or returned an empty string for the global half, whenever `fstat` raised or answered something unreadable. The shorter string then differed from the kept one, so a failed read arrived at the watcher as a change and cost a full reload -- preferences re-read, theme dropped, scene rebuilt -- and a second one when the file read again.
  - A failure to measure is now `nil`, which both callers already treat as "no comparison this pass". A session with no per-model file is unchanged and still stamps the global half alone.
- **SmartFuel Consumption Export (`tasks/events/telemetry_bg/smart.lua`)**:
  - Removed the Ethos-only `system.getSource` mirror fallback and its two query tables; the EdgeTX script environment registers no global `system`, so that step could never run.
  - `SmCp` is now published only in voltage mode, where the suite computes the virtual consumption itself. Where the flight controller reports consumption, the sensor was a duplicate of it and cost a telemetry slot plus a model write per push.
- **Stale Sensors No Longer Shadow Live Ones (`lib/sensors.lua`)**:
  - A sensor a model still carries but the radio no longer receives reads as `0` through `getValue()` and used to win its search path ahead of the live sensor behind it; that reading is now treated as a miss.
- **The Volume tile draws an icon (`app/pages/settings/audio/volume/icon.png`) (fixes #324)**:
  - Every page registered in `app/pages/init.lua` is given `<page folder>/icon.png` as its tile icon, and nothing checks that the file is there. `Settings > Audio > Volume` was the one tile in the tree that carried neither that file nor an icon of its own in `app/manifest.lua`, so it rendered as a label with an empty icon slot beside `Events`.
  - The icon is a 40x40 8-bit grayscale PNG, which is the size and the encoding `.vscode/scripts/optimize_icons.py` keeps for a monochrome source, and it is drawn to sit with the existing tile set rather than taken from one.

- **The arming state is searched on the first read after a sensor reset again (`lib/sensors.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/reference/sensor-selection.md`) (follows #424)**:
  - #424 limits the first searches after `Sensors.reset()` to four per pass. The dashboard widget asks for `armflags` after five other sources, so after every reset it was held back for one read and the widget knew the arming state about half a second later than before. `armflags` is now exempt from that limit: it is the one source whose absence reads as "not armed", and its list is five names long.
  - A long press on RTN that leaves fullscreen on a pass that waits for a theme reload now drops the tuning surface and the battery picker on that pass rather than the next one.
  - `docs/reference/sensor-selection.md` described the widget and the configuration tool together; the tool forgets its sensor choice on every audio tick while its connection is not ready, and now has a bullet of its own.
- **Setup > Telemetry opens again on a second visit (`app/pages/setup/telemetry/page.lua`) (fixes #405)**:
  - Leaving the page clears its runtime, while the page module stays in the page cache. On the next visit `ensureDeps` recreated the runtime as an empty table rather than as the page's own, so `activeGetters` was missing and the build raised at `getActiveGetter`. The page showed the page-build error screen instead of the sensor list, with Save, Reload and the star disabled. That happened on every visit for as long as the module stayed cached.
  - `ensureDeps` now creates the runtime through `ensureRuntime()`, the page's own constructor, so a second visit builds the same page as the first.
- **The four mixer pages save only what this visit read (`app/pages/setup/mixer/swash/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/mixer/swashgeometry/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/mixer/tail/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/mixer/trims/page.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`) (fixes #401)**:
  - Swash, Swash Geometry, Tail and Trims each write the whole `MSP_SET_MIXER_CONFIG` record -- and Swash, Swash Geometry and Tail the mixer-input records -- from what the page holds, with its own fields laid over it. Their guard against saving without a read asked whether such a record was there, and it always was after a first visit: the page is kept between visits and fills its records from its own session copy before the visit's read is queued. A visit whose read failed, was rejected by its parser, or had not returned yet could therefore save the record of an earlier visit -- including another page's older tail mode or swash type -- and Trims committed it to EEPROM.
  - Each page now has the per-visit read flag and `canSave` the other read-backed pages use since #273: set only when every read of the visit's chain parsed, cleared when a read starts and when the page closes. Save on such a visit reports *Not saved* through the host's existing check, and the page's own write refuses as well.
  - A reply the parser rejects ends the read chain instead of reading on, so the rest of a chain is no longer read after its first record failed.
  - The live write that Trims (with the swash override on) and Swash Geometry (in setup mode) send while a value is changed sends the same whole records, and waits for the same read.
  - The values from the earlier visit are still shown while the page reads again; they no longer count as a read.
- **Three help texts no longer repeat their last sentence on an installed package (`app/pages/settings/general/help.lua`, `app/pages/tools/copy_profiles/help.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/preferences/help.lua`, `docs/developer/i18n.md`)**:
  - Each wrote its English fallback as `i18n.t("key") or "first part" .. "rest"`. The precompiler replaces the call together with the first literal, so the package carried the translated text followed by the rest of the English fallback: `Settings > General` showed half a sentence twice in English and, in German, an English tail after the German text.
  - The fallback is now joined in a local of its own and the call reads `i18n.t("key") or fallback`. The precompiler rewrites that call to a marker without a fallback, so the three keys now have to exist in `en.lua` for the build to finish; they do.
  - `docs/developer/i18n.md` now describes what the precompiler does with a `..` chain or a variable after `or`: a variable works in the `i18n.t` forms only and makes the key required there, while the `tr` and widget `t` forms need a single flat literal.
- **Three announcement defects: a lost arm or governor call, an early flight-time call, and temperatures in the wrong unit (`lib/audio.lua`, `app/pages/settings/audio/events/category_page.lua`, `docs/audio/events.md`, `docs/pages/settings/localization.md`) (fixes #308)**:
  - **An arm or governor announcement is no longer lost to the cooldown.** Both recorded the new value as spoken before trying to play it, and a play refused because the previous file's 0.25 s cooldown was still running was never tried again. The ordinary case was a spool-up on arm: *Armed* played and the governor state due in the same pass was dropped for the rest of the connection. Both now wait for the cooldown the way the profile and battery announcements already do, so the governor state follows a moment later. A file the sound pack does not carry is still given up after one try.
  - **The flight-time callout no longer speaks the previous flight's time at take-off.** At an arm edge the dashboard's armed state can come from a telemetry read one pass ahead of the flight record's own arm edge, and the flight time it read was the record's copy, refreshed on telemetry reads only. Until the next read that copy still held the last flight's duration, so a last flight at or past the configured time made the callout speak on arming and then again at the real time. On the dashboard the callout now reads the flight record itself, only while it is open. The tool keeps its own clock, unchanged.
  - **Temperatures follow Settings > Localization > Temperature Unit.** The ESC and MCU alert thresholds are shown in °F on a Fahrenheit radio and in °C otherwise, where the page used to show a bare `°` beside the value and in the row's label; they are still stored and compared in Celsius, the unit the flight controller reports, so no stored threshold changes meaning and the defaults stay what they were. The spoken MCU temperature is in the chosen unit as well. A threshold typed as a Fahrenheit number before this change is now shown converted (200 reads 392 °F) and should be entered again.
  - **The ESC alert's help text names its settings one line each**, in the order the page shows them, instead of one long paragraph, and says that the thresholds follow the Temperature Unit. What it no longer carries -- why the MCU threshold is radio-wide, and how *Until cleared* and a count repeat -- is on `docs/audio/events.md`.
- **Five help texts are short again, one line per setting (`i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/theme/help.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/overrides/help.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/settings/help.lua`, `docs/audio/events.md`)**:
  - The `?` sheets of *Audio > Events > Voltage* and *Link*, and of *Dashboard > Design*, *Per-Model Settings* and a theme's page under *Dashboard > Settings*, were single paragraphs of up to 1332 characters. At 480x320 the longest ran behind the Close button.
  - Each now names the settings in the order the page shows them, one line each, starting with the label as it reads on screen. The longer explanations were already on the documentation pages (`docs/audio/events.md`, `docs/pages/settings/dashboard/`). `docs/audio/events.md` gains the one fact only the old help text had: a receiver that reports no link quality leaves the link alert silent.
  - In German, the *Link* page's section header and its first row now spell *link quality* with an umlaut, as the help text and the rest of the German translation do.
- **The post-flight voltage tiles keep the landing value after the pack is unplugged (`widgets/dashboard/objects/text/stats.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/themes/@aerc/postflight.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/themes/@rt-rc/postflight.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/themes/@srb-rc/postflight.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`) (fixes #489)**:
  - The *Voltage* tiles on the @AERC and @RT-RC post-flight pages read the live pack voltage, so they showed `--.-V` once the pack was unplugged from a flight controller that stays powered, and the next pack's voltage once one was plugged in. They now show the landing voltage (`stattype = "last"`), with the two decimals the tiles declare. While the pack stays plugged in they no longer follow its live voltage; they keep the value read at the disarm.
  - The per-cell tile on the @SRB-RC post-flight page divided the live voltage by the live cell count. A new `stattype = "lastcell"` shows the landing voltage per cell, divided by the cell count taken with it at the disarm, so a pack with a different cell count plugged in afterwards does not change it.
  - `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md` says which voltage values a `stats` box keeps after the pack comes off.
- **The Urban theme's tile draws an icon of its own (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/icon.png`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`)**:
  - Urban shipped with a byte-for-byte copy of the *Default* theme's icon, so on *Dashboard* → *Settings* and *Per-Model Settings* its tile could not be told from *Default*'s or *RF Status*'s by its picture. The new icon is a small picture of Urban's flight screen: the top and bottom bars, the left panel, the vertical battery gauge in the middle and the value rows on the right.
  - It is a 40x40 8-bit grayscale PNG with no alpha, the size and encoding `.vscode/scripts/optimize_icons.py` keeps for a monochrome source, drawn in the stroke width and greys of Urban's own page icons under `themes/urban/icons/` so the theme tile and its page tiles read as one set.
- **The help sheet behind `?` keeps its title and no longer lets a long text run behind a button (`ui/help_view.lua`, `docs/pages/README.md`) (fixes #490)**:
  - The sheet put its text into a `page` node with a height and drew its own header and a Close button around it. EdgeTX builds every `page` full screen with a header of its own, so the hand-drawn header was painted over (the sheet showed an empty title) and the Close button sat on top of the text; at 480x320 a long help text ran behind it.
  - The sheet is now a page like the menu pages: the header carries the title of the page the help belongs to, and the text runs down the page with nothing over it. EXIT closes the sheet, and so does the X in the header on a touch screen. A long text is read on with the rotary encoder, one line of the text per step in either direction, a thin marker above each line showing the place in the focus colour, or by dragging on a touch screen. The height estimate the old layout needed is gone with it.
- **The Logs summary no longer reports a column it cannot name, and the graph says why it cannot plot a file (`app/pages/logs/`, `docs/pages/logs.md`) (fixes #305)**:
  - The summary read each of its figures from the column whose header names it, and took any figure it could not name from columns 1 to 5 by position. On a log from a model without Rotorflight telemetry those columns are link statistics, so the link quality was shown as the maximum throttle, and a raw sensor column as the headspeed and the ESC temperature. A figure whose column the log does not have is now shown as `--`; a named column is read exactly as before, and on a log that names all of them the summary is unchanged.
  - A log without an ARM column has nothing for the headspeed rows to tell armed from disarmed by; the code now says so where it gates them on throttle and current alone, which is what it already did.
  - A `.csv` file whose name does not carry the date and time EdgeTX gives its logs is already opened for its date; it is now listed only if its first line is EdgeTX's `Date,Time` header, so a spreadsheet in `/LOGS/` is no longer offered as a flight. A file named the way EdgeTX names its logs is still listed without being opened.
  - The graph of a file with no `Date` and `Time` columns, or with no row whose time it can read, opened on the column chooser, and every *Show* was refused without a word and drew the chooser again. It now shows *Not an EdgeTX telemetry log* or, where the log has rows but none carries a time in EdgeTX's `HH:MM:SS.mmm` form, the new *No row carries a time the graph can read* -- such a log still has a summary, which reads its times more leniently. A refused choice says so as well.
- **A theme's settings page reports a failed save of a model's own settings (`widgets/dashboard/themes/*/configure.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/lib.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/theme/page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/settings/page.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/pages/settings/dashboard/settings.md`, `docs/pages/settings/dashboard/theme.md`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`) (fixes #287)**:
  - A theme opened from *Per-Model Settings* stores its values in the model's own file, and the seven shipped themes other than Urban threw away what that write answered: the page reported *Theme settings saved* on the strength of the radio's file, which holds none of those values. A card that refused the model's file therefore kept none of the new values while the page said they were saved.
  - In the model scope the save now reports the model's file: a refused write shows *Save failed* with its reason, and so does a `lib/model_preferences.lua` that will not load. In the standard scope the values are in the radio's file and its write is what the save reports, as before; the model's file, rewritten beside it while a flight controller is connected, carries none of them and does not turn a saved standard value into a failure. The shape is the one Urban's settings page already uses.
  - The reason the pilot reads after *Save failed*, on the eight theme settings pages and on *Dashboard* → *Theme*, was what the store answered: the module's name, `model_preferences`, where `lib/model_preferences.lua` will not load, and on a card that refused the write a token of `lib/config_store.lua` (`io`, `write`, `delete`, `rename`) or the error text `io.open` gave, the file's path followed by `file error` -- for the model's file and for the radio's alike. Every refused write of either file now reads *the settings file could not be written to the SD card* (`store_write_failed`), a model store that will not load *model settings store not available* (`model_store_unavailable`), and a model store with no board id *Connect the flight controller to save this model's settings* (`model_store_missing`), all under `settings_dashboard_settings`, English and German. The mapping is one function, `saveFailureReason` in `app/pages/settings/dashboard/lib.lua`, which every theme's settings module and the *Theme* page already load.
- **In-Flight Tuning (preview): a trim press no longer rebuilds the surface, and a press that comes too early says so (`widgets/dashboard/inflight/drive.lua`, `docs/dashboard/inflight-tuning.md`)**:
  - The adjust trim moved the layout counter the tuning render key is built from on every press and on every release, so each trim step tore the live surface down and built it again -- at most once per half second -- although nothing on it had changed: the snapshot carries nothing derived from the trim's state and the screen reads none of it. A trim press and release now move no counter. A trim that changes what the surface shows still rebuilds it: in the *rows* layout the trim of another row selects that row, and the row is in the render key on its own; the bank and walk trims of the *navigate* layout are unchanged.
  - A trim press during the previous press's pulse or the cool-down after it started nothing and showed nothing, so it looked like a trim that is not wired up. It now shows *too fast - one step at a time*, the sentence a step control already shows in the same case, set once at the press rather than on every pass the trim is held, so it moves no render key and does not republish the snapshot per pass. A trim held past the cool-down still steps then, and that step clears the sentence.
- **In-Flight Tuning (preview): one trim press is one step, however long the trim is held (`widgets/dashboard/inflight/drive.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/inflight/setup.lua`, `docs/dashboard/inflight-tuning.md`, `docs/pages/settings/dashboard/inflight.md`, `docs/pages/setup/controls/inflight.md`)**:
  - A held trim kept its row's value on the value channel for as long as it read as held, and the flight controller repeats a value that stands still every 200 ms. The trim is read once per widget pass, and a pass sees only whether it is down at that instant, so a release and the next press that both fall between two passes read as one continuous hold: on a radio that runs the widget at a few passes a second, a quick series of taps could stand on the channel for seconds and step the parameter many more times than the trim was pressed. A trim press now puts exactly one pulse on the channel -- the configured length, to the next widget pass -- and nothing more, held or not; the next step needs the trim to come up and go down again. The step controls on the screen still repeat while held, because their release is reported rather than sampled.
  - A trim press during the previous press's pulse or the cool-down after it is refused, as before, and now steps nothing later either: a trim still held when the cool-down ends no longer takes its step then. In the *rows* layout a press on another row's trim while a pulse runs is refused the same way, and the running pulse finishes for the row it was started for; a press for a row that cannot be stepped puts nothing on the channel.
  - The comment on the largest pulse length now says what that cap bounds: the length the setting adds, at EdgeTX's 50 ms widget pass. On a radio that runs the widget at about 300 ms per pass the channel stands for a whole pass whatever is set, and the settings page's documentation says so.
- **The dashboard no longer ends a flight on one disarmed arm-flags reading the MSP runtime contradicts (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`)**:
  - The widget reads the `armflags` sensor every 0.5 s, and the MSP runtime reads the same ARM sensor on every logic tick, at the top of the same pass and ahead of the custom-telemetry drain. A reading with bit 0 clear that appeared between the two -- a frame the drain published in between, including frames queued before the drain took over from the background decoder -- was taken as a disarm while the runtime still held the model armed: the phase went to post-flight and back to armed, *Disarmed* and *Armed* were announced in flight, the landing voltage and the disarm time were stamped mid-flight, and the in-flight tuning overlay left its live phase.
  - Such a reading is now left untaken, for `armed` and for `armFlags` alike, and one info line per run of them names the value, the RSSI and that the runtime still reads armed. A real disarm is taken by the first read after the runtime's next tick: on the same read as before where the background decoder publishes the sensor, and one read (0.5 s) later where the widget's own drain publishes the disarm frame on a pass that reads. A reading of armed is always taken -- the runtime's "not armed" is also what it holds when no arm source answers, so it cannot overrule one.

- **A dashboard build that raises every time is given up after three tries instead of being retried for as long as the widget runs (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`, `docs/dashboard/user-themes.md`) (fixes #340)**:
  - The job dispatcher catches a raising step and clears the slot and `built`, and the next state pass armed the same job again. For a cause that does not go away -- a theme module that throws in `build`, a `nil` in a scene definition -- that was a loop with no end: the failing step ran every other pass, and every run wrote a `LogSink.fault` line to the card and an error line to the log. With a theme that raises on every build, measured on the simulator against a flight controller: 187 raises and 187 fault lines on the card after 20 s, 562 and 562 after 60 s. The screen meanwhile kept the connection splash from before the loop, so the pilot was told the widget was still connecting.
  - Raises are now counted per job kind, consecutively: a step of that kind that draws its surface clears the count. After three the kind is not armed again, and the widget draws *Dashboard error -- This screen could not be drawn* in its place, once. Only the first raise of a run goes to the card as a fault; each raise still writes its error line, and the third says that the job is not retried.
  - The theme drawn at full screen (`fullscreen = "theme"`), the quick menu and the in-flight tuning surface are counted the same way, each as its own kind, and at full screen *Dashboard error* carries the tool control, as the connect splash does there. The views a theme registers are not counted: the widget already gives one up on its first raise.
  - If *Dashboard error* itself raises three times in a row -- or the connect splash does, since both are drawn by the same builder -- the widget draws *Dashboard error* as a single label on a plain background, without that builder and without the tool control, so the screen does not keep a surface that is no longer true. The label is counted as well; only if it raises three times too does the widget stop drawing, after at most nine raises in all.
  - A theme reload clears every count, since it replaces the code that raised: choosing a theme, any change to the preferences, the flight controller reconnecting, and a flight phase that brings up another of the theme's modules all start over.
  - What it costs: once a kind is given up, the error screen stays until the next theme reload, where the widget used to retry and recover by itself as soon as the cause went away. A flight phase reloads the theme only when it brings up another of the theme's modules, so a fault in flight can keep the error screen up for the rest of the flight. The cause has to last only three build attempts in a row to get there -- about a third of a second at the rate measured above. A theme that always raises writes one fault line per theme reload rather than one per pass.
