  - The battery prompt's two jobs are not counted: each is armed by a request it clears before it can raise, so a raise does not bring it back, and a new request should run it again. They are logged in full every time, as before.
  - A CPU-limit kill inside a step is rethrown to the widget's entry point as before, which owns the back-off. It is counted as well since the entry for #473 and #474 below.
- **Telemetry Lost speaks for a flight controller that stops sending, and no longer for a lost RF link (`lib/audio.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry_bg/tasks.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/audio/events.md`)**:
  - The announcement was decided where the connection gate shuts, from the link sensors read on that pass. The gate shuts on `getRSSI()` reaching zero, and on a plain link loss `RQly` and the RSS sensors can still read a live link on that pass, so a lost RF link -- the radio's own announcement -- took the announcing branch. A flight controller that stops sending while the link is up never shuts the gate, so the case the announcement is for was never reached.
  - The telemetry drain now records when the flight controller's custom telemetry frames last arrived, in its own Lua state or through the background function script, and `Audio.process` announces once they have stayed away for 6 s while the model is armed and `getRSSI()` is above zero. Frames arriving again are the recovery. Neither connection gate announces anything any more.
  - The two files it plays, `stat/alerts/telemetrylost.wav` ("Telemetry Lost") and `stat/alerts/telemetryok.wav` ("Telemetry Recovered"), are added to both packs and declared in both word lists; without them the announcement was silent. The Link Quality help text and the audio documentation no longer say the files may be missing.

- **The saving box and every other notice or loading box are drawn centred on the screen (`ui/loading_overlay.lua`)**:
  - The box was placed 64 px above the centre of the area it was given, a fixed lift with no screen-size term, and then clamped to 8 px from the top. On 480x320 and 480x272 every box with a button -- the save progress and the save outcome with *Continue*, the armed notice, the connection notice -- reached the clamp and sat on the top edge of the screen with the rest of it empty below; on 800x480 the boxes stood 64 px above the centre.
  - The box is now centred vertically in its area, as it already was horizontally. Its height, its contents and the cap that keeps a long message on screen are unchanged. On a page the area is the content below the header, so a page's loading box is centred there.
- **Setup > Controls > Adjustments opens inside the dashboard, and names the adjustment functions in the radio's language (`app/pages/setup/controls/adjustments/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/controls/adjustments.md`)**:
  - Opened from the dashboard, the page stopped on *This page could not be drawn* on every visit: its build ran into the widget's instruction limit. Most of that build was the *Function* list. Each of its 83 names went through the translation call on every build, the finished list was sorted although it is already built in id order, and the flight controller's API version was parsed again for each of the 18 entries that depend on it.
  - The names are now a `labelKey`/`labelFallback` pair, which the packager resolves into the locale being built, so the build reads them without a lookup; the list is no longer sorted; and the API version is parsed once per build. The function name a slot shows in the *Range* list is found by its id instead of by a search through the list.
  - The translation call never found those names in an installed package, which carries no translation tables, so every locale showed them in English. A German package now shows *Nick-Rate* where it showed *Pitch Rate*. The English names are unchanged.
  - Counted under a per-instruction hook in desktop Lua 5.3 on the packaged tool, with the accounting's flight-controller stub answering: the page's build 16 979 -> 6 978 instructions, the pass that runs it 23 444 -> 13 443, the whole open 91 199 -> 71 215. The three reads it sends and the save are unchanged, and so is the scene it builds in English.
- **An adjustment announcement names every adjustment function the firmware defines, apart from the profile switches (`tasks/events/telemetry_bg/adjustments.lua`, `src/rfsuite/audio/`, `bin/sound-generator/json/`, `docs/audio/events.md`)**:
  - Pitch, roll and yaw RC expo (11-13), governor TTA gain (53), cross-coupling ratio (62), yaw dynamic ceiling gain (72) and the governor's idle, auto, maximum and minimum throttle and headspeed (76-80) had no entry in the word table, because the words to say them were missing from both packs, so changing one of them announced its value with no name.
  - Ten words are added to both word lists and built for `en` and `de` with the voice each language already uses: `expo`, `tta`, `ratio`, `ceiling`, `idle`, `auto`, `throttle`, `max`, `min` and `headspeed`. `adj/headspeed.wav` says the same as `stat/alerts/headspeed.wav`; the teller reads only `adj/`, so it has its own copy.
  - The battery profile (82) stays without a name, like the rate, PID, LED and OSD profile switches (1-4): its change is already announced by the battery profile announcement, and a second voice for the same event is worse than none.
- **The radio model keeps the craft name after a link drop inside the configuration tool (`tasks/events/runtime.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/model.md`)**:
  - With *Set Model Name on the Radio* on, a link that dropped for more than two seconds and came back while the configuration tool was open left the radio's model with its own name while the craft was connected, until the next disconnect. The tool put the model's own name back on the drop and used up the record of it; its reconnect does not rename (the rename is a widget task), and the dashboard widget, which EdgeTX does not run while a tool is open, never saw the drop.
  - The model's own name is now put back only by a widget, the same context that renames. A drop that is still in effect when the tool is closed is seen by the widget then, and a radio switched off while connected still gets the name back at the next start.
  - What it costs: a model that carries neither the dashboard widget nor the service widget any more when the craft disconnects keeps the craft name. The configuration tool used to put it back when it ran on that model with no link, and no longer does. The record stays on the card, and the name is put back once one of the two widgets is on the model again and runs without a link.
- **The Ports page offers a baud rate only where there is a choice, writes only the ports that changed, and reads them back after the save (`app/pages/setup/ports/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/ports/help.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/ports.md`)**:
  - A rate is a list only where the function offers more than one: MSP, GPS, MAVLink, Blackbox, and a custom port whose stored rate is not AUTO. A function fixed at AUTO -- the receiver, the ESC sensor, most telemetry -- shows AUTO as text, and a disabled port shows no rate. Each list is titled with its port's name instead of "Ports", and identifier 40 is named `LPUART1` instead of "Port 40".
  - A save writes only the ports whose record changed, in the same order, then stores and restarts as before. `MSP_SET_SERIAL_CONFIG` stores each record into the port its identifier names and nothing is checked before the EEPROM write, so the flight controller ends up with the same configuration; a save with nothing changed still stores and restarts.
  - Once a save reports done, the page reads the ports from the flight controller again, so the rows show what the board holds rather than what was sent -- including a configuration the firmware reset to its defaults because it found it invalid when it stored it. A save that times out reads nothing and leaves the page marked unsaved, as before.
  - Opening the page no longer reads the receiver configuration: `serialrx_provider` was stored and never shown or read anywhere. The function list is filtered by API version once per open instead of once per function per row, the exclusivity masks are collected once per build, and a function is found by its id instead of by a walk of the list.
  - Checked offline on the packaged page with an eight-port configuration (MSP, ESC sensor, receiver): after UART3 is set to GPS a save sends one write instead of eight, a finished save queues the port read and the row then shows what the board answered, a timed-out one reads nothing. `bin/accounting/measure.lua` is unchanged.
  - Counted offline under desktop Lua 5.3 on the packaged tool, from the press to the settled page, over twenty offsets of the periodic background work: the page's build **20 512 -> 10 176 instructions**; the heaviest pass of the open 26 864 -> 16 526; the whole open 87 524 -> 69 841, the difference being the receiver read and the per-row walks that are gone. No other page moves.
- **Four pages no longer read names that EdgeTX does not define (`app/pages/developer/msp_speed/page.lua`, `app/pages/logs/page.lua`, `app/pages/tools/diagnostics/smartfuel/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/am32/page.lua`, `docs/pages/developer/msp_speed.md`, `docs/pages/tools/diagnostics/smartfuel.md`)**:
  - *Developer > MSP Speed*: the Duration row read `labelW`, `comboX` and `buttonX`, whose locals had been removed together with the row's old fixed heights, so the label was built with no width and the Duration choice and the *Start*/*Stop* button with no x position. The three are computed again as they were, and as the API Tester page still computes them: the button right-aligned with a 10 px margin, the choice 8 px to its left, the label up to 8 px short of the choice.
  - *Logs*: the five value labels of a log's summary asked for `COLOR_WHITE`, which EdgeTX does not define, so they were drawn in EdgeTX's default for a label without a colour. They now ask for that colour, `COLOR_THEME_SECONDARY1`, by name, as the rest of the page does; nothing changes on screen.
  - *Tools > Diagnostics > SmartFuel*: the page called `Log.emit` but never loaded the log module, so all nine of its log lines were dropped. It now loads `lib/log.lua` as the SmartFuel setup page does: a read that fails is a warning and reaches the *Session Logs* page at any debug level; the start and end of each load and each read are debug lines, so a page opened while logging is off leaves nothing in the log.
  - *AM32 ESC page*: every row after the ESC target assigned and read `rowH` as a global, so each build left it in the tool's global table. It is declared local once, as on the BLHeli_S and Bluejay pages; the rows are laid out exactly as before.
- **The ESC tools menu reads the ESC telemetry protocol itself when it is missing, so the manufacturer entries light up in the tool opened from the dashboard (`ui/home.lua`, `bin/accounting/budgets.lua`) (fixes #506)**:
  - The ten manufacturer entries under *ESC & Motors* → *ESC Tools* are lit by the ESC telemetry protocol the flight controller reports. The on-connect read that fills it runs only where the event tasks run as the tool, so in the tool opened from the dashboard every entry stayed greyed out.
  - The menu's own read for that case looked for the MSP queue at `rfsuite.tasks.msp`, which nothing assigns, and never sent anything. It now queues through the MSP runtime, while the menu is open, a flight controller is connected and the protocol is still unknown.
  - A read that fails or answers short is asked again after five seconds rather than on every pass; leaving the menu and coming back asks at once. A request the queue drops unsent is asked again on the next pass.
  - The same read also refills the protocol in the standalone tool after a link drop shorter than two seconds, or after the on-connect read failed, where it used to stay empty until the next connection.
  - The read waits until the menu has taken over the queue's default client, so it is not filed under the menu being left and dropped with that menu's reads.
  - `bin/accounting/budgets.lua` gets rows for the AM32, BLHeli_S and Bluejay pages. The accounting's scripted flight controller answers the read with protocol 1, so with the read working these three pages are reached and measured for the first time; their builds are 4944, 5050 and 5126 instructions.
- **Select Profile offers only the profiles the board has, and says why it refused a save (`app/pages/tools/select_profile/page.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/pages/tools/select_profile.md`) (fixes #358)**:
  - The PID and rate profile lists are built from the counts the flight controller reports in its status reply instead of always offering six. A board with 256 kB of flash or less has three or two PID profiles and as few as three rate profiles, and `MSP_SELECT_SETTING` does not refuse an index beyond them: it switches to profile 1 and answers as a success. So choosing a profile such a board does not have switched it to profile 1, a different tune from the one chosen, with nothing saying so. Until the status reply has been read, or where it carries no count, both lists offer six as before.
  - The selected profile is kept inside the reported range in the lists. A SAVE whose choice lies beyond the board's count -- possible when the count arrives after the lists were drawn -- is refused and the lists are redrawn, instead of being written as some other profile. SAVE is held until the board has reported its counts at all; pressed before that, the host's notice asks for the read to finish.
  - A choice made before the page's own status read arrives is no longer replaced by that read. The PID write takes the selection only when the rate write is acknowledged, so a reply landing in between could send the board's current PID profile instead of the chosen one.
  - A SAVE the page refuses -- no MSP queue to send it on, or the previous change still being sent -- is reported through the save notice the host passes to every page, as on *Copy Profiles*. Before, the first returned a reason the host never shows and the second returned `false` with no reason at all, so the notice came and went with the screen unchanged.
  - The page has a documentation page.
- **The setup assistant's Link step asks before either *use this* button writes (`app/pages/setup_wizard/proc_radio.lua`, `docs/pages/setup_wizard.md`) (fixes #336)**:
  - Where the transmitter module and the flight controller disagree, each row of the Link step carries a *use this* button. One press started the sync at once: the flight controller's row sets the module's packet rate and telemetry ratio through `crossfireTelemetryPush`, the module's row writes the flight controller's telemetry configuration and saves it to EEPROM. Both start the same sync task (its own instance) that the two buttons on *Tools > Diagnostics > ELRS Link* start, and those already ask first.
  - Each button now puts up the question that page asks, in its words, with both rows quoted as the step shows them. Declining it writes nothing and the Probe row reads *Nothing was written* until the next probe, read or write starts; a radio that cannot put the question up writes nothing either. Where the arming state cannot be read, the question says so, under the key the header's Save uses.
  - No new strings: the question, the title and both notices are the ELRS Link page's own keys, borrowed by their full page key so the build resolves them into every language.
  - The two pickers on the same step are unchanged and still write on a pick, because the value there is the pilot's own choice. Writing nothing while the model is armed is still the task's own refusal and is unchanged.
- **Trims and Swash Geometry switch the override on only after the visit's read (`app/pages/setup/mixer/trims/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/mixer/swashgeometry/page.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`) (fixes #498)**:
  - The header star that switches the swash override on (Trims) or setup mode on (Swash Geometry) was offered as soon as the page opened. After a visit whose read failed, was rejected by its parser or had not returned yet, a confirmed star still sent `MSP_SET_MIXER_OVERRIDE` on all four axes, while the live write -- held back on such a visit since #496 -- sent none of the values changed on screen, although the confirmation says that live changes are sent.
  - The star now switches the override on only once the visit's read has succeeded -- the condition Save already waits for -- and is shown disabled until then. Switching it off stays available at any time, and closing the page still switches it off.
- **Fuel, current and consumption are read from the flight controller's sensors on a model that also still carries the native CRSF battery sensors (`lib/sensors.lua`, `docs/reference/sensor-selection.md`) (fixes #479)**:
  - The native CRSF battery frame creates `RxBt`, `Curr`, `Capa` and `Bat%`. A model whose sensors were discovered while the flight controller was on native telemetry keeps them after it is switched to custom telemetry, ahead of the flight controller's own `Curr`, `Capa` and `Bat%`, and the radio answers a sensor name with the first sensor carrying it. Fuel, current and consumption -- on the dashboard, in SmartFuel with the flight controller as its source, and in the flight record -- then read the native rows: no value at all where they were never received, their last value, frozen, where they were.
  - Where a name is carried by more than one sensor, it is read from the one the radio is receiving: the first, in the model's sensor order, that `getSourceValue()` reports as current; failing that, the first that has received anything; failing that, the name as before. The sensor is addressed by its slot name (`telemN`, with `+`/`-` for the extremes), and the zero test asks the same sensor, so a current of 0 A from the flight controller's row is taken. The choice is made on every read, so a native row still counted as current -- the radio keeps that up to 20 s after its last value -- gives way once it is not.
  - The model's sensors are listed with `model.getSensor()` only on a model that carries `RxBt`, the radio's own receiver battery sensor that native telemetry creates and the suite never does: about two seconds after each connection, and again when a sensor has been added since. A model without `RxBt` pays one `getFieldInfo()` per connection and otherwise reads every name as before.
- **Saving the ESC threshold again after a failed save writes it (`app/pages/settings/audio/events/category_page.lua`, `docs/audio/events.md`) (fixes #514)**:
  - With a flight controller connected, *Settings* → *Audio* → *Events* → *ESC* saves *Threshold [Model]* into the model's own file. A save whose write of that file failed reported the failure, but had already put the new value into the session's copy of the model's settings, and the page decides whether to write that file by comparing against the same copy. So the next *Save* found nothing changed, wrote nothing and showed the success it shows for a save that worked, and the threshold was back to its old value at the next start.
  - A failed write now puts the session's copy back to what it held before that save: the previous value, or no value where the model had none of its own. The page keeps showing the number that was entered, and the next *Save* writes it. *Reload* after a failed save shows the value from before the save instead of the one that was not saved.
  - Setting the number back to its loaded value after a failed save no longer gives a model that had no threshold of its own a copy of the radio-wide one.
- **A held step control on the in-flight tuning surface is no longer let go by a theme reload (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/dashboard/inflight-tuning.md`) (fixes #437)**:
  - A theme reload, a move of the voltage bounds or new widget options clear the widget's built flag and render key, and while the tuning surface was up that rebuilt the surface even with a step control held. The rebuild lets go of the hold before it clears the screen, so the held step ended with the finger still on the button -- most likely at the change into flight, which reloads the theme on a theme with an in-flight module.
  - The widget now remembers which surface the screen was built for and under which key. While a control is held and the pass would build that same surface again, the rebuild waits, and the first pass after the release draws it. Only a rebuild of the same surface under the same key waits; a build of a different surface is queued as before.
  - The wait is bounded: once a control has been held for five seconds -- 25 steps at the flight controller's repeat rate, the whole 0-250 range of most parameters at a step of 10 -- a rebuild still waiting then runs and ends the hold, as it did before. A release that EdgeTX never reports therefore cannot hold the rebuild back for as long as the surface is up.
- **Two call shapes the i18n precompiler did not rewrite now reach a packaged card (`.vscode/scripts/precompile_i18n.py`, `.vscode/scripts/test_precompile_i18n.py`, `app/pages/setup/power/{battery,alerts,preferences,sources}/page.lua`, `docs/developer/i18n.md`) (fixes #446)**:
  - `pageText(ctx and ctx.i18n, "key", "Fallback")`, the form 105 calls in 47 pages use for a save notice or a status text, is rewritten like `pageText(i18n, ...)`. Before, every one shipped as a call, and a packaged card, which carries no locale bundle, showed its English fallback: the German save notice of *Accelerometer*, for one, existed in `de.lua` and never reached the radio. 87 of the 105 calls ask for a key with a German text; the other 18 ask for keys neither bundle has and keep their English fallback. A fallback that is itself an `@i18n(...)@` marker is not carried inside the new marker, so a key missing from both bundles still stops the package build there.
  - The 19 row help texts of *Battery*, *Alerts*, *Preferences* and *Sources* are asked for through `pageText(i18n, "help_...")` instead of a page-local `optionalPageHelpText`, whose call sites the precompiler never rewrote. On a packaged card the rows had no help text in any language; now they carry the locale's text. Without an inline fallback a help key missing from both bundles stops the package build instead of shipping an empty help sheet.
- **Spoken numbers follow the WAV volume level, and an alert vibrates once (`lib/audio.lua`, `docs/pages/settings/audio/volume.md`, `docs/audio/events.md`) (fixes #400)**:
- **Spoken numbers follow the WAV volume level, and an alert vibrates once (`lib/audio.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry_bg/adjustments.lua`, `docs/pages/settings/audio/volume.md`, `docs/audio/events.md`) (fixes #400)**:
- **Spoken numbers follow the WAV volume level, and an alert vibrates once (`lib/audio.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry_bg/adjustments.lua`, `docs/pages/settings/audio/volume.md`, `docs/audio/events.md`, `docs/developer/audio-announcements.md`) (fixes #400)**:
  - Seven `playNumber` calls passed the level from *Settings > Audio > Volume* as the third argument, which EdgeTX reads as the number's attributes; the volume is its fourth. So the capacity, profile, temperature, link quality and fuel numbers were spoken at the radio's own WAV volume while the sound file before them played at the chosen level. They now pass `0` as the attributes and the level fourth, as the voltage callout already did.
  - The adjustment announcement spoke its value with a `playNumber` of its own, which carries no volume, after words that `lib/audio.lua` plays at the chosen level. The value now goes through a new `Audio.playNumber` in that module and is spoken at the same level as the words.
  - The alert vibration called `playHaptic(15, 10, 3)` as strength, duration and pause. EdgeTX takes `playHaptic(duration, pause [, flags])` and reads the low four bits of `flags` as a repeat count, so every alert that asks for haptic buzzed four times instead of once. It now calls `playHaptic(15, 3)`, one pulse, the values EdgeTX's own alarm vibration uses.
- **The Flyrotor ESC page logs a reply it refuses instead of ending the tool (`app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/flrtr/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/flrtr.md`) (fixes #406)**:
  - When the page's read came back and `EscParametersFlyrotorApi.parse` refused it -- shorter than the 56-byte block, or not starting with the Flyrotor signature `0x73` -- the branch meant to log that called `logMsg`, which the page no longer defined. The call raised `attempt to call a nil value (global 'logMsg')` out of the reply handler, and the MSP queue and the tool's `run()` call that handler unprotected, so the tool script ended on EdgeTX's script error screen.
  - The page has its own `logMsg` again, in the shape the BLHeli_S and Bluejay pages use: it loads `lib/log.lua` and emits under the tag `rfsuite.flrtr`. A refused reply is now a warning on the *Session Logs* page, at every debug level; the page leaves its values as they were and keeps Save refused until a read succeeds, as `docs/reference/saving.md` already describes.
  - No reply a supported flight controller sends is known to reach this branch: the firmware answers `MSP_ESC_PARAMETERS` with an MSP error until the Flyrotor block is complete, and with 56 bytes starting `0x73` once it is.
- **Two call shapes the i18n precompiler did not rewrite now reach a packaged card (`.vscode/scripts/precompile_i18n.py`, `.vscode/scripts/test_precompile_i18n.py`, `app/pages/setup/power/{battery,alerts,preferences,sources}/page.lua`, `docs/developer/i18n.md`) (fixes #446)**:
  - `pageText(ctx and ctx.i18n, "key", "Fallback")`, the form 105 calls in 47 pages use for a save notice or a status text, is rewritten like `pageText(i18n, ...)`. Before, every one shipped as a call, and a packaged card, which carries no locale bundle, showed its English fallback: the German save notice of *Accelerometer*, for one, existed in `de.lua` and never reached the radio. 87 of the 105 calls ask for a key with a German text; the other 18 ask for keys neither bundle has and keep their English fallback. A fallback that is itself an `@i18n(...)@` marker is not carried inside the new marker, so a key missing from both bundles still stops the package build there.
  - The 19 row help texts of *Battery*, *Alerts*, *Preferences* and *Sources* are asked for through `pageText(i18n, "help_...")` instead of a page-local `optionalPageHelpText`, whose call sites the precompiler never rewrote. On a packaged card the rows had no help text in any language; now they carry the locale's text. Without an inline fallback a help key missing from both bundles stops the package build instead of shipping an empty help sheet.
- **The setup assistant's Link help describes the step as it is (`i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/pages/setup_wizard.md`)**:
  - The `?` sheet of the *Link* step ended with *"Set module writes the transmitter, set board writes the flight controller"*, two buttons the step no longer has; each row now carries a *use this* button. It also said the picker lists come from the module, where they are the suite's own list reduced to what the module offers.
  - The sheet now has one intro line and then one line per row of the step, in screen order, each starting with the row's label. It also says that the module row's *use this* switches the flight controller's CRSF telemetry to Custom mode. Why the pair matters, and what the step shows and writes, are on the documentation page.
  - Five strings no code reads any more are removed from both locales: `link_match`, `link_agree`, `link_differ`, `link_set_module`, `link_set_board`.
- **The dashboard's voltage bounds follow the pack's cell count when a pack is plugged in with the flight controller already running (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`) (fixes #466)**:
  - The bounds the widget derives from the cell count remember the count they were derived for, and are derived again when the count moves. Before, bounds derived from a count inferred from a still rising pack voltage -- 11 for a 12S pack -- were kept after the flight controller reported 12, because per cell they still looked plausible, and the gauges stayed scaled to the wrong pack until the theme reloaded. The same happened at the connect itself, with no pack swap at all, where the pack voltage is read before the cell count: a 6S pack at 3.9 V per cell gave bounds for 5 cells that stayed.
  - While the flight controller's cell count reads 0, and on a model without that sensor, the widget takes the cell count configured on the flight controller before estimating one from the voltage, as the voltage callouts and the flight record already do. That is the count the board itself uses for any pack, so a pack of another count now shows the configured count instead of an estimate. Only a configured count of 0, the board detecting it itself, or a configuration not read yet still leaves an estimate.
- **The post-flight *Min V/Cell* tile divides by the cell count of the pack that flew (`widgets/dashboard/objects/text/stats.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`) (fixes #494)**:
  - After a landing, the flight's minimum pack voltage is divided by the cell count taken at the disarm, the count the per-cell landing voltage (`lastcell`) already uses. Before, it was divided by the live count, so plugging in a 12S pack after a 6S flight turned `4.07V/c` into `2.03V/c` on the three post-flight pages that carry the tile (default, @RT-RC, @RT-RC-N). During a flight, and where the pack read 0 V at the disarm, the live count is used as before.
- **A dashboard build that the radio stops at its instruction limit every time, or a free-form theme `build` that returns no node list, is given up after three tries instead of being run again for as long as the widget runs (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`, `docs/dashboard/user-themes.md`) (fixes #473, fixes #474)**:
  - A job step stopped at the CPU limit was not counted against the three tries a job that raises gets: the job dispatcher rethrew it to the widget's entry point first, which writes it to the card as a fault, waits 1.2 s and clears the job, and the next state pass armed the same build again. A build that always overruns -- a large free-form theme on a slow radio -- was therefore stopped at the limit roughly once per back-off for as long as the widget ran, each time with a fault line on the card. The stop is now counted under its job kind before it is rethrown, so after three in a row the widget draws *Dashboard error*, as it does for a build that raises. The entry point still owns the back-off and still writes a fault line for every stop, so three for a build that is stopped every time, until the next theme reload. This holds for every surface the count covers: the scene, the theme at full screen, the quick menu, the battery picker, a theme's own views, the in-flight tuning surface and the connect splash, each on its own count. A theme's own view that raises is still given up on its first raise; one that is stopped at the limit is now counted like the rest.
  - A free-form theme whose `build` returns anything but a node list -- a missing `return` is enough -- drew nothing, left the widget marked as not built, and was run again every other pass, with nothing in the log. The scene build and the fullscreen theme build now raise for it, with a message naming the theme, so it is logged, goes to the card once, and is given up after three tries like any other build that raises.
  - What it costs: a build that the radio stops at its limit only sometimes is still drawn as soon as one try completes, which starts the count over; but three stops in a row -- about two and a half seconds with the back-off -- leave *Dashboard error* up until the next theme reload, where the widget used to keep trying. A stop is not always the build's own doing, since the same pass also runs the MSP queue; the count still starts over only on a completed build. The in-flight tuning surface is one of the surfaces counted, so three stops of it in a row in flight leave *Dashboard error* in its place until the next theme reload. A theme that returns nothing on purpose while it waits for a reading now ends in *Dashboard error* instead of leaving the previous screen up; it has to return an empty list instead.
- **Texts asked for under keys no bundle had reach the pilot's language, and the resolver names the keys it answered from a call's own fallback (`i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `.vscode/scripts/resolve_i18n_tags.py`, the ten ESC manufacturer pages, `escmfg/hw5/profile.lua`, `setup/power/smartfuel/help.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/power/smartfuel.md`, `docs/developer/i18n.md`) (fixes #311)**:
  - Fifteen keys the governor, mixer, ports, alignment, adjustments and rate table pages ask for were in neither bundle, so every locale showed the call's English: the loading and saving overlays of the four governor pages, the loading overlay of the four mixer pages, of Ports and of Alignment, the titles of the Tail, Trims and Rate Table pages, and four labels on Adjustments. Fourteen are added to `en.lua` and `de.lua`; the Rate Table page asked for `table` and now asks for `rate_table`, which both bundles already had.
  - The save notices of the mixer, servo, alignment, configuration, ports and radio configuration pages asked for ten keys no bundle had; eleven are added. Those calls pass `ctx and ctx.i18n`, which the precompiler does not rewrite yet, so they take effect once it does (#561). The mixer's `saved_message` was asked for with two different texts and is now two keys.
  - The Hobbywing page's option lists used `api.ESC_PARAMETERS_HW5.*` keys that no bundle has, and a dictionary inside the resolver answered them in English for every locale. The seven words are keys of `app.pages.setup_esc_motors` in both bundles now, and the resolver's two ESC dictionaries are removed: no marker asked for their other entries, and a missing key of six or more parts no longer resolves to a capitalised fragment of itself instead of its own fallback.
  - The ten ESC manufacturer pages titled their loading and saving overlay with the throttle page's text ("Loading throttle configuration..."); they use their own `loading_title` and `saving_title` now.
  - The SmartFuel page's `?` showed the battery page's old help text. It describes the SmartFuel settings now, one line each. The *Charge drop rate* help and its documentation said the setting limits how fast the estimate may recover; the firmware uses it to limit how fast the estimate may drop (`slewDownLimit` in `sensors/smartfuel.c`), as the Configurator's help says, and both say so now.
  - The resolver counts the keys it answered only from a call's inline fallback, prints the count on its `DONE` line and lists the keys. The exit status is unchanged. Four comments that quoted the call shape were turned into such markers by the precompiler; they are reworded so that they are not.
- **The dashboard's BLACKBOX box reads READY instead of "MSP Wait..." on a board without dataflash (`i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`) (fixes #476)**:
  - Every shipped theme with a BLACKBOX box (Default, @AERC, @AERC Nitro, @RT-RC, @RT-RC Nitro) takes its label from `Common.blackboxLabel` in `themes/default/common.lua`, which `objects/text.lua` hands to every text box. It shows REC while armed, the used and total size while the flight controller reports a dataflash, LOGGED after an armed flight, and `widgets.dashboard.bb_ready` otherwise. That key was translated as a wait message ("MSP Wait..." in English), so a board without a dataflash chip -- whose storage summary reports a size of 0 -- showed a wait message from the connect to the first armed flight, with nothing being waited for.
  - The key reads READY again, in both languages, the same word as its fallback in `blackboxLabel` and in `objects/text/blackbox.lua`. The dashboard is drawn only once the on-connect reads, the storage summary among them, have finished (or after the splash's time limit), and a summary read that fails is asked again only after the next disarm, so a wait message in this place would either never be seen or stand until then.
  - On the four @AERC and @RT-RC themes, whose box switches to a smaller font above ten characters, the eleven-character wait message was drawn in the small font; READY is drawn in the box's own font.
- **Blackbox Status lights `*`, so the dataflash can be erased from the page, and keeps the erase on screen until it has finished (`app/pages/setup/controls/blackbox/status/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/blackbox/status/help.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/controls/blackbox/status.md`) (fixes #531)**:
  - The page asked for a `tool` header action, which the header never reads, instead of `star`, so `*` was drawn greyed out and its erase, behind a confirmation, could not be reached although the help text pointed to it. `*` is now lit once the flight controller has reported a dataflash, and greyed out on a board without one -- whose summary never reports a flash that is ready, so an erase the flight controller accepts there would be waited for indefinitely -- and while an erase is running.
  - Once reachable, the erase ended on the next pass: the wait for the flash to report ready again read the ready flag of the summary taken before the erase, and the rows showed the usage from before it. The page now waits for the flight controller to acknowledge the erase and for a summary asked after that, so *Erasing dataflash...* stays until the flash is ready again and the rows then show the usage after the erase.
  - While the erase runs, Reload is greyed out too (it reloaded the page state, which dropped the wait), the progress bar stays where the erase put it instead of jumping to full on every poll, and the erase command waits 10 s for its acknowledgement, as the quick menu's erase does, instead of being sent again after 2 s. The question before the erase is titled *Blackbox Status* instead of *Blackbox Configuration*.
  - The page has a documentation page.
- **The dashboard quick menu no longer offers ERASE BLACKBOX or BATTERY PROFILE while the model is armed (`widgets/dashboard/fullscreen_menu.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/views.lua`, `docs/dashboard/quick-menu.md`, `docs/pages/settings/dashboard/quick_menu.md`, `docs/dashboard/urban-theme.md`, `docs/developer/dashboard-views.md`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`) (fixes #418)**:
  - While the model is armed the MSP runtime clears its queue on every pass, so an erase or a profile write pressed in the air was queued, dropped within a fraction of a second and never sent -- neither in the air nor after the disarm -- while the press still closed full screen as though it had worked. Both entries now carry `visibleWhen = "modelDisarmed"`, like MAIN MENU, so the menu and a theme drawing the same records (Urban's *Profile & Tuning* page) no longer show them while armed.
  - A press on a row the menu does not offer now is refused: nothing is queued, nothing follows it, full screen is kept and the surface is built again without the row. A menu that was open when the model armed is not rebuilt on the arm edge, so its rows stay drawn; the same applies to an ERASE answered after the arm, and to the BATTERY row, which no longer opens the picker in the air. The battery picker's own picks and its close are not affected.
- **Both Servos pages save only what this visit read (`app/pages/setup/servos/pwm/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/servos/bus/page.lua`, `tasks/msp/api/servo_configurations.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`) (fixes #569)**:
  - PWM Output and BUS Output write the selected servo's whole record with `MSP_SET_SERVO_CONFIGURATION` from what the page holds. Neither page had the per-visit read flag and `canSave` the other read-backed pages use since #273: the page is kept between visits and puts its session copy back before the visit's read is queued, and a read that failed, was rejected by its parser or had not returned yet still completed the page. Save on such a visit wrote the servo record of an earlier visit, and committed it to EEPROM.
  - Save now waits for a read of this visit that parsed in full -- the mixer, status and serial replies as well as the servo record, since the servo count and the bus ports decide which servo the write addresses -- and, after another servo has been picked, for that servo's own read. Until then Save reports *Not saved* through the host's existing check, and a servo whose record this visit has not read shows no fields.
  - The header star that switches the servo override on waits for the same read and is shown disabled until then; switching it off stays available at any time. The live write under the override waits for it too.
  - `MSP_SERVO_CONFIGURATIONS` (the whole-table read below API 12.09) refuses a reply shorter than `1 + servo count * 16` bytes. It read the missing bytes as zeros, so a short reply became a servo record of centre 0, minimum 0, maximum 0, which Save then wrote.
  - PWM Output puts back, on Reload or when it is left with a change that was not saved, the value of this visit's read or of the last save that completed. It used to put back the first value the page had read since it was loaded, sending a centre from before a save to the flight controller (#579, the first of its three sites).
  - Reload on PWM Output reads the flight controller again after putting the values back, as BUS Output does. It used to only put them back, so a read that had not succeeded could not be repeated from the page, while the host's refusal tells the pilot to use Reload.
- **A failed save says why in words, not with a module name, a store token or a file path (`app/pages/settings/common.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/lib.lua`, `app/pages/settings/audio/events/category_page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/overrides/page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/quick_menu/page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/inflight/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/inflight/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/model_preferences.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/alerts/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/preferences/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/battery/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/smartfuel/page.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, docs) (fixes #513)**:
- **A failed save says why in words, not with a module name, a store token or a file path (`app/pages/settings/common.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/lib.lua`, `app/pages/settings/audio/events/category_page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/overrides/page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/quick_menu/page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/inflight/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/inflight/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/model_preferences.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/alerts/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/preferences/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/battery/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/smartfuel/page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/general/page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/localization/page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/audio/volume/page.lua`, `app/pages/developer/developer_settings/page.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, docs) (fixes #513)**:
  - *Settings* → *Audio* → *Events*, *Per-Model Settings*, *Setup* → *Controls* → *In-flight tuning* and the four *Power* pages that keep a copy in the model's file showed *Save failed: model_preferences* (or `model_preferences_unavailable`) when the module that writes the model's own file could not be loaded or failed as it ran. Since #539 the tool opened from the dashboard keeps a module it has loaded until it closes, as the tool started from the radio's menu does, so a file missing at the moment of the save no longer gets there; a first load or a run under `pcall` that fails still does. They now show the translated *model settings store not available* that the theme pages show since #509, and *Connect the flight controller to save this model's settings* where there is no board id to name the model's file by.
  - A write the card refused put the store's own answer on screen -- a token such as `io` or `rename`, or `io.open`'s message with the file's path (*Save failed: SCRIPTS:/TOOLS/rfsuite.user/....lua.tmp: file error*). These pages, and *Quick Settings* and *In-Flight Tuning* under *Settings* → *Dashboard*, now show *the settings file could not be written to the SD card*. The raw answer still goes to the log.
  - The sentences come from one function, `Common.saveFailureReason` in `app/pages/settings/common.lua`, which every one of these pages loads already. `DashboardLib.saveFailureReason`, which the theme pages call, now hands over to it, so there is one set of sentences.
  - *Setup* → *Controls* → *In-flight tuning* loaded the module with an `assert`, so a missing file raised and the loader's own error text became the notice. It now answers like the other pages.
  - The *Battery* and *SmartFuel* notices for a model copy that was not written (*Battery values sent to FC. Model prefs save failed: ...*, *FC write pending and model prefs save failed: ...*) and the *Warning* title on SmartFuel's two of them are translated. On these lines and on the *Save failed* notice of *Alerts* and *Preferences* the page's text call now passes `ctx.i18n` rather than `ctx and ctx.i18n`, the form the i18n precompiler rewrites, so a packaged card in another language shows them translated.
  - *Settings* → *General*, *Localization*, *Audio* → *Volume* and *Developer* → *Settings* put the same raw answer after *Save failed* when the radio's own file was refused, and now show the same sentence.
- **Adjustment announcements made by the background decoder follow the WAV volume level (`lib/audio.lua`, `docs/pages/settings/audio/volume.md`, `docs/reference/background-decoder.md`) (fixes #571)**:
  - `SCRIPTS/FUNCTIONS/rfsbg.lua` tells the adjustments whenever it runs, and loads `lib/audio.lua` in a Lua state where nothing ever resolved the level from *Settings > Audio > Volume*, so the words and the value were played at the radio's own WAV volume. In a state where neither the dashboard nor the tool drives the module, `Audio.playEventFile` and `Audio.playNumber` now read the level from the preferences on every call. The master-volume global variable is still written by the dashboard and the tool only.
- **Adjustment announcements made by the background decoder or the service widget follow the WAV volume level (`lib/audio.lua`, `docs/pages/settings/audio/volume.md`, `docs/reference/background-decoder.md`) (fixes #571)**:
  - Whenever `SCRIPTS/FUNCTIONS/rfsbg.lua` runs, it makes the adjustment announcements, and it loads `lib/audio.lua` in a Lua state where nothing ever resolved the level from *Settings > Audio > Volume*, so the words and the value were played at the radio's own WAV volume. The service widget runs the same announcements without the dashboard's audio pass, with the same result. In a Lua state where neither the dashboard nor the tool drives the module, `Audio.playEventFile` and `Audio.playNumber` now read the level from the preferences on every call. The master-volume global variable is still written by the dashboard and the tool only.
- **A card log's session file stops at its 5000-line cap for faults too, and stops holding lines once it has (`lib/log_sink.lua`, `docs/troubleshooting/collecting-logs.md`) (fixes #472)**:
  - With *Log Session To Card* on, a fault the suite caught -- a widget pass or a dashboard job that raised -- was appended to the session file without being counted and without looking at the cap, and it took every line held since the last write with it. A fault that repeats grew the file past its 5000 lines, and after the cap each one still wrote the lines held since. A fault's line now goes through the same count and the same cap as every other line: the write that crosses the cap ends with the *session file cap reached* line, and after it a fault reaches only the step file, which names it as before.
  - Once the cap is reached the logger stops collecting lines for the card. Before, it kept collecting up to 400 lines for the rest of the session, which only a fault would still write out. Turning *Log Session To Card* off and on again starts a new file, as before.
- **The documentation and the `?` text say what *Log Session To Card* writes on its own (`docs/troubleshooting/collecting-logs.md`, `docs/reference/background-decoder.md`, `docs/pages/developer/developer_settings.md`, `app/pages/developer/developer_settings/help.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`) (fixes #341)**:
  - *Collecting logs* said *Log Session To Card* and *Debug Level* are both needed and that a report asked for with one of them set comes back empty. With *Log Session To Card* on and the level at *OFF*, the configuration tool and the widgets already write their `tool_*` and `widget_*` files: errors, warnings and most info lines are kept at every level. Only the background decoder's `function_*` files need *DEBUG* or above as well, and the page now says so. It also no longer says that `widget_step.txt` needs the background decoder and *DEBUG*.
  - The background decoder's page said its log is written whenever *Log Session To Card* is on; it also needs the debug level at *DEBUG* or above.
  - *Collecting logs* also said that *TRACE* prints decoded telemetry payloads and that a session file reaches its 5000-line cap at *TRACE* in well under a minute. Among other lines, *TRACE* adds the raw bytes of every request to and reply from the flight controller, and a file grows by a few hundred lines while connecting and slowly after that, so the cap takes a long session.
  - The `?` text of *Developer* → *Settings* was one line about logging and debugging in general. It now has one line per setting, and the page has a documentation page.
- **The log graph's column sets plot the same column as the flight summary where a log repeats a column name (`app/pages/logs/graph.lua`, `docs/pages/logs.md`) (fixes #572)**:
  - A model that holds two sensors of the same name -- `Curr` and `Capa` from the native CRSF battery frame beside the same names from custom telemetry -- logs two columns of that name, and EdgeTX writes the one it is not receiving as 0. The summary read the last of them and the *Power*, *Battery* and *Governor* sets the first, so on such a log the graph drew current and capacity flat at 0 beside a summary with the flight's real figures.
  - The sets now take the last column of a repeated label too, the column the summary reads. A column of the same name with another unit is not a repeat: `Thr(%)` stays apart from the `Thr` stick column.
  - The summary is unchanged, and so is the cost of reading a log; the sets are resolved each time the column chooser is built, as before.
- **The setup assistant's overview turns its pages (`app/pages/setup_wizard/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup_wizard.md`) (fixes #570)**:
  - Where the overview's list of steps does not fit the screen it is cut into pages, and the heading says so (*Setup Assistant 1/2*), but nothing on the overview moved to the next page: the footer's *Continue* started the walk, the rotary cycled through page 1's buttons, and *Back* left the assistant. A step on page 2 -- *Link* on the radio section of an 800x480 radio -- could only be reached by walking every step before it to the end, and on a model whose CH5 to CH7 are not set up yet *Next* stays disabled on CH5 until a switch is chosen there.
  - While a later page is left, the button beside *Close* reads *Next* and turns to it, as *Next* does on a step's own screen; on the last page it reads *Continue* and starts the walk as before. *Back* on a later page turns back a page; on the first page it still leaves the assistant. An overview that fits on one page is unchanged.
  - The overview opens on its first page again after the assistant was closed from a later one.
- **Labels, statuses and notices whose key a page read out of a table reach an installed suite in the pilot's language (`app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/*/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/beepers/configuration/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/beepers/dshot/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/blackbox/logging/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/failsafe/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/alignment/page.lua`, `app/pages/tools/diagnostics/elrs_link/elrslink_task.lua`, `app/pages/tools/diagnostics/elrs_link/page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/general/page.lua`) (fixes #567; sections 1 to 3 of #595, section 7 of #612)**:
  - An installed suite carries no translation bundle, so a string is in the pilot's language only where the packager translated it at build time, and it does that only for a key it can read as a literal. Where a page took the key out of a table, the English fallback shipped in every language, although both bundles have the text.
  - The table entries are now `labelKey`/`labelFallback` pairs, which the packager already translates in place: the ten ESC pages' *Read the ESC before saving.* notice and FlyRotor's *ESC data could not be built* notice, with the dialog's *Error* title; the switch labels of both *Beepers* pages and of *Blackbox* > *Logging*; the *Failsafe* channel names; the *ELRS Link* status row, and the same status on the setup assistant's *Link* step; and the three questions *Settings* > *General* asks before a preview feature is switched on.
  - *Alignment*: the *Mag* choices had the key as their fallback, so every language, English included, showed `mag_default`, `mag_cw_90_flip` and the like. They now read *Default*, *CW 90 deg flip* and so on, translated.
  - *ELRS Link*: while a module field is written, the status keeps the field's name after the translated *Writing*. Where a translation was found, it used to replace the whole text and drop the name.
- **A telemetry sensor is taken for a value only while the radio is still receiving it, whatever it reads (`lib/sensors.lua`, `docs/reference/sensor-selection.md`) (fixes #363)**:
  - Before a sensor is taken for a value, the suite asks the radio whether it is still arriving. It used to ask whether anything had arrived for it since the model was loaded, which stays true across a dropped link and a sensor that has stopped, and it asked only about a reading of 0. `getSourceValue()` returns that answer as its second value -- false once a sensor has been silent for 20 seconds, the timeout behind the radio's *Sensor lost*, and for every sensor while the link is down -- and the test now reads it instead of only the first.
  - What changes: a sensor that stopped arriving before a value was matched to it is no longer taken, at its held value or at 0. A value whose list has a second sensor that is arriving is matched to that one, and the re-try of a list's first name no longer moves a value back onto a first sensor that is holding a stale reading.
  - A reading of 0 is no longer treated differently from any other: 0 A, 0 RPM and 0 degrees are taken exactly when any other reading would be.
  - A value whose sensor the model carries but the radio is not receiving at the moment -- the link is down, or the flight controller has stopped sending -- stays on the two-second retry instead of the wait that doubles up to half a minute, so it is picked up within about two seconds of coming back. The configuration tool forgets its choices only when its connection stops being ready, so with a doubling wait the link quality it waits for after a dropped link would have been held off for up to 30 seconds.
  - A session minimum or maximum (`Hspd+`, `RQly-`) is taken whenever its sensor has arrived since the radio last reset telemetry, as before: it stays a valid figure after the sensor has stopped.
  - Not changed: a sensor that stops after it was matched keeps its last value until the choice is made again on a link edge. A row a script writes before anything has arrived -- RFTool's background script does that for every sensor the flight controller is configured to send -- reads as arriving for 20 seconds after the write, which is the radio's resolution.
  - The question is asked where a value is matched and on the re-try of a list's first name, never on the read of a value already matched. The cost is that one call now also comes with a non-zero reading. `bin/accounting/measure.lua` moves four rows, all inside their targets: `pass.service` 5242 -> 5290, `unit.events.wakeup.armed` 4630 -> 4633, the *SmartFuel* diagnostics page's build 8017 -> 8117 and the *Battery* page's build 12448 -> 12473. Its stand-in for `getSourceValue()` returns the value only, which this test reads as arriving, so the accounting walks the same path as before plus that call.
  - *Sensor selection* now describes the 20-second timeout and what it does and does not separate.
- **Flight tuning pages take the flight controller's own modes, defaults and ranges (`app/pages/flight_tuning/advanced/rescue/`, `advanced/rates_advanced/table/page.lua`, `advanced/pid_controller/page.lua`, `advanced/main_rotor/page.lua`, `governor/page.lua`, `tune_advisor/page.lua`, `docs/pages/flight_tuning/`) (fixes #582)**:
  - *Rescue*: a profile set to altitude hold opened with *Rescue mode enable* off and its settings hidden, and switching it on wrote climb. Rescue now reads as on for either mode, a profile that holds altitude shows an *Altitude hold* switch, and the mode written is composed from the two switches: switching rescue off and on again keeps altitude hold, and *Altitude hold* off makes rescue climb instead. As in the Configurator, altitude hold is not offered to a profile that does not use it yet.
  - *Rate Table*: choosing the Rotorflight type and confirming the reset wrote rates of 245, 240 and 125 deg/s, a collective of 6.25 deg and no expo. It now writes the firmware's own Rotorflight defaults: 250, 250 and 400 deg/s, 12.5 deg collective, shape 12, expo 40, 40, 50 and 0.
  - *PID Controller* Inflight Error Decay *Limit* takes 0 to 250 and *Main Rotor* cross-coupling *Ratio* 0 to 200, the firmware's ranges; a board holding more than 25 or 100 used to show the lower ceiling and write it on the first wheel step.
  - *Governor* *Min Throttle* and *Max Throttle* take 10 to 100 %, the firmware's range, instead of 0 to 255; a value outside it was reported as saved and then replaced by the flight controller.
  - *Rescue* collectives move in whole percent per wheel step; one step was 0.1 % and showed no change on nine steps of ten.
  - *Tune Advisor* suggests a rate no higher than the *Rates* page takes for that column (1000 deg/s for Actual and Rotorflight), instead of up to raw 255 (part of #589).
- **A profile switch under an open tuning page no longer writes one profile's values into another (`app/pages/flight_tuning/pids/`, `rates/`, `governor/`, `advanced/` except `filters/`, `app/pages/setup/power/sources/`, `ui/home.lua`, `lib/profile.lua`, `tasks/msp/cache.lua`, `tasks/msp/queue.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`, `docs/pages/flight_tuning/rates.md`) (fixes #577)**:
  - `MSP_SET_PID_TUNING`, `MSP_SET_RC_TUNING`, `MSP_SET_GOVERNOR_PROFILE`, `MSP_SET_PID_PROFILE` and `MSP_SET_RESCUE_PROFILE` carry no profile index, so the flight controller stores the record in whichever profile is active when it arrives. PIDs, Rates and Governor checked for a switch only while nothing was edited, so an edit, a switch and Save wrote the old profile's whole record, plus the edit, into the new one and reported it saved. The advanced pages noticed the switch and re-read, dropping the edit without a word.
  - Every one of these pages now refuses Save while the active profile differs from the one its values were read from: **Not saved**, with a notice that the profile changed after the page was read. Nothing is written and no EEPROM commit is queued. The edit stays on the page; switching back makes Save available again, and Reload reads the active profile and replaces it. A page with nothing edited reads the new profile as before. A page may now give the host a reason as the second value of `canSave()`; `"profile_changed"` is the one with a notice of its own (`app.save.profile_changed`).
  - The page heading names the profile the values on screen were read from, so an edit kept across a switch is not shown under the new profile's number.
  - What Save is held to is the profile the values on screen were read under, recorded when a read's reply is taken into the page. A reply that lands on a pending edit is not taken in -- Rates and the advanced pages used to load it over the edit -- so an edit made while the page re-reads after a switch is kept and Save refuses it. A page takes a new profile over only together with a read sent under it, so a switch while a read is out, or a Reload pressed then, is read again once that read has landed rather than being swallowed by it.
  - *Rates (Advanced) > Rates Table* checks again when its reset question is answered, since the question can stand across a switch.
  - Rates is redrawn after every read, so a switch with nothing edited shows the new profile's number in the heading and its polar or standard rows; it used to be redrawn only when the rates type differed.
  - *Power* → *Sources* sends back the per-battery-profile cell values the read carried, as the *Battery* page does, so on MSP API 12.10 its Save no longer stores the cells it read under one battery profile into whichever battery profile is active when the write arrives.
  - The MSP response cache keeps an `MSP_PID_TUNING` reply only under the profile the telemetry reports (`pid_profile`, or the *System Config* sensor), never under the profile the connection reported, which does not follow a switch made on the transmitter; and only if that profile is the same when the reply lands as when the request went out. A model without such a sensor now reads its PID gains from the flight controller on every visit to PIDs (part of #588).
- **The setup assistant counts a channel as done only where the board acts on it as the assistant writes it, and its writes keep what they did not read (`app/pages/setup_wizard/proc_radio.lua`, `app/pages/setup_wizard/radio.lua`, `app/pages/setup_wizard/proc_board.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/pages/setup_wizard.md`) (fixes #584)**:
  - CH5 Arming and CH8 Rescue counted as done when the board had a range for the mode on the channel's aux slot in any window. On a board configured elsewhere with ARM at 900-1300, the channel and the Write step read done, nothing was written, and the model armed at the other switch position. A channel is now done only with a range in the window the assistant writes, 1700-2100, and none in another window on that slot; Write moves such a range instead of adding a second one. CH7 Profile is done only where both adjustments read the channel's own aux slot over its whole travel onto profiles 1 to 3.
  - Orientation read the alignment through a `.parsed` wrapper the API no longer returns, so it showed *Default* on every board and a pick wrote zeros for the second gyro and the magnetometer. It now reads the flat fields, writes those two back as read, and where the read gets no reply it says *could not be read* and writes nothing.
  - An adjustment slot whose reply was lost during the Profile channel's read counted as neither used nor free, so a profile function held in it could be given a second slot. While one of the two profile functions has not been found, such a slot now blocks the Profile row, the Write screen says why, and opening the step again reads the table again. After a Write that moved a profile adjustment, the step reads the table again before CH7 counts as done.
  - *Set up* on the Sticks step and *Write* replace inputs at fixed indices. Where another channel's mixer line uses one of those inputs, they now ask first and name the input and the channels (a line that inverts its source counts, channels the same press lays out do not); declining writes nothing, the trims on the Sticks step included, and a plan that changes while the question stands is asked about again.
  - Naming a channel rewrote its output without the curve, and EdgeTX's `model.setOutput` clears the whole output first, so the stick step erased any output curve on CH1-CH4. The curve is now carried across.
- **BLHeli_S and Bluejay settings are offered under the numbers the ESC stores them as, so a choice reaches the ESC as the choice it names (`app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/blheli_s/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/bluejay/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/`) (fixes #575)**:
  - Both firmwares number these settings from 1 -- direction 1 = Normal, 2 = Reversed, 3 = bidirectional, 4 = bidirectional reversed -- and the pages numbered them from 0 while sending the chosen value to the ESC as the byte. Every pick on those rows therefore reached the ESC one entry off, and a factory ESC was shown one entry off. On Bluejay, *Normal* wrote 0, which the firmware decodes as bidirectional and reversed.
  - Renumbered: *Motor Direction*, *Motor Timing*, *Demag Compensation* and *Beacon Delay* on both pages, *Startup Power* on BLHeli_S and *Power Rating* on Bluejay. The last entry of each list -- *Bidirectional 3D Rev*, *1.50*, *High* timing, *High* demag, *Infinite*, *2S+* -- now matches a byte the ESC can hold; before, the top byte of each range had no entry at all, Bluejay's factory power rating (2, *2S+*) among them.
  - Not changed, because the firmware stores them raw or numbers them from 0: *Temperature Protection*, *Brake on Stop*, Bluejay's *Rampup Power*, *PWM Frequency*, *LED Control*, *Startup Beep*, *Force EDT Arm*, *Dithering* and *Low RPM Power Protection*. The bytes on the wire are what they were: the parameter modules read and write them unchanged, and a Save with nothing edited still writes back the block it read.
  - A byte with no entry in its list -- a 0 written by an earlier version of these pages, say -- is shown as *Unknown (n)*, cannot be chosen, and is written back as it was read. Before a read has arrived, *Motor Direction*, *Motor Timing*, *Demag Compensation* and BLHeli_S's *Startup Power* show *Unknown (0)* rather than *Normal*, *Low*, *Off* or *0.031*.
  - **An ESC saved from an earlier version of these pages holds these settings one entry off what was picked there** -- *Reversed* was stored as Normal, and Bluejay's *Normal* as bidirectional reversed. The pages now show what the ESC holds, so check direction and the other renumbered rows after updating.
- **Configuration, Radio Config, Accelerometer, SmartFuel and Copy Profiles save only after the flight controller has been read (`app/pages/setup/configuration/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/radio_config/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/accelerometer/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/power/smartfuel/page.lua`, `app/pages/tools/copy_profiles/page.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`) (fixes #573; site 3 of #589)**:
  - These pages had no read check, so Save after a read that timed out, failed or was refused by its parser wrote the page's starting values as if they were the board's. On *Configuration* that is a feature word of 0, an empty craft name and a restart: the board comes back without its serial receiver, so the link the suite talks over is gone. *Radio Config* wrote centre 1500, deflection 510 and throttle 1100/1900, *Accelerometer* trims of 0/0, and *SmartFuel* mode off with its default rates.
  - Each now has the per-visit read flag and `canSave` the other read-backed pages use: Save is held until every reply of this visit's read has parsed, and the host's notice says so. A reply the parser refuses ends the read as failed.
  - *Copy Profiles* holds Save until the board has reported how many profiles of the selected kind it has, as *Select Profile* does; before that the lists offer six and a copy onto a profile the board does not have is ignored there while it still answers as done. The page now has Reload, which reads the counts again, and a choice beyond the board's profiles made before the counts arrived is refused rather than sent. The lists no longer move such a choice into range on a rebuild, where it would have reached the overwrite question as a profile the pilot did not pick; it shows as *Unknown (n)* until a profile is chosen again.
- **A held step control on the in-flight tuning surface ends after five seconds, whether or not its release is reported (`widgets/dashboard/inflight/drive.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/inflight/screen.lua`, `docs/dashboard/inflight-tuning.md`) (fixes #624)**:
  - A held `+` or `-` keeps the row's magnitude on the value variable, and the flight controller repeats the step every 200 ms. The hold ended only at the button's release handler, which EdgeTX raises on `LV_EVENT_RELEASED` alone. LVGL can forget a pressed button without that event -- EdgeTX's touch read does so when a touch arrives with the backlight off (an alarm flash, the backlight timeout) or under a *Disable Touch* special function -- and neither `LV_EVENT_PRESS_LOST` nor an input reset reaches a Lua script. The hold then stood after the finger was lifted, and the board went on stepping until something else ended it.
  - The drive now lets go of a hold five seconds after the press -- 25 steps, the same limit the dashboard already puts on a rebuild held back by a held step -- and the magnitude falls to 0 on the same pass. The surface shows *hold ended after 5 s - press again*, or *5 s over - press again* where the line is too narrow. A release that arrives later changes nothing; a new press starts a new hold.
- **Mixer > Tail keeps the yaw limits and centre trim the flight controller holds when Tail Mode is changed and starts Yaw Calibration from the new mode's value, and a Tail Mode or Swash Type change that did not finish saving still restarts the flight controller on the next Save (`app/pages/setup/mixer/tail/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/mixer/swash/page.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`) (fixes #578)**:
  - The page read the two yaw limits and the centre trim in the unit of the mode it read -- percent for a motorised tail, degrees for variable pitch -- and wrote them back in the unit of the mode selected at Save. A Tail Mode change with nothing else edited therefore rescaled all three: from variable pitch to a motorised tail the firmware's default limits of 1250 went out as 300, from a motorised tail to variable pitch as 5208, twice the firmware's own maximum. The flight controller applies the same stored numbers in every tail mode, so the page now holds them as stored and converts only on screen; a field that is not edited is written back as it was read. The Rotorflight Configurator handles the change the same way.
  - Swash and Tail decide whether a Save restarts the flight controller by comparing the swashplate type or the tail mode with what the page read. The Save wrote the new value into that read record before the save had started, so a Save that was refused (another save running, the model armed) or failed after the mixer configuration was written left the new value in it, and the next Save found nothing changed and did not restart the board -- although the new mixer mapping is built only at startup. The save is now built on a copy, and the read record takes the written values only once the save is done.
  - A Tail Mode change sets *Yaw Calibration* to 100 % for a motorised or bidirectional tail and 25 % for variable pitch, keeping the yaw direction, as the Configurator does on the same change; before, the calibration of the previous mode was kept.
  - Not changed: a stored value beyond a field's range in the new mode is shown at the field's end and written back unchanged unless that field is turned; a turn that ends at the end stop writes the end value.
- **MSP API 12.08 (Rotorflight 4.5) is no longer supported, and a refused flight controller is rejected cleanly on connect (`lib/version.lua`, `tasks/msp/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/runtime.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `widgets/service/runtime.lua`, `README.md`, `docs/troubleshooting/no-connection.md`, `docs/reference/connection-status.md`) (fixes #613)**:
  - The suite connects to MSP API 12.09 and 12.10, Rotorflight 4.6 and later. Several pages already parsed or sent the 12.09 layout only and could not complete their read on a 12.08 board; that board is now refused like any other unsupported version.
  - The tool's *Unsupported MSP API* notice names the version the board answered with and the firmware the suite needs. It named version 0 before, because the refusal resets the version it reads.
  - The dashboard stays on its start screen and its status line says the same, instead of showing a connect step that never finished and then drawing the dashboard after its 25 s soft timeout. The service widget's tile names the refused version as well.
  - The connect sequence does not run against a refused board. Nothing is sent to it, so each step only waited out its timeout and retries in turn.
- **Motor Pole Count, Scorpion Bailout Time and YGE Current Limit offer only values their wire format carries (`app/pages/setup/esc_motors/rpm/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/scorp/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/yge/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/`) (fixes #576)**:
  - *Motor Pole Count* ran to 256 on a one-byte field, so the top value reached the flight controller as 0 poles, which leaves the motor with no RPM source after the page's restart. It now stops at 254.
  - *Bailout Time* on the Scorpion page ran to 100 s on a 16-bit count of milliseconds, so 66 s to 100 s wrapped on the ESC to under 35 s (66 s arrived as 0.46 s). It now stops at 65 s.
  - *Current Limit* on the YGE page stepped in whole amps from 0.01 A, so every value it offered below the top of the range ended in .01 A: a limit of 100 A read from the ESC was shown as 100.01 A, and any edit wrote x.01 A. It now runs from 1 A to 655 A on whole amps.
- **Modes writes only the ranges that were changed, and covers itself while it writes; Reload on Adjustments discards every change (`app/pages/setup/controls/modes/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/adjustments/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/controls/modes.md`, `docs/pages/setup/controls/adjustments.md`, `docs/reference/saving.md`) (sites 1, 3 and 4 of #581)**:
  - *Modes* wrote all 20 mode ranges and committed EEPROM on every Save, edited or not. Each `MSP_SET_MODE_RANGE` makes the flight controller set its mode conditions up again, and every range went out with its AUX index clamped to AUX 13, so a range another tool had stored above that was moved by a save that never touched it. The page now keeps the set of ranges edited since its last read or save, as *Adjustments* and *Failsafe* do, writes only those, and writes EEPROM only after at least one of them; a Save with nothing edited sends nothing. A failed write keeps the set, so the next Save sends the same ranges again.
  - *Modes* stayed editable while its save ran. The save reads each range only when it reaches it, so an edit to a range already sent -- ARM's included -- was dropped, *Unsaved changes* was cleared and *Mode configuration saved* reported while the page went on showing the unsent value. The page is now covered by the save's progress, as *Adjustments*, *Failsafe* and *Stats* are, and the header's Save and Reload, which stay reachable over it, do nothing until the save has finished: a second Save used to start a second chain beside the first, and a Reload a read whose replies replaced the ranges the chain had still to send.
  - Reload on *Adjustments* asked whether to discard unsaved changes and then kept the edited slots marked for writing. From MSP API 12.09 it reads again only the selected slot, so the next Save wrote a discarded edit on any other slot to the flight controller and reported *Adjustment configuration saved*. Reload now discards the marks on both pages, together with an *AUTO* channel detection still waiting for a switch.
  - *Modes* has a documentation page.
- **Picking another servo on Setup > Servos asks before an unsaved change is dropped (`app/pages/setup/servos/pwm/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/servos/bus/page.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`) (site 2 of #579)**:
  - Both pages show one servo at a time, keep one unsaved-changes flag and save only the servo that is selected. An edit on one servo followed by picking another and Save wrote the second servo, cleared the flag and reported *Servo settings saved*, while the first servo's change never reached the flight controller and was still on screen when it was picked again.
  - Picking another servo with a change on screen now puts up the same *Unsaved changes* box Back puts up on the other pages, with a message about the servo: *Stay* keeps the servo and its change, *Save* saves it and picks the other servo once the flight controller has stored it (a refused or failed save picks nothing and keeps the change), *Discard* drops the change and picks the other servo. The box is the host's; a page can now put it up for a change of its own with `askUnsavedChanges`, giving the message and what Save and Discard do there.
  - On PWM Output, Discard puts back the values read from (or last saved to) the flight controller and sends the centre again where it differs, as Reload does; BUS Output keeps no such copy and reads the servo again the next time it is picked (a centre sent live under the override is still on the flight controller and is what that read shows). A Save whose write fails now leaves the page's change marked unsaved instead of clearing the mark.
- **Setup > Power > Battery sends a cell voltage back as the flight controller holds it unless it is edited, and the four cell voltage fields take 1.00 V to 5.00 V (`app/pages/setup/power/battery/page.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry/flight_record.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/power/battery.md`) (site 1 of #589)**:
  - On firmware with one set of cell voltages, the page raised a cell voltage below 2.50 V to 2.50 V when it read it and wrote that back on every save, edited or not. A NiMH or LiFe pack with a minimum of 2.30 V had its minimum raised to 2.50 V by a save that only changed a capacity.
  - The fields started at 2.50 V and showed a lower value as 2.50 V, on per-profile firmware too. They now cover the board's own range, 1.00 V to 5.00 V, as the Rotorflight Configurator does.
  - The flight log's sag count judged a minimum cell voltage only from 2.50 V, the page's old floor, and left `sags` empty below it. It now takes the same 1.00 V to 5.00 V.
- **The flight statistics take no reading while the telemetry link is down (`tasks/events/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry/flight_record.lua`, `docs/reference/flight-statistics.md`) (site 3 of #587)**:
  - EdgeTX answers every telemetry value with 0 once the link has not streamed for a second. The record kept sampling through such a gap while the craft was armed, so a short RF gap in flight put a 0 into the lowest ESC temperature and, depending on where the fuel figure came from, the lowest fuel, and from there into the flight log's `tesc_min` column and the post-flight page.
  - Nothing is sampled while the link is not streaming, and sampling resumes as soon as it is back. The flight clock keeps running through the gap.
  - Unchanged: a gap long enough for the link to count as lost (down for 2 s, about 3 s after the last frame) still ends the session, and the record starts again when the link comes back.
