- **A connect no longer takes the previous craft's name, model-name flag or SmartFuel configuration, and the model is not renamed when its own name cannot be recorded (`tasks/events/onconnect/tasks/name.lua`, `tasks/events/onconnect/tasks/model_params_sync.lua`, `tasks/events/common/smartfuel_config.lua`, `tasks/events/onconnect/tasks/uid.lua`, `tasks/events/onconnect/tasks/model_name_sync.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/model.md`) (sites 1 and 2 of #587, #428)**:
  - The connect tasks that read the craft name, the pilot config with its model flags and the SmartFuel configuration left them in the session for the next connect: nothing cleared the craft name or the SmartFuel configuration, and the pilot config was cleared only in its task's `reset()`, which never runs because the runner drops a completed task's module without it. With the radio left on and a second flight controller connected whose read failed, the model was renamed to the first craft's name, on the first craft's flag, and the local SmartFuel calculation used the first board's drop rates and sag gain. Each task now drops its value as its read starts, so a failed read leaves nothing rather than the previous board's value: no craft name means no rename, and no flag means the radio's own preference decides.
  - Not at the connect edge: a reconnect while armed runs no connect task until the disarm, and the same craft's name and SmartFuel configuration have to stay for that flight and for the flight log.
  - The `uid` task's `modelPreferencesResolved` was the previous link's in the same way until the task completed; it is now cleared as the task runs. The flag the task waits on, `uidReadSettled`, cannot be the previous link's when the task is woken on a new link: the connect runner is woken only once the MSP runtime reports the link up, and the runtime clears that flag in the same step.
  - The model is renamed only once its own name is recorded on the card. A refused record was ignored and the model renamed anyway, so a radio switched off while connected kept the craft name for good; the model now keeps its own name for that connection, and the refusal is logged. The same holds where the store cannot be written at all, and for a record carried over from the former per-board file whose move to the store is refused.
- **An ELRS link sync confirmed before the model was armed no longer resumes and writes at the next disarm (`lib/armed.lua`, `app/pages/tools/diagnostics/elrs_link/elrslink_task.lua`, `app/pages/tools/diagnostics/elrs_link/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup_wizard/proc_radio.lua`, `docs/pages/tools/diagnostics/elrs_link.md`, `docs/pages/setup_wizard.md`) (site 2 of #589, site 3 of #614)**:
  - The tool does not wake a page while the model is armed, so the sync's armed check never ran during the armed period: a transfer that was walking the module's parameters, or waiting between its writes, carried on at the first pass after the disarm and sent its `PARAMETER_WRITE` frames, or the flight controller's telemetry configuration and EEPROM commit, on a yes given before the flight. The same held for the setup assistant's Link step, including a *use this* answered and a picker set while armed.
  - `lib/armed.lua` now counts every change of its answer, which the tool asks on every pass whether or not it wakes the page. The sync notes the count when it is confirmed and stops at the first pass on which it has moved: nothing more is sent and the Status row reads *Stopped: model was armed*, also when the arming clears a flight controller write that was already queued. A write that had already left before the arming is not undone. A probe that had already started its walk when the model was armed stops the same way rather than ending on a half-read walk, which recorded what it had read as the module's settings. Where the suite's arming check itself is missing, the row says *Arming state unknown* and nothing is written. All of this rests on the flight controller's arming flags reaching the radio: an arming between two readings of them is not seen, and without them nothing stops a sync at an arming (the question then says the arming state cannot be read).
  - On *Tools > Diagnostics > ELRS Link* the Status row reads *Unavailable while armed* for as long as the model is armed and nothing else afterwards, and *Probe* is refused while armed like the two syncs; so is *Read* on the setup assistant's Link step. Before, a *Probe* pressed while armed greyed out all three buttons until the disarm with the row showing *Idle*, and a refused press left *Unavailable while armed* on the row of a disarmed model.
- **An MSP request the flight controller answers with an error reply is given up as `refused`, and Developer > MSP Speed counts timeouts (`tasks/msp/queue.lua`, `tasks/msp/service.lua`, `app/pages/developer/msp_speed/page.lua`, `docs/pages/developer/msp_speed.md`) (site 2 of #588, #638)**:
  - A request the flight controller answered with an error reply was retried and then given up as `max_retries`, the same reason as a request nobody answered. It is still retried, because the firmware answers some reads with an error until it has the data (an ESC's parameters, before the ESC has sent them), but once the retries run out it is now given up as `refused` when an error reply came back, and as `max_retries` only when nothing did.
  - The queue's `timeout` reason could never arrive: its branch could only fire where the retries-exhausted branch had already fired and returned. It is removed, with the `setErrorHandler` hook that nothing set. *Timeouts* on MSP Speed counted only `timeout` and so stayed at 0; it now counts `max_retries`, and a refusal shows in *Last Error*.
  - The `rfsuite.msp` request surface documented `timeout` and `aborted`, which never arrived, and passed on `cleared`, which it did not document. It now documents and passes on the four reasons that arrive (`refused`, `max_retries`, `cleared`, `cancelled`), and its contract version is 2, so a script that relies on `refused` can ask for it at `register()` and is refused on an install that does not deliver it.
- **Modes and Adjustments read AUX 4 and above from the channel the flight controller reads (`app/pages/setup/controls/modes/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/adjustments/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/controls/adjustments.md`) (site 2 of #581)**:
  - Both pages counted AUX 4 to AUX 13 on from the radio channel the receiver's channel map gives AUX 1. The flight controller's map covers only its first eight channels, whose last three are AUX 1 to AUX 3, so AUX 4 and above always sit on radio channels 9 and up. On a model whose map moves AUX 1 the live value, the in-range marker and *AUTO* read another channel than the one the flight controller uses, and *AUTO* stored the AUX number of whichever channel it saw move. The ELRS channel order the setup assistant writes puts AUX 1 on channel 5, so there every AUX 4 and above was read one channel too low; a map that leaves AUX 1 on channel 6 was not affected.
- **A read that carries an index is no longer taken for a write (`tasks/msp/queue.lua`, `tasks/msp/service.lua`) (site 3 of #588)**:
  - The MSP queue took any request with a payload and no `isWrite` for a write. Setup > Mixer > Swash, Swash Geometry and Tail read their mixer inputs with `MSP_GET_MIXER_INPUT` and the input's index as the payload, so each of those reads cleared the whole response cache on its way out (the craft name, feature, advanced and PID tuning replies), and a read still waiting when its page was left was kept like a write instead of being dropped with the page's other reads: its reply then ran against the closed page and stopped the tool (`attempt to index a nil value`). Every indexed read sent through `rfsuite.msp` cleared the cache the same way. Those reads also went out over CRSF as an MSP write frame rather than a request frame, which the flight controller answers the same way.
  - The queue now knows the commands the firmware answers as a read with an argument -- `MSP_GET_MIXER_INPUT`, `MSP_GET_SERVO_CONFIG`, `MSP_GET_BUS_SERVO_CONFIG`, `MSP_GET_ADJUSTMENT_RANGE`, `MSP_RPM_FILTER_V2`, `MSP_BOXNAMES`, `MSP_BOXIDS`, the two VTX table reads and `MSP_DATAFLASH_READ`, the read cases of the firmware's `mspFcProcessOutCommandWithArg` and its dataflash read -- and treats a payload on one of them as the index of a read. A payload on any other command is still a write, and a request that sets `isWrite` is still taken at its word.
  - `rfsuite.msp` requests take an optional `isWrite`, passed on to the queue when it is a boolean -- for a write without a payload, which the queue would otherwise take for a read.
- **Adjustments, Failsafe, Blackbox Configuration and Logging, Stats and both Beepers pages ignore Save and Reload while their save is running (`app/pages/setup/controls/adjustments/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/failsafe/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/blackbox/configuration/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/blackbox/logging/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/stats/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/beepers/configuration/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/controls/beepers/dshot/page.lua`, `docs/pages/setup/controls/adjustments.md`, `docs/reference/saving.md`)**:
  - *Adjustments*, *Failsafe* and *Blackbox Configuration* send their save as a chain: each write after the first is built from what the page holds when the one before it is answered, and an EEPROM write ends it. The page is covered by the save's progress meanwhile, but the header's Save and Reload stay reachable over it. A second Save started a second chain beside the first, so every edited slot or channel was written twice with two EEPROM writes, and the first chain's reply cleared *Unsaved changes* while the second still ran. A Reload put the page back to the values last read, or started a read whose replies did so, while the chain still had writes to build from them: on *Adjustments* a changed slot not yet sent went out with the function the flight controller had stored -- and with every field as stored if it was the slot the Reload reads in full (the one the page shows after it), or for every slot below MSP API 12.09; on *Failsafe* a changed channel went out with its stored mode and value; on *Blackbox Configuration* the debug settings went out as stored while the logging settings went out as edited. The EEPROM write then stored that mix.
  - *Stats*, both *Beepers* pages and *Blackbox Logging* send a single write, built when Save is pressed, then the EEPROM write. A second Save during it sent the same write and a second EEPROM write.
  - Save and Reload now have no effect on all seven pages while the save runs, as on *Modes* (a confirmation the preferences ask for first is still shown). Once it has finished they work as before.
- **Three documentation pages say what the pages do (`docs/pages/flight_tuning/tune_advisor.md`, `docs/pages/setup/power/preferences.md`, `docs/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/yge.md`) (sites 9 and 10 of #589, #355)**:
  - *Tune Advisor*: with polar cyclic rates the *Rates* page's one *Cyclic* row edits the Pitch rates, which the flight controller then applies to the whole cyclic stick; the Roll rates are left as they were. The page said the row set Roll and Pitch together. It now also says that the advisor does not take polar into account: a suggestion for Roll works its values out from the Roll rates, which are not in use while polar is on.
  - *Power Preferences*: the battery alerts under *Setup* → *Power* → *Alerts* are kept on the radio's card and checked on the radio; the page said they were the flight controller's and written to it.
  - *YGE*: the *ESC Target* row is gone from the settings table; the page has not had that control since it was removed from the ESC pages that cannot address a second ESC.
- **A dashboard on another screen page is no longer recorded as stopped (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `src/functions/rfsbg.lua`, `docs/troubleshooting/collecting-logs.md`, `docs/reference/background-decoder.md`) (site 4 of #588)**:
  - The dashboard published its heartbeat for the background decoder only from `refresh()`. EdgeTX calls a widget that is not on the visible screen -- on another screen page, or behind one of the radio's menu pages -- through `background()` instead, and the dashboard's background work runs there; so after 3 s on another page the background decoder wrote `dashboard widget silent` for a widget that was running, a record meant for one the radio has stopped calling.
  - `background()` now publishes the heartbeat too, so the counter stands still only while the radio calls the widget not at all. Off screen, the instruction share it carries is that of the last pass that drew. With two dashboards on two pages, only the one drawn last (or built last, until one draws) publishes from `background()`, so the other cannot keep the counter moving after the one on screen has stopped. The record is behind the debug level as before; nothing else reads it.
- **A save that restarts the flight controller says so when the restart could not be confirmed, and an ESC's current limit is kept only once it is on the card (`tasks/msp/save_pipeline.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/esc_current_limit.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`, `docs/pages/setup/power/preferences.md`) (sites 6 and 7 of #589)**:
  - A save that ends in a restart -- Configuration, Alignment, GPS, Ports, Radio Config, ESC/Motors RPM, Telemetry and Throttle, and Swash or Tail when the type or mode changed -- reported *Settings saved.* as soon as the flight controller answered again after the reboot command, whether or not it had restarted. A reboot command lost on the link leaves a board that never restarted answering just the same, with the settings that take effect at boot (a port's function, for one) not in effect. The pipeline already told the two apart -- the board's RTC flag is set before the reboot and cleared by a reset -- and nothing showed its answer to the pilot. The notice now reads *Settings saved, but the flight controller's restart could not be confirmed. Power it off and on before flying.* and stays up until it is closed, instead of clearing itself after two seconds. A restart that was seen reads *Settings saved.* as before. A board without a clock, or one whose clock the connection could not set, cannot show a restart, so its restarting saves now always end on that notice.
  - Opening the AM32, Scorpion or YGE page put the ESC's current limit into the model's preferences before writing them to the card. Where that write failed, the limit stayed in memory only: every later visit found it unchanged and wrote nothing, and a dashboard reading the card in its own Lua state never had it. The limit is now kept only once the write succeeded, so the next visit tries again.
- **A failed read on GPS, Model, Autolevel and Motor Override says so instead of drawing values the flight controller did not send (`app/pages/settings/common.lua`, `app/pages/setup/gps/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/model/page.lua`, `app/pages/flight_tuning/advanced/autolevel/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/esc_motors/motor_override/page.lua`, `docs/reference/saving.md`, `docs/pages/setup/model.md`) (sites 4 and 5 of #589, sites 1 and 2 of #610)**:
  - The four pages show one sentence after a read that fails -- *Could not read the settings from the flight controller. Use Reload to try again.* -- in place of their fields, and read again on Reload. `Common.failPageRead` marks the read as failed, and `Common.appendReadFailedNotice` draws the sentence, so another page can say the same thing in the same words.
  - GPS called every failed read *built without GPS support* and sent the pilot to reflash. That sentence now stands only for an error reply to `MSP_GPS_CONFIG`, which is how a firmware built without GPS answers it; a timeout, a cleared request or a reply that cannot be parsed is a failed read. Save also waits for the read: pressed while it was still out, it sent the page's starting values and restarted the board.
  - Setup > Model queued its read again from every wakeup after a failure and showed *Reading* for as long as the flight controller did not answer. It now stops and says the read failed, and Save is refused until a read has succeeded, so a Reload that fails cannot send an earlier edit as if it were confirmed.
  - Autolevel drew the session's PID profile -- an earlier visit's, or another board's -- after a failed read, as if it had been read. Motor Override marked the page loaded after a failed status read and offered Motor 1 alone; it now offers no motor until both its reads have succeeded, and gains a Reload, which ends a running override first.
- **The first adjustment after a connect is announced, and a battery profile adjustment is no longer spoken twice (`tasks/events/telemetry_bg/adjustments.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry_bg/tasks.lua`, `docs/audio/events.md`) (sites 4 and 5 of #587)**:
  - The adjustment announcements took the first adjustment they read as the state to join rather than as something the pilot had just done. The flight controller reports 0 while nothing is being adjusted, and the dashboard's and the tool's announcements start again from nothing at every disconnect, so there the first adjustment after every connect was not announced (with the background decoder, the first one after the model was loaded). Once a 0 has been read, the next adjustment is now announced with its name. A first reading that is already an adjustment -- made in the three seconds the flight controller keeps reporting it, before the announcements started -- is still not announced: when the configuration tool opens, when the background decoder is loaded and when the dashboard takes over from it, the part that stood before has usually announced it already. The dashboard now also starts afresh each time it takes the announcements back from the background decoder, so a report the decoder has announced is not announced a second time.
  - The battery profile was left out of the adjustment names so that the change would be announced once, by *Battery Capacity*, but its value was still spoken, so a battery profile adjustment was announced twice: as a bare number, and as the pack's capacity. It is no longer announced by the adjustment announcements at all, and neither are the four profile switches, which the flight controller does not report.
- **The tool opened from the dashboard builds a page stopped at the instruction limit again instead of showing it as broken, and the Logs page keeps each wakeup within the limit there (`ui/home.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/tool_host.lua`, `app/pages/logs/page.lua`, `app/pages/logs/graph.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-views.md`, `docs/pages/logs.md`) (site 4 of #583, #554)**:
  - Inside the dashboard widget a call is stopped at 20 000 Lua instructions, and EdgeTX raises that stop once and lets the rest of the call run, so a page's build that was stopped was caught by the tool's own build guard and shown as *This page could not be drawn* -- although the host's comment promised that such a page completes over the next passes; only a later rebuild, where one came, drew it. A build stopped at the limit now leaves the body empty and is run again on the next pass, up to three passes in a row, before the failure page is shown; a build that is above the limit on its own still does not open there. The host's comment says what actually happens.
  - Hosted, reading a log for its summary spent up to twelve units of about 10 000 instructions per wakeup, so every wakeup was stopped in the middle of its second unit, and the row being read at that moment was counted without all of its values. It now spends one unit per wakeup there, for the summary and for the graph alike; the tool started from the radio's menu keeps its six. A long log therefore takes more passes to read there than before, but none of them is stopped.
  - A stop between the end of the header's columns and the move to the next phase cleared the header line first, so every later unit raised and the loading notice never ended. The header line is now let go only after the phase has moved on, and a unit stopped there is simply done again.
  - The search for logs was bounded by the clock, which does not bound instructions, and a search stopped in the middle of an entry had already moved past it, so that log was missing from the list; stopped between two search paths, it could not go on at all. Hosted, it is now bounded by a number of entries, an entry counts as read only once it has been added, a name read from a folder is kept until it has been taken, and a step stopped half-way is repeated rather than leaving the search unable to go on.
- **The tool opened from the dashboard releases its pages however it ends, and a theme settings page no longer leaves its edit scope behind (`widgets/dashboard/tool_host.lua`, `ui/home.lua`, `app/pages/init.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-views.md`) (sites 1 and 3 of #583)**:
  - When the dashboard drops the tool without its closing sequence -- the widget sent to the background, twelve instruction-limit stops in a row, or an error escaping the tool -- the pages it held are now released first, so each page's `onClose` runs. The servo, mixer and motor override pages switch their override off only there; before, a servo or mixer override stayed on the flight controller, which holds one with no timeout and refuses to arm while it is on (the motor override times out on its own after a second). The writes go out through the dashboard's own MSP tick. A tool that went through its closing sequence has released its pages already and is not released twice.
  - The closing sequence's release of every page now reaches the dashboard theme settings page as well, which the page registry caches by its file rather than by a menu id. Its `onClose` clears the theme edit scope it set; left set on the dashboard's session after the tool closed, the dashboard drew a theme with the standard settings where the model's own were switched on, or the other way round, until a theme settings page was opened and left again. The standalone tool was not affected, because its session ends with it.
### Performance, Memory & Build System
- **A check for a dashboard theme that takes fullscreen (`bin/themes/validate.lua`, `bin/themes/README.md`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`)**:
  - `lua5.3 bin/themes/validate.lua <theme folder> [--size 800x480|480x272]` builds every phase module of a theme with `fullscreen = "theme"` at the fullscreen size, with a recording `ctx`, and fires every press in the tree. It is red where no press opens the quick menu, where nothing leaves fullscreen (no press with `exitFullscreen`, no `ctx.keys.exit = "exitFullscreen"` for RTN, which every radio has, and no `fullscreenExit = "longRtn"` declared in `init.lua`), where a `rectangle` is drawn over a pressable node, where an action or a view is unknown in a press or in `ctx.keys`, and where a build raises.
  - A tree that binds no press, and a declarative theme, are green: the widget draws its own menu control and X over them. A theme without the key is not checked.
  - Offline and self-contained, with its own stubs; it is not part of the CI workflow.
- **The UID read no longer asks for its modules on every tick while they fail to load (`tasks/msp/runtime.lua`) (fixes #429)**:
  - When `tasks/msp/api/uid.lua` or `lib/model_preferences.lua` could not be loaded, the MSP runtime's UID read returned without marking itself done and was entered again on the next tick. The loader does not remember a failed load, so each of those ticks read the file from the card again and printed the failure.
  - The read now waits out the same backoff as a UID read the flight controller did not answer -- 4 s, growing by 2 s an attempt to 30 s -- and stays owed, because a load can fail once and succeed later: in a widget, a module whose execution runs into the CPU limit can load on a later pass. The connect sequence is not held; the read counts as having an outcome from the first failure, as before.
  - Measured offline on the dashboard's background tick with the accounting's flight-controller stub, the module made to raise when run, over one 108 s connect, idle, armed and post-flight run: 355 loads of the module before, 6 after; in the idle phase the loads per tick fall from 1.02 to 0.04 and the mean tick from 2 138 to 2 055 instructions, against 2 052 with the module loading normally. Where the module loads after three failures, the UID is still read on the same link, 18 s later than before (after 4, 6 and 8 s of backoff) instead of on the next tick; until then the session has no UID, which the connect tasks after `uid` already tolerate, because on master too the read settles at the first failure. With the module loading normally nothing changes: every phase's cost and the single `MSP_UID` request are the same on both trees.
- **A telemetry sensor that answers is read without reading the clock (`lib/sensors.lua`) (fixes #398)**:
  - `Sensors.getValue` read the clock on every call, and for a source adopted on its primary name -- the steady case for every sensor the model sends -- that reading was only compared against a one-second miss record that was not there. Such a read now takes no clock reading while no miss is on record for the name. The clock is still read for a name with a miss on record, to stamp a new miss, on every call of a source adopted on a fallback name (for its three-second probe), and before the search gate of a source with no adopted name. A sensor that answers again after a miss has its record cleared, so it goes back to being read without the clock.
  - The answers are the same, with one qualification about timing: the reading a miss is stamped and compared with is now taken when the miss is found rather than when the call began. Where the 10 ms clock ticks inside that call, the waits measured from it -- the one-second window of the name, and the search gate after a fresh miss of an adopted name -- can end one call earlier or later, so an answer can come one call earlier or later. It is never an answer master would not give. The first second after power-on, where master's comparison against a missing record read as a miss, is not reached: no source has an adopted name then, because the search that adopts one does not run in the first two seconds after power-on. No call reads the clock more often than before: a miss hands the reading it took to the search that follows.
  - `measure.lua --check`: 51 rows, 0 failures, no row rises. `pass.state` 8 232 -> 7 911, `pass.state.armed` 9 482 -> 9 161, `theme.default` 9 384 -> 9 063, the five `@` themes -359 each, `pass.tuning.state` 10 181 -> 9 879, `pass.tuning.prime` 14 533 -> 14 325, `unit.events.wakeup` 4 140 -> 4 086, `unit.events.wakeup.armed` 4 842 -> 4 600, `pass.service` 5 316 -> 5 315. A steady read goes from 126 to 104 instructions under the same count hook; a source that is never found costs about 2 instructions more per call than before.
- **The dashboard's telemetry-read pass no longer also runs the announcements and SmartFuel's wake (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry_bg/tasks.lua`, `docs/developer/audio-announcements.md`, `bin/accounting/measure.lua`, `bin/accounting/README.md`) (fixes #439)**:
  - The STATE pass reads telemetry every 0.5 s. The announcements (`Audio.process`, throttled to 0.25-0.6 s) and SmartFuel's wake (1.0 s) run on cadences of their own, are independent of the read and drift onto its tick; on an arm or disarm edge they land there together with the drain, the edge runner and the read, and which of them coincide depends only on the phase of the cadence grid at the edge.
  - A pass that is going to read now carries both to the next logic tick, as a theme reload already is (#424). The dashboard hands the event runtime a flag, the event runtime hands it to `telemetry_bg`, and `telemetry_bg` skips SmartFuel's wake on that pass; the dashboard skips its own announcement pass. SmartFuel's flag has to be set before the read is decided, so it is the read test on the state the previous pass left and can be a tick off at a link transition; the announcement pass runs after the read and uses the decision itself. SmartFuel keeps its 1.0 s interval; an announcement comes at most one logic tick (0.1 s) later. The tool, the service widget and the accounting call the event runtime without the flag and are unchanged.
  - Only where logic ticks come closer together than the read interval: where they are that far apart -- the passes of a scene build in between, or a widget called that rarely -- every tick reads, and a carry would only move the work onto the next read. That also means the announcements are never put off twice in a row; SmartFuel's wake can be at a link transition.
  - The arm/disarm edge runner is deliberately not carried. The flight record is opened by its first task and takes its 0.5 s samples from that moment on; carrying the runner would move every sample of the flight off the read pass in the phases where it shares the read today, at about 1 500 instructions a sample.
  - Measured with `bin/accounting/measure.lua --phases`, added here, under desktop Lua 5.3: the armed scenario's widget alone with 0-9 extra passes before the arm and, separately, before the disarm. The mode is a report only and leaves `--check` unchanged. The worst pass of the first 16 armed passes goes from 10 049-12 740 to 8 581-10 832 (over 12 200 in 2 of 10 phases before, none after); the worst of the first 16 post-flight passes from 11 658-13 977 to 9 764-11 516 (5 of 10 before, none after); the steady armed peak from 9 526-10 068 to 8 525-8 941. Every run's total falls by 1.7-1.8 %, because the announcement pass now runs 150-152 times in a run where it ran 178-181 times: a carried pass waits a tick, and its throttle counts from there.
  - `measure.lua --check`: 51 rows, 0 failures. `pass.state` 10 696 -> 8 226, `pass.state.armed` 10 995 -> 9 540, `pass.tuning.state` 12 514 -> 10 835, `theme.default` 11 848 -> 9 378. Two rises: `pass.startup.worst` 10 696 -> 12 063, where the announcement carried off a read pass at connect lands on the pass that loads `telemetry_bg`'s third module; and the five `@` theme rows +172 each, whose scenarios run logic ticks a read interval apart, so nothing is carried there after the first tick and SmartFuel's wake falls on a different read pass of the same run.
- **The flight record samples on the passes the dashboard reads (`tasks/events/telemetry/flight_record.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/reference/flight-statistics.md`) (#450)**:
  - The record offers its readings to the dashboard's telemetry read in the same pass (#389), but it sampled on a 0.5 s clock of its own that started at the arm, while the dashboard reads on a 0.5 s clock of its own. With both at the same interval, whichever phase of the read the arm happened to fall in held for the whole flight, and in six of ten phases every sample paid for a read of its own on a pass that does not read.
  - The dashboard now decides its read before it drives the event runtimes -- the same test, computed once -- and tells the record its clock for the pass and whether its read falls due. While a reader is counting passes the record samples on the passes on which that read falls due and measures its 0.5 s on the reader's clock; the interval still applies, so a reader that skips its read cannot make the record sample on every pass. Without a counting reader -- the service widget alone -- the record keeps its own clock.
  - The first sample of a flight is taken on the first read after the arm instead of on the arm pass itself, normally within about half a second; until then a box showing a statistic of the flight in progress shows the previous flight's value. The flight clock is not affected.
  - `bin/accounting/measure.lua --phases`: the sample is on the read pass in all ten arm phases; steady armed, the worst pass is 8 545 in every phase (was 8 525-8 941) and the window sums fall 3.8 %; the arm window's worst falls from 11 070 to 10 696 and its sums 4.5 %; the disarm window's worst passes rise by 6 (the two fields).
  - `measure.lua --check`: 51 rows, 0 failures. `pass.state.armed` 9 608 -> 9 482, `pass.tuning.state` 10 835 -> 10 181, `unit.events.wakeup.armed` 4 971 -> 4 842 (the armed scenario's second, direct event-runtime call no longer samples), every other state and theme row +6.
- **Offline accounting: every scenario starts from a clean set of module singletons (`bin/accounting/stubs/edgetx.lua`, `bin/accounting/measure.lua`, `bin/accounting/budgets.lua`, `bin/accounting/README.md`)**:
  - The world is rebuilt between scenarios, but several modules survive a reload by parking themselves in a global -- the MSP runtime, the event runtime, the configuration store, the MSP service, the environment, the locale, the model name store and the object and theme commons -- and the rebuild did not reach them. Every scenario after the first therefore ran on the previous scenario's runtimes: the new session was never told the link was up, the connect chain stopped on the `telemetry` task at 4 of 10, and the custom-telemetry drain never started. `Stubs.install` now clears those globals by prefix, and the last scenario ends at 9 of 10 with the session connected.
  - **No measured source changed, so the fourteen rows that move are the instrument seeing the suite differently.** Seven of them land within 10 % of their targets, and those targets are left untouched so the report keeps saying so. One goes over: `pass.service`, whose block is now measured in a session that finished connecting and so runs the custom-telemetry drain in every pass -- which it never did before.
  - The service block now holds the link backlog at one pass's worth of frames before every pass, as the other blocks that run the drain do. Measured, that moves none of its 600 passes: the drain takes the same number of frames per wake however many are waiting, so the frames `feedLink` piles up were never what the row priced. `pass.service` is therefore set from that figure -- `target = 6000, measured = 5314, proposed = 2200`, so every run reports the row as re-apportioned.
- **Offline accounting: the run is given a card of its own, so the host's `/SCRIPTS` cannot reach it (`bin/accounting/stubs/edgetx.lua`, `bin/accounting/measure.lua`, `bin/accounting/README.md`) (fixes #393)**:
  - The suite addresses its settings by absolute card path (`/SCRIPTS/TOOLS/rfsuite.user/...`, spelled `SCRIPTS:/TOOLS/...` in two modules), and the stubs remapped `loadScript` onto the repository but not `io.open`. Those opens therefore went to the host's root filesystem: a run read whatever settings file the machine had, and wrote its own into the machine's card. A local `--check` and the CI job could disagree over a file outside the repository, and a run that died on a control left that file behind for the next one.
  - Every card path is now remapped onto a directory in the system temp directory, the way `loadScript` is remapped onto the repository, and that directory is emptied when the run starts and again when the last measurement is done -- so nothing on the host can reach the measurement and nothing the measurement writes survives it. `loadScript` routes a path under `/SCRIPTS/TOOLS/` that is not the suite's own to the card as well, rather than reading it as `src/rfsuite.user/...`, a path that exists in neither the repository nor the card.
  - **No measured source changed, and four budget rows move, all upward and all inside their targets**: the card exists, so the settings store can be written and the 30 s look in the service block can parse one. A traced run writes no card path at all, on master as much as here, so what the four rows price is not a store being written and parsed but the Lua wrapper every `io.open` now goes through -- removing that one wrapper puts all four back on master's figures, and the report is then identical to master's line for line. `pass.service` is not one of the four, and the dearest pass of that block is an early one-off rather than a look at the store. A report from a machine with a populated card and a report from one without are byte-identical.
- **A phase change that loads a different theme module builds the new scene once, not twice (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`) (fixes #407)**:
  - `refresh()` copies the phase onto the state at the top of every pass, before the state pass works out that the phase has changed and reloads the theme. The render key computed after that reload was therefore still the previous phase's -- or, with the 2 Hz throttle closed, the key cached for it -- so the new scene was built under the old key and built a second time when the throttle next opened: armed to in flight, in flight to post-flight, and post-flight to armed on a re-arm.
  - `reloadActiveTheme` now writes the phase onto the state itself (`state.flightMode`, `state.themePhase`) and opens the throttle, as `widget.reload` and a preference reload that read the file already do. The key computed next is the new module's, so on the dashboard the scene queued in the reload pass is the only build. The cached key is not cleared by this change: where the reload leaves it standing -- a theme with a voltage range of its own -- the in-flight tuning overlay, which can take the throttle first, falls back to it rather than to no key at all.
  - The source list the reload collects from a theme whose `boxes` is a function now sees the new phase in `state.flightMode` too; before, that function saw the phase of the previous pass. No shipped theme's `boxes` function reads the phase.
  - Measured with `bin/accounting` under desktop Lua 5.3, taking one widget on `system/default` through armed, in flight, post-flight and link lost: one build at the in-flight and one at the post-flight edge instead of two each; the second builds were 4 330 and 5 160 instructions over three job passes that skipped the telemetry read, audio and events. `reloadActiveTheme` costs 8 instructions more; where the throttle would have been closed in the reload pass, that pass now also computes the render key, 83 instructions for the engine's key on `system/default`. `measure.lua --check`: 51 rows, 0 failures; the only row that moves is `pass.startup.worst`, 10 688 -> 10 696.
- **The dashboard's "Please wait" pass on connect no longer searches every telemetry source at once (`lib/sensors.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/reference/sensor-selection.md`) (fixes #402)**:
  - After `Sensors.reset()` -- when the widget starts, and on both edges of the flight controller link -- no source had a miss on record, and a source with no miss was exempt from the one-search-per-pass throttle, so the next telemetry read searched every source's whole list in one pass. With CRSF custom telemetry every sensor exists but carries no value until the suite's own decoder publishes it, so every candidate of every list was paid for, in the pass the connect chain also runs in.
  - At most four sources now start their first search per clock reading -- one pass, unless a pass outlasts a 10 ms tick, the same key the repeated-search throttle uses. A source held back is not marked as missed and is searched at the next read, so the dashboard's values are matched over about two seconds after a reset instead of on the first read. The limit is module-wide: another reader in the same pass shares it.
  - `Sensors.getValue` reads the clock once per call and hands it to every candidate, instead of a `pcall(getTime)` per candidate. No behaviour change.
  - A theme reload that falls due in the pass that ran the telemetry read moves to the next logic tick, and the flight mode moves with it. A reload is carried once.
- **The tool keeps a page's compiled code only while it keeps the page (`src/main.lua`) (#412)**:
  - The tool's `loadScript` wrapper kept every chunk it loaded until the tool closed, page modules included. The page cache in `app/pages/init.lua` already holds the two most recent page modules, so on top of that the wrapper held the bytecode of every page opened in the session. A page's own `page.lua` is no longer kept by the wrapper; the other files under `app/pages/` and everything outside it stay cached. One page file is also loaded by another page for its functions -- *RF Status* loads *Validate Sensors*' -- and is now read once per open of *RF Status*.
  - Measured on the English package of this tree, driven offline under desktop Lua 5.3 through the real entry script, opening each of the 95 pages reachable from the manifest once and backing out of it: the memory the walk leaves retained falls from 3607 KB (256 cached chunks) to 1200 KB (163), and a second walk over the same pages adds 1.1-1.2 KB on both trees. The instruction count of the walk moves by 0.02 %, and a full collection after it takes about half as long as before. These are 64-bit desktop sizes and so upper bounds for a radio.
  - The cost is the trade the cache was there to avoid: a page that has left the page cache is loaded from the card again the next time it is opened -- 106 file loads over the 95 pages of the second walk, where there were none. That includes every opening of a dashboard theme's settings page, which is let go of as soon as it is left. The tool compiles the tree when it starts so that such a load finds current bytecode beside the source; how long the load takes on a radio's SD card was not measured.
  - A page whose file cannot be run -- the card cannot be read, the file is missing, memory runs out while it loads, or its top level raises -- no longer ends the tool with EdgeTX's script-error screen, which a reload after eviction could now reach where a cached chunk could not. The page registry (`app/pages/init.lua`) hands out a stand-in whose `build` raises the loader's message, so the tool shows its existing *This page could not be drawn* screen with that message and the header's back key still works. The stand-in is kept until the page is left, so the header, the page body and a back press ask the card once per visit, and it never enters the page cache: opening the page again reads the card again. The same now holds for a page's first open, which could already end the tool this way.
- **The dashboard stops copying its voltage configuration on every pass for a 6S pack with 3.0/4.2 V cell limits (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`) (fixes #314)**:
  - `updateVoltageThemeConfig` runs in the widget's logic tick and already kept its table when the bounds in hand were custom, had no cell count to check against, or were plausible for the pack. Six cells at the flight controller's 3.0/4.2 V limits normalize to 18.0/25.2 V, which is exactly the pair the helper reads as an unnormalized default, so that craft never reached the early return: every pass copied every key of the configuration into a new table and wrote the same two numbers back.
  - The bounds the normalization would write are now computed first, and the existing table is kept when they are the bounds it already holds. Every other input takes the same path as before -- a custom voltage range still wins, the first normalization of a default still happens, a changed cell count or cell limit still re-derives the range, and bounds held as strings are still converted.
  - Measured with desktop Lua 5.3 on the accounting stubs, after one normalization: a call on that craft falls from 259 instructions to 147 and from 185 184 bytes allocated per 1 000 calls to 1 184, the same allocation as the 3.3/4.2 V control, whose own 83 instructions do not move. `bin/accounting/measure.lua` shows no row moving, because its stubs set no battery configuration and so run on the 3.3/4.2 V fallback.
- **The card log writes a long backlog in batches of 50 lines, and waits the full interval after a write the card refused (`lib/log_sink.lua`) (fixes #408)**:
  - With *Log Session To Card* on, each write formatted every line the sink held in one call, in whichever widget pass ticked the sink -- about 42 instructions a line, so a full list of 400 cost 16 613 instructions of the widget's 20 000 in that pass. While the card refuses the session file the lines are kept, so the list stays full and every interval formatted all 400 again: 14 160 instructions every 3 s for the rest of the session.
  - A tick now formats and writes at most 50 lines. A longer backlog goes out one batch per tick on the ticks that follow, without waiting for the interval -- but only after a write that reached the card; after one that failed, the next attempt waits the full interval as before, so a refusing card is asked once an interval and not on every tick.
  - The written lines are dropped with one `table.move` and a tail clear instead of one `table.remove(list, 1)` per line.
  - The tool's orderly exit (`Sink.shutdown`) and a caught fault (`Sink.fault`) still write everything held in one call: a batch there would drop the tail on exit, or put the fault line before lines that preceded it.
  - Measured with desktop Lua 5.3 on the accounting stubs, counted as `bin/accounting` counts: one tick costs at most 2 114 instructions whatever the backlog (16 613 before at 400); with the session file refused, 10 s of logic ticks cost 12 053 instructions with three failed opens (48 579 before, also three). For the same sequence of lines the session file ends up byte for byte what it was; the last lines of a long backlog reach it one interval later, and at the 5 000-line cap the file now stops within 50 lines of it rather than within 400. While a batch is draining the list has less room left -- 400 minus what is still held, as little as 50 right after the first batch of a full list -- so lines arriving faster than that between two logic ticks are now dropped and counted where they were written before; the highest rate measured was about 31 lines a second during a connect, about 3 per tick.
- **The decoders that yield several sensors publish through the drain's throttle (`lib/rf2tlm_sensors.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry_bg/drain.lua`, `docs/reference/telemetry-sensors.md`) (fixes #293)**:
  - The cell voltages, the GPS fix, the adjustment pair, the control angles, the attitude and the acceleration are decoded by six functions that yield several sensors each, so they publish them themselves. They did so on every frame whatever the values, and returned `nil`, which is exactly what makes the drain skip its publish-on-change throttle: 13 `setTelemetryValue` calls per frame for the cells of a 12S pack alone.
  - They now ask the drain first, handing over where in the frame the group was decoded from. The drain hands that check to the decoder module once, when it loads it, so the single-value decoders are called exactly as before. A group whose bytes equal those of the frame it was last published from is not published, and it is refreshed every `FORCE_REFRESH_INTERVAL` like a single sensor. The drain keeps a reference to that frame rather than a copy: every frame the radio hands out is a table of its own and nothing writes into one. **A group goes out whole or not at all**: the radio forms the `Cels` total only when the last cell arrives, and the GPS fix is three calls on one id. When a group goes out, the calls are the ones it made before, argument for argument.
  - Sensor registration, where the module is called with a list of ids, passes no throttle and makes the same calls as before.
  - The drain's own comment gave the wrong reason for its throttle: it said every `setTelemetryValue` call marks the model dirty. The firmware marks it only when a call creates a sensor; a call for an existing one looks the sensor up among the model's sensor slots and stores the value. The comment now says that, and so does the SmartFuel paragraph of `docs/reference/telemetry-sensors.md`, which gave the same reason for switching the two sensors off. The throttle is kept for what remains true -- an unchanged value does not need re-sending -- and no saving on the radio itself was measured.
  - The group is recorded as published before its calls are made. So a decoder that raises -- on a truncated frame, which the flight controller does not send -- raises again only once the refresh is due rather than on every frame, and in the widget and the tool, where `tasks/events/runtime.lua` catches the error and logs it, the sensors behind it in the same frame are decoded in between. The background decoder catches nothing, and the radio stops a function script on its first error, so there nothing changes: it stops on the first such frame on either version.
  - Counted with the accounting stubs and the drain's own code, on frames carrying all six groups plus `Vbat` and `Hspd` as controls: 200 identical frames over 5 s went from 3110 `setTelemetryValue` calls to 103: each group is now published three times in those 5 s, like `Vbat`. **A group that changes in every frame saves nothing, and the comparison costs Lua instructions**: over 40 frames in which five of the six groups change every time and the adjustment pair holds still, the calls go from 641 to 603 -- the 38 saved are the adjustment pair -- and a wakeup decoding four such frames goes from 3085 to 3688 Lua instructions, while the same wakeup on unchanged frames goes from 3085 to 2799. A frame carrying no group at all costs 4 more per wakeup (3635 to 3639 for four frames of fourteen single-value sensors). Attitude, acceleration and the control angles are the groups to expect in the changing state while the model flies; that was not measured on a radio. Nothing is allocated per frame. The `bin/accounting` rows that include the drain move by +4, `pass.function` by +15, because its frames carry no multi-value payload (#312).
- **The dashboard takes the readings the flight record has already made in the same pass (`tasks/events/telemetry/flight_record.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/reference/flight-statistics.md`)**:
  - The event runtimes are driven at the top of the dashboard's pass, ahead of the widget's own telemetry read, so on a pass where the record samples its statistics it asks for up to fourteen sensors — the governor state among them — and the widget then asks for the same names again a few hundred instructions later. A second read inside one pass cannot answer anything the first did not — a pass runs to completion without yielding, so a telemetry value does not move inside it — so the record now offers what it has just read under `rfsuite.session.telemetryRead`, and the widget takes those readings instead of asking for them again.
  - The readings are handed over **raw**, before the record's rounding, its watts inference and its fuel clamp, so each reader keeps its own derivations and no number either of them publishes changes. A name the sensor answered nothing for is absent from the offer and is read exactly as before, and so are the SmartFuel sensors whenever SmartFuel has handed consumption and fuel over in this Lua state, because neither reader asks them then. An offer counts for one pass only: the reader counts the pass before the runtimes are driven and takes a fill only while it carries that count, so a fill from a wakeup anywhere else in the same Lua state can never be taken for this pass's.
  - Measured on `bin/accounting`: `pass.state.armed` falls from **12119 to 10875** against a target of 12200, from 1 % of margin to 11 %. Per pass over the armed scenario's 240: the 17 passes on which both readers run fall by **1244** each — twelve reads not made, one per name the scenario answers for — the passes on which only the record samples rise by 62, and the rest rise by 27 or 32 or not at all: counting the pass, and the comparison that finds no offer. `pass.state` goes from 11237 to **11269**, and every `theme.*` row and the two tuning rows move by the same +32, `pass.startup.worst` by +34. `unit.events.wakeup` and `unit.events.wakeup.armed` are unchanged at 4502 and 6162.
  - A model that keeps the record **without** a dashboard — the service widget running the runtimes — has no reader, so nothing creates the table, the record offers nothing and pays one comparison on a sampling pass.
- **The offline accounting row for the service widget now contains the pass it is meant to bound (`bin/accounting/measure.lua`, `bin/accounting/budgets.lua`) (fixes #348)**:
  - The service block drove 200 passes and the stub clock advances 0.1 s per pass, so its window ended at 20 s. `widgets/service/runtime.lua` looks at the settings store every 30 s, so `pass.service` had never contained that pass and could not have gone red on it. The block now drives 600 passes -- 60 s, which holds the look twice.
  - Widening the window alone turns `--check` red: the pass at 30 s measures 1784 instructions against the row's target of 1600. No measured source changed, so that is what the pass costs rather than a regression, and the target is raised to 2200 with `proposed = 1600`, so every run reports the row as re-apportioned. What the pass adds is the resolution of the settings path and three `fstat` calls; the store is parsed only where one of them has moved, or where the firmware has no `fstat` at all. The target leaves room for the dearer forms of the same look that the block does not drive -- `fstat` answering, and a save inside the window that makes the look parse the store.
  - The block now counts the settings looks it drove and stops with an error where the window held fewer than two, so moving the stub clock's step, the warm-up or the interval cannot shorten the window again with the check still green. The row reports the pass its worst figure came from (`worst at pass 300 of 600`). Driven to 200 passes the block stops with *held 0 settings look(s)*, to 400 with *held 1*.
- **Two budget holes in the widget shells (`widgets/service/runtime.lua`, `src/widgets/rfsuite/main.lua`) (fixes #290)**:
  - The service widget re-read the whole settings store from the card every 30 seconds whether or not anything had changed, in the pass that also carries the log sink flush, the MSP tick, the events wakeup and the MSP pump. `lib/config_store.lua`'s `load()` holds no memo, so every one of those ticks was a read of the file, a compile of it and a merge of every declared section. The 30 second look is still there and now costs **three `fstat` calls** where nothing has moved: one on the settings file, for its own stamp, and one on each `reload.req` the user roots can hold, for the rotating counter every writer bumps. Those are the two signals the dashboard widget already watches, so a save made in the configuration tool is still picked up within the same 30 seconds. Where `fstat` does not exist at all the store is parsed exactly as before, because a gate that skipped it there would be skipping it blind; an `fstat` that answers *the file is not there* is a state rather than a failure to look, and is recorded as one, so a card on which the pilot has never saved a setting no longer re-parses a file that does not exist.
  - A load that fails is not recorded as a load that succeeded. This `pcall` catches the firmware's instruction-limit error like any other, and adopting the stamp on that pass would mean the setting the pilot had just changed was never picked up again for the life of that Lua state; the next 30 second tick finds the signals still moved and tries again.
  - A pass that did re-read now ends there, so the work below it starts on a fresh instruction budget instead of behind the parse. `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua` defers after its own reload for the same reason and in the other order -- it does the link work first and returns before the theme build, because its expensive phase comes after the reload. Here the link work is the only phase there is, so deferring it is the only way to keep the parse off the pass that carries it, and it costs one 0.1 second turn of the background service on a pass in which the settings actually changed.
  - Measured on `bin/accounting`, driven to 600 passes because the shipped service block drives 200 and the stub clock advances 0.1 s per pass -- a 20 second window against a 30 second interval, so the row could never contain the pass in question. With a 1561-byte settings file on the card and `fstat` answering, the pass carrying the re-read falls from **2852 to 1811 instructions**, and the settled median is unchanged at 1271/1273. The re-read itself is 1208 instructions; what replaces it is about 255. Over the same 60 seconds the store is parsed **once instead of three times**. The shipped 200-pass row was unchanged at 1272 -> 1274 and the check stayed green; that row never contained the pass, and #348 widens it to 600 passes. Those are instruction counts, which is what the firmware bills a widget call against; the card access behind `fstat` and `io.open` is under-counted by that hook on both sides, and the side this removes is the one that also opened and read the whole file.
  - `background()` in the dashboard widget's entry point set `_cpuBackoffUntil` on an instruction-limit fault and was the one entry point that never read it. A background pass that overran was therefore called again on the very next host cycle with the same work in front of it, while the path that *was* suppressed is `refresh()` -- so the widget the pilot is looking at was the one penalised for a fault that happened while it was off screen. It now stands down for the same back-off it arms, and releases what the widget is holding while it does, which is what `refresh()` already did: the in-flight tuning overlay's two global variables are taken back to zero on every path out, and `background()` is where that otherwise happens.
- **The widget states read the bytecode the suite compiles (`lib/require.lua`, `widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `widgets/service/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/`, `tasks/msp/`, `lib/audio.lua`, `lib/system_locale.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/lib.lua`) (fixes #279)**:
  - `lib/require.lua` publishes the load mode the whole suite uses, and the tool applies it to every load by replacing `_G.loadScript` with a wrapper (`src/main.lua`). A widget state has no such wrapper, so a call site that wrote the mode out as the literal `"t"` was live there -- and EdgeTX takes `"t"` to mean *parse the source*, however new the `.luac` beside it is. The bytecode `lib/precompile.lua` exists to produce was therefore written and never read by the dashboard widget, the service widget or the background decoder's special-function script.
  - The 42 call sites those states reach now name the published mode, the same way the widget entry points already did for their own loads. That covers the dashboard's theme loads -- a theme's `init.lua` and its `preflight.lua`/`inflight.lua`/`postflight.lua`, re-read on every theme and flight-mode change -- the three modules `Runtime.new` loads on a cold start, and every event task module, MSP transport and shared library those states pull in.
  - **A theme under `SCRIPTS/TOOLS/rfsuite.user/dashboard/` is deliberately left on source loading.** That is a file a pilot edits on the card, `lib/precompile.lua` walks the two shipped roots only, and nothing sweeps a `.luac` out of the user tree -- so bytecode left beside an edited theme would be preferred whenever the two timestamps tie at the two-second resolution a FAT card stores, and the pilot's edit would not appear. The same reasoning already keeps `lib/sensors.lua` reading a user simulated-sensor file from source.
  - The wrapper itself is deliberately **not** installed in a widget state: that Lua state is shared with every other widget on the radio, so replacing `_G.loadScript` there would change the load mode of loads that are not the suite's, and the tool's wrapper additionally holds every chunk it has ever loaded for the life of the state.
  - Nothing new is written to the card. Under `"t"` a source is compiled on its first load and after it changes, which is the same condition `"bt"` uses; the change only reads back what is already there. The call sites that ask `_G.rfsuite.require` first are untouched, because that memoizer has always used the published mode.
- **The event tasks stop reloading the logger and the MSP runtime on every connect (`tasks/events/common/`, `tasks/events/onconnect/tasks/`, `tasks/events/ondisarm/tasks/flight_log.lua`) (fixes #411)**:
  - The runner drops a task module when its task completes, so every connect loads the connect tasks again -- and each of them bound `lib/log.lua` and `tasks/msp/runtime.lua` through a private bare `loadScript`. In a widget state that read and compiled two files the Lua state already holds; in the tool, whose `loadScript` wrapper keeps every chunk it has loaded (`src/main.lua`), it re-ran their top level. The fourteen task modules that did so now take those two through `lib/require.lua`, which hands back the loaded instance, and fall back to the bare load where it is absent, as `tasks/events/runtime.lua` already does. Everything else those loaders load is loaded exactly as before -- among it the shared task modules under `tasks/events/common/`, which keep the state of one run (`done`, `requestSent`) and have to start fresh each time.
  - Counted offline in the dashboard widget's background tick over a connect, on the English package: **70 `loadScript` calls become 50**, the 11 of `tasks/msp/runtime.lua` become none and the 10 of `lib/log.lua` become one. A second connect in the same session goes from 56 to 36 with neither left, and the first disarm from 18 to 14 (21 to 16 with the flight log on). Every other file is loaded exactly as often as before, and the flight controller is sent the same requests at the same times.
  - The one logger load left per connect is the runner's own, once per runner object; `tasks/events/common/runner.lua` is not changed here.
  - `bin/accounting` reports the same figure on every row before and after. It counts Lua instructions, and what this removes is card access and a bytecode load, which run in C; nothing was timed on a radio.
- **SmartFuel reads the pack voltage once per wake and takes its modules from the Lua state's shared instances (`tasks/events/telemetry_bg/smart.lua`) (fixes #409)**:
  - `Smart.wakeup` read the `voltage` sensor twice, the second time a few lines below the first with only the cell-count estimate, the reserve and the battery signature computed in between. The wake now uses the first reading. On the accounting's own stubs that is 118 Lua instructions less on every wake, and SmartFuel wakes once a second inside the dashboard widget's background pass: 1 327-1 414 instructions per wake before, 1 209-1 296 after.
  - `smart.lua` loaded its five modules through a private bare `loadScript`, beside the instances the rest of the Lua state holds through `lib/require.lua`. It now asks `_G.rfsuite.require` first, as the flight record's loader does, and keeps the bare load as the fallback. Only a table is taken from the memoizer, which stores `true` for a module that returned nothing (#420). The copies it no longer makes held 31.4 KB (`lib/sensors.lua`), 12.1 KB (`lib/log.lua`) and 2.6 KB (`lib/api_version.lua`) on desktop Lua, 64-bit; the whole heap at the same point is 45.6 KB smaller. `tasks/msp/runtime.lua` already returned the shared instance through its own guard, so for it only the second compile goes away.
  - With one `lib/sensors.lua`, SmartFuel's sources share the dashboard's path cache and the `Sensors.reset()` the link edges call, and a repeated search for a source SmartFuel reads counts against the same one-search-per-pass limit as the dashboard's.
  - In `bin/accounting`: `unit.events.wakeup` 4287 -> 4169 (the read); `unit.events.wakeup.armed` 6170 -> 5779, whose worst pass is SmartFuel's first module load, where the second copy of `lib/sensors.lua` no longer runs its top level; `pass.state` 10772 -> 10594, whose worst pass is a different pass on the two trees. `unit.telemetry.drain` and `unit.telemetry.handoff` each rise by 43: the call they count is one in which SmartFuel is still loading its modules one per wakeup, and in that world `lib/require.lua` meets an empty cache, where its lookup costs more than the bare load did. That is once per module per Lua state.
  - `getSmartConfig` still builds its table on every wake. Keeping it until an input changes would need a check on table identity, and the SmartFuel settings page edits `session.smartfuel_config` in place (`app/pages/setup/power/smartfuel/page.lua`) in the tool, which runs this module as well.
  - Measured offline on desktop Lua 5.3 against the accounting's stubs; nothing was counted on a radio.
- **A box's thresholds are compiled where the box is rendered (`widgets/dashboard/objects/`, `bin/accounting/budgets.lua`, `docs/dashboard/user-themes.md`)**:
  - A theme may give a threshold's limit or colour as a function, so that a warning level can come out of the theme's own configuration. Such a list could not be cached, because nothing knew when the function's answer had moved, so it was rebuilt and every colour in it normalised again on **every change of the value being watched** -- in the reactive sweep, on whatever instruction budget `refresh()` left over. It is now compiled once, where the box is rendered, and the sweep only walks the list it already holds. On the shipped BEC arc whose warning level is a theme setting the colour reference falls from **301 instructions and 528 bytes per change to 141 and no allocation at all**; the same shape on the BEC reading of another shipped theme falls from 271 and 256 bytes to 149 and none. What such a function may read is now stated in the theme documentation: only something whose change rebuilds the screen, which is what the theme configuration already does.
  - The **first** sweep after a scene is swapped in gets the same treatment, and that is the pass with the least budget left of any. A threshold given as a translated string, and a colour given as a name such as `"orange"`, used to be resolved on the first call of each getter, which is always that sweep. Resolved at render instead, the first sweep of one shipped preflight screen falls from **4338 instructions to 2152**, and of its inflight screen from 1328 to 947.
  - The `default` theme's preflight screen resolves its own font once per build rather than handing the firmware a reference per box: **17 reactive references become 13**, a sweep in which nothing moved falls from 721 instructions to 451, and one in which everything moved from 1118 to 825.
  - Six budget rows are re-apportioned, each carrying its previous target so that every run says so: work moved out of the sweep, which runs on every foreground pass, and into the render, which runs once per scene. No sweep target was lowered. Every `theme.*` row -- the ones that decide whether a widget survives its pass -- falls or holds; the largest, on the theme that had the most to gain, falls from 11905 to 9910.
- **A telemetry source the radio has no sensor for stops searching every two seconds (`lib/sensors.lua`)**:
  - `Sensors.getValue` remembered a source that had answered nowhere for a flat 2 s and then ran its whole name search again -- a `getFieldInfo` for every candidate name in `Sensors.search_paths` -- for as long as the model was connected. A radio without an altimeter, without a BEC voltage sensor, without ESC or MCU temperature, or before the suite has published its own `SmFt` and `SmCp`, paid that for every one of those sources for the whole flight. The wait now doubles with each further miss, from the same 2 s up to 30 s, so the first retry is exactly where it was and the steady state is fifteen times rarer: measured on the offline accounting's own stubs, the interval between two searches of an absent source goes from 2.0 s to 30.0 s.
  - **And no more than one repeated search happens in a pass.** `readTelemetry` asks for every source in one pass, and each absent one recorded its miss with the same timestamp, so their searches expired on the same boundary and landed together in the same pass -- the pass that is held against the firmware's per-call instruction limit. The simulator half of this file has always throttled its own searches to one per pass for that reason; the telemetry half does it now as well. A source that has not missed yet is never held back, so the pass that first asks still resolves everything the radio actually carries.
  - Measured with the offline accounting's stubs on a sensor set missing nine of the sources the dashboard asks for: the worst recurring background pass falls from 11682 to 9898 instructions, against a `pass.state` target of 12200 and the firmware's hard limit of 20000. One search of a three-name source is 345 instructions against the 62 the same call costs while the miss is remembered. On the accounting's shipped sensor set, where only one source is absent, `pass.state` and `pass.state.armed` are unchanged and the theme rows move by under 70.
  - A sensor that appears later is still found, and the delay is bounded by those 30 s. The two events that make one appear both clear the back-off first: `Sensors.reset()` runs on each edge of the flight controller link, and the suite's own `SmFt` and `SmCp` are published within the first seconds after a connect, where the wait is still 2 to 8 s.
- **Configuration is stored as Lua data, and the widget never parses it (`lib/config_store.lua`, `lib/preferences.lua`, `lib/model_preferences.lua`, `lib/model_name_store.lua`, `lib/system_locale.lua`, `docs/reference/configuration-files.md`)**:
  - `preferences.ini` and `<mcu id>.ini` become `preferences.lua` and `<mcu id>.lua`: a chunk that returns a table, read whole and compiled with `load(src, name, "t", {})` into an empty environment. The compile happens in C, which the count hook the firmware bills a call with does not see, so a load is a table constructor at one to two instructions per key instead of the hundred a line-oriented parse pays. **On the 88-key settings file of a real radio a load falls from 10155 VM instructions to 1282** -- from half of everything one widget call is allowed to a sixteenth of it -- and it no longer grows meaningfully with each setting a release adds.
  - **The card is brought across once, by a Lua state that is yielded rather than cut off**: the configuration tool at its entry point, or the background decoder at its first pass. The former file is parsed by the one parser that is left, the new file is written, and the `.ini` is kept beside it as `.ini.bak` for one release. Nothing is asked of the pilot. A stray `preferences.luac` from an earlier loader is removed at the same time, since the firmware would prefer it whenever the two timestamps tie at the two-second resolution of a FAT timestamp.
  - **A widget never migrates, and never writes a store while reading one.** A widget call is cut off at a fixed instruction count and the parse of a real settings file is larger than what is left of one, so a pass cut off inside it writes nothing and starts the same parse again next pass. Until the tool or the background decoder has run, a widget reads the former file into memory on every load and writes nothing -- which is what the suite did before, at the same cost. The connect tasks run in the widgets as well, so the per-board store follows the same rule and is settled at the next tool session instead. The dashboard's preference watcher stamps whichever of the two files is on the card, so the migration itself is the change that makes the widget read the new format.
  - **What a store may hold is declared once, as a schema, and the saver serialises the schema** rather than the table it is handed, so a key the suite no longer declares is not written back and the file stops growing for ever. Sections whose key names are built at runtime -- a theme's configuration, the announcement pages' per-state enables, the radio half of the in-flight tuning overlay, the setup assistant's skip flags -- are declared open and keep everything they are handed; `[dashboard] theme_config_target` and `connection_guard`, which nothing has ever read, are named as retired so that an open section can lose them too.
  - **Writes are atomic**: the new content goes to a temporary name and then takes the real one, so a reader can never catch a half-written store and a card that fills up loses the temporary rather than the settings. A save interrupted between the two steps is finished by the next load. A trailing generation counter moves the file's byte count on every save, so two saves inside one FAT timestamp slot can still be told apart. `reload.req` is unchanged.
  - **A file that cannot be read is reported, not swallowed**: a chunk that does not compile, does not run or does not return a table gives the declared defaults and a logged error carrying the compiler's line number.
  - **And the first load of a Lua state can report at all now.** The log reads its level from the settings, and while they are absent it answers `off` and refuses the console at every level -- which on a first load is always, since that load is what fetches them. The store hands its messages back instead of writing them, the settings module publishes the table it has just read and then writes them out, so what happened to a pilot's settings file is said at the level that pilot chose. A file that cannot be read at all still reaches the log ring and the card log only: the file carrying the level is the file that could not be read.
  - **The four separate INI readers in the tree become one**, and it is reached in two places only: by the migration, and by the in-memory read a state that may not migrate answers with. `model_name_restore.ini`, where the suite keeps a model name it has renamed until it can put it back, becomes `model_name_restore.lua` through the same store: one open section whose keys are the radio's model files, brought across on the first read with the same `.ini.bak`. It stays a file of its own rather than a section of the settings, because it is written about twice per flight and every write to the settings makes the dashboard re-read them. `lib/system_locale.lua` reads the store for the language rather than scanning the card itself, and reaches the former file through that same one reader, once per Lua state, so the language does not flip for the one boot before the migration runs.
  - `lib/system_locale.lua` is reached from a theme's `t()`, which resolves the locale on EVERY call and therefore runs in the firmware's reactive sweep, once per frame. It holds on to the store it built, and **the resolved language is now memoised for two seconds**, so that path stops being a file read per call and becomes a table lookup: 31 VM instructions inside the window, against the 867 the former per-call scan spent every single time, with one read of 289 at most every two seconds. Two seconds is also what a language changed in the configuration tool waits before the dashboard says it, which is well inside the reload that settings change triggers anyway.
- **Offline accounting: the clock advances per pass, not per call (`bin/accounting/stubs/edgetx.lua`, `bin/accounting/measure.lua`, `bin/accounting/README.md`)**:
  - The stub's `getTime` advanced a fixed step on every call, which made a second worth however many times the code under test happened to ask the time. Anything the suite does on a cadence -- the dashboard's 0.5 s telemetry read, the audio cooldowns, the smart-sensor refresh, the service widget's UI interval -- therefore ran in almost every measured pass or in almost none, and could not be priced at all. The clock is now advanced once per measured pass through `Stubs.tick()`, ten ticks of 100 ms, about the cadence a widget really runs at.
  - Determinism is unchanged: nothing reads a wall clock and three runs are byte-identical. The pass budgets of the time-bounded loops are ten times what they were, because the same elapsed time is now reached in about ten times the passes; nothing waits longer in seconds.
  - No measured source changed, so the 22 rows that move are the instrument seeing the suite differently rather than the suite doing anything different. The budget targets are left untouched, so the two rows that land in the report's own "margin to widen" band stay visible as warnings rather than being quietly widened.
- **Documentation rule checked in CI (`.github/workflows/pr.yml`, `bin/docs/verify_documentation_rule.py`)**:
  - Added a fourth PR job that fails when a pull request changes `src/` and no file under `docs/`, unless its body states on a line of its own why no documentation was needed. Tooling, CI and documentation-only changes are not asked.
  - The job proves the check can go red before it trusts a green one, like the instruction-budget and i18n jobs beside it.
- **Translation coverage reported in CI (`.github/workflows/pr.yml`, `bin/i18n/check_translations.py`)**:
  - Added a fifth PR job that lists the strings a locale build cannot translate: a literal in a `label`, `title` or `message` field, or in the label argument of a `Controls.append*` helper, that never reaches the translator; and a translation key assembled at runtime, which the precompiler cannot resolve and which therefore ships the English fallback in every locale.
  - The report groups by literal and sorts by frequency, because the work is concentrated: 25 literals account for 46 % of the total.
  - Both counts are held at a baseline rather than required to be zero, so the job is green on the tree it was written for and goes red only when a number grows. Names that stay English in every locale live in `bin/i18n/allowed_untranslated.txt`.
- **Dashboard preference reload reads only the file that changed (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`)**:
  - The watcher compares a stamp built from the global preferences file and the per-model one, and knows which of the two has moved -- but the reload then re-read both. A save made from a settings screen touches the per-model file alone, so the global file was parsed again on every such save even though its bytes had not changed, and that parse is the expensive half by a wide margin.
  - The reload now reads only the half the stamp says has moved. Where the signal does not name a file -- a forced reload, the rotating sequence file, a stamp that changed shape, or a changed half whose timestamp did not move (which is the case the sequence file exists for, and where an unchanged half proves nothing) -- both are read exactly as before.
- **The announcement pack is generated from a word list again (`bin/sound-generator/`)**:
  - Every WAV under `src/rfsuite/audio/` is synthesised, but this repository carried no way to make one: adding an announcement meant adding a binary nobody else could produce or review. The generator from the Ethos suite is now here, adapted to write this tree's layout and the 16-bit PCM the firmware plays, with the word lists in `bin/sound-generator/json/en.json` and `de.json`.
  - `--check` needs no credentials and is the mode for reviewing a change: it reports files declared with nothing on disk, files on disk declared nowhere, and entries with no translation. It reports one of each today -- `stat/alerts/notfull.wav` is asked for by the pack-not-full alert and is in no pack, and `en/stat/alerts/batteryempty.wav` is a file with no entry.
  - `update-missing-translations.py` propagates a new line from `en.json` to every other language, keeping existing translations.
