  - Documented in `bin/sound-generator/README.md` and in `docs/developer/audio-announcements.md`, which also covers the settings schema, the i18n keys and the packager's folders.
- **The SmartFuel discharge curve is a constant instead of a table built in a widget pass (`tasks/events/telemetry_bg/smart.lua`)**:
  - The 121-entry curve the voltage-based fuel estimate reads is a sigmoid over a fixed grid of cell voltages with no input other than its own two constants, so every build of it produced the same table. It was built on the first estimate a model made from voltage, which put **3395 instructions into whichever widget pass that was**, on top of what the pass already carried and against the 20000 a widget call is billed at. It is now written out in the source, with the formula and the grid above it so that it can be regenerated: the first call of `fuelPercentageFromVoltage` costs **76 instructions instead of 3476**, and every later call 76 instead of 81, since there is no longer a memo to test.
  - In the offline accounting that pass was the one that set `pass.state`, so the row falls from **11579 to 11150** against its target of 12200, and `theme.default` falls with it from 13144 to 12715. No other row moves, and no budget target is changed.
  - What it costs instead: the module body is 184 instructions rather than 61, and about 1.1 kB of the Lua state's heap is held from the moment the module loads rather than from the first estimate -- including on a model whose flight controller computes the fuel percentage itself.
  - The entries are the formula's own numbers: the table was checked entry for entry against the loop it replaces, and the estimate returns the same percentage at every cell voltage from 2.50 V to 4.40 V for one to fourteen cells.
- **The Ports page builds its function list once per open (`app/pages/setup/ports/page.lua`) (fixes #413)**:
  - `getPortFunctionsList` built a new 15-entry table on every call, and every lookup of a function's type or exclusion mask went through it. One build of the page therefore made the list N + 3 times per row for N ports, N + 2 for a disabled one: each row asks for the exclusion mask of every other port, then for the list itself, then twice while building its baud choices, then once more for the active baud index. The list does not change while the page is open, so it is built on the first call and dropped in `onClose`; the names still go through `pageText`, so a reopened page resolves them again.
  - Measured offline under desktop Lua 5.3 on the English package, from the press to the settled page, with the API module's own 12-port serial reply: **103 461 -> 85 596 instructions and 1 033 -> 421 KB allocated**, the largest single pass 45 105 -> 27 240. With six ports: 76 333 -> 71 308 and 545 -> 373 KB. The built page is identical on every one of five opens, and so is the page after each function of a row is picked in turn. The list is not held once the page is closed; the heap after a close differs by the 0.2 KB the page's own chunk grew by.
- **The connect runner stops walking a finished queue on every pass (`tasks/events/common/runner.lua`) (fixes #313)**:
  - Once the connect sequence was through, the dashboard still asked the runner three questions on every logic tick -- is it active, how far is it, which step is pending -- and each one walked the whole task queue again; the progress question also built a new table every time. The events wakeup asked `active()` twice more around its own `wakeup()`, which walked the queue a third time to find nothing to run. None of those answers can move until a task is marked complete or failed, the queue is reset or loaded, or the context asking changes.
  - The runner now answers all of them from one walk and keeps that answer until one of those things happens: it is dropped where a task is marked complete or failed, when the queue is reset (a disconnect, `Events.rerunOnconnect()`; the arm and disarm edges reset the two runners they drive, which run the same code) and when the manifest is loaded, and it carries the context it was taken for, so a tool-context question in a widget state walks again rather than getting the widget's answer.
  - **The answers are unchanged.** Driven through the connect sequence, a `rerunOnconnect()`, a link drop long enough for the detector, the reconnect and two arm/disarm cycles, the widget-context answers of all three questions, and what the arm and disarm runners report, are identical tick for tick before and after; the tool-context answer in the same Lua state is still the tool's (the tool-only step pending). The same holds for the two ways a task fails: a task whose module cannot be loaded, and a task that never reports itself complete and is given up after its retries. In both the sequence settles as before, `done` equal to `total`, `failed` 1 and nothing pending; with the drop removed at either of the two sites it stalls at the failed task instead, still reported as running.
  - `getProgress()` now returns the same table until something changes, so it is handed out read-only: a write to it raises instead of becoming the next reader's answer. No caller in the tree writes to it, and none iterates it. The read-only form costs nothing on a read in steady state; building it adds 7 instructions to a walk, which is 15 of the `pass.state.armed` figure above and 8 of `pass.startup.worst`. Its `pending` is now exactly what `getPendingTaskName()` answers; the two could only differ for a manifest entry without a string name, which no shipped manifest has.
  - Measured on `bin/accounting` against the master this entry was rebased on (after #389, #390, #391 and #399): `pass.state` 11340 -> 10741, `unit.events.wakeup` 4506 -> 4287, `pass.state.armed` 11156 -> 10988, `pass.tuning.state` 12421 -> 11808, `pass.service` 1274 -> 1184 (the 200-pass row; #348 widens that row to 600 passes, so its figures are on a different window); `pass.startup.worst` 10618 -> 10639, the view being built while the sequence runs. After the sequence each of the three questions costs what it cost against an empty queue, and the progress question allocates nothing.
  - The configuration tool's home screen already stopped asking once the sequence was through, so the saving is in the widget states.
- **Release Version (`lib/version.lua`)**:
  - Bumped the Rotorflight Lua EdgeTX Suite version to `0.1.7`.
- **The flight controller's UID is read once per connect (`tasks/msp/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/onconnect/tasks/uid.lua`, `docs/troubleshooting/no-connection.md`) (fixes #410)**:
  - The MSP runtime reads `MSP_UID` itself once the API version is in and its queue is idle, and the `uid` connect task queued a second read of its own whenever the runtime's answer was not yet in the session when the task started. On a link with round trips of a couple of hundred milliseconds that was the usual case: two requests, and two loads of the per-model preferences file within a few hundred milliseconds.
  - The task's read could not deliver anything the runtime's did not. The runtime's `publish()` copies its own UID over `session.mcu_id` after every queue pass, so where the task's reply was the first to land, the UID and the preferences it had written were gone again in the same pass, and the runtime then read the UID a second time anyway.
  - The runtime now owns the read and records when it has an outcome on the current link -- answered, given up after its retries, or not possible at all, because a module it needs is missing, the API version read returned nothing it could parse, or the flight controller's API version is refused. "Given up" means at least once: the runtime still retries after its backoff, and a later answer still reaches the session. The task sends nothing; it waits for that outcome and then marks the preferences resolved exactly as before, with or without a UID. It stays in the connect sequence because it is what puts the UID ahead of the tasks that read `session.mcu_id`.
  - Because the connect sequence now waits on the runtime's read, that read no longer waits for an idle queue: it is queued behind whatever is already there, as the task's own read was. Measured offline with a page client that keeps the queue busy for 100 s from the moment the API version is in: with the idle wait the sequence was held for those 100 s; without it the UID and the preferences are in 1.1 s after link-up and the sequence finishes 6.45 s after link-up, as on master -- where the UID read went out too, but the runtime erased its result, so `session.mcu_id` stayed empty to the end of the run.
  - Measured offline on the dashboard's background tick with the accounting's flight-controller stub and a link of 0.20-0.25 s per round trip: one `MSP_UID` request and one preference load per connect instead of two, and the background tick in which the second reply used to land falls from 5012 to 2236 instructions. Where the flight controller never answers the UID read, the connect sequence finishes 9.05 s after link-up instead of 28.25 s, because the task no longer sits out a read of its own with a 5 s timeout. With a link that answers at once nothing changes: one request and one load, before and after.
  - The runtime's warning for a failed UID read named the command as 2; `MSP_UID` is 160, and the troubleshooting page said the same.
- **A move of the dashboard's voltage bounds builds the scene once, not twice (`widgets/dashboard/runtime.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`) (fixes #436)**:
  - When the widget moves the bounds its gauges are scaled to -- the cell count changes, say -- `applyThemeConfig` dropped the render key and the cached key, but left the key's 2 Hz throttle as it was. On a pass where the throttle was closed, `refresh()` then handed on the cleared cached key, `nil`: a scene was queued under no key and built again as soon as the throttle computed the real one, up to half a second later. Both builds drew the same theme with the same bounds.
  - `applyThemeConfig` now opens the throttle as well, so the key computed in the same pass is the one for the new bounds and the scene is queued once, under it. The four fields it now writes are four of the five `bin/accounting/measure.lua`'s own `invalidate` writes; the fifth is the tuning surface's key, which a move of the bounds leaves alone.
  - Whether a move lands on a closed throttle depends on the phase between the telemetry read, which is where the cell count arrives, and the throttle -- two clocks of 0.5 s each. A theme reload opens the throttle on a pass that normally does not read, so after one the two are apart. Measured under desktop Lua 5.3 in the accounting's world, with `Cel#` moved from 6 to 12 at 0 to 5 passes after a throttle opening: with the two clocks in phase, master and this change build once (6 of 6 each); after a reload of the per-model preferences, master builds twice on 6 of 6, the first time under `nil`, and this change once on 6 of 6, under the new key.
  - The in-flight tuning surface shares the throttle. A move of the bounds while it is up rebuilds it once on master and here alike, and a held step control ends at that rebuild on both.
  - Closing the in-flight tuning surface within half a second of a move can still build the dashboard twice: the surface spends the throttle opening on its own key. That path is not changed here.
  - `measure.lua --check`: 51 rows, 0 failures, and the report is byte-identical to master's.
- **The telemetry drain loads its decoder table on a wakeup of its own (`tasks/events/telemetry_bg/drain.lua`, `bin/accounting/measure.lua`) (fixes #442)**:
  - `drain.lua` loaded `lib/rf2tlm_sensors.lua` inside its wakeup and went on to pop and decode in the same call, so the first drain pass of every Lua state carried the table's top-level chunk on top of its first decode. The wakeup that loads the table now returns without popping, as `telemetry_bg/tasks.lua` already does for each module it loads: the frames stay queued, the first decode comes one wakeup later -- a logic tick (0.1 s) in the widget, one run in the background function script -- and no liveness is published for a pass that took nothing off the wire. A table that fails to load is asked for again on the next wakeup, as before.
  - Measured with `bin/accounting/measure.lua` under desktop Lua 5.3, per-pass call trees of the reference scenario's settle: the first drain pass cost 5 430 instructions, of which 2 749 were the table load, against 2 390 for a steady drain pass. Now the load pass's drain costs 2 759 and the first decode 2 673 -- a steady pass plus the CRSF multiplexer's own load (108) and the first publication of every sensor and of the decoder's two counters (176), which every first decode pays.
  - What the worse of the two passes costs depends on where they fall against the 0.5 s telemetry read. With the start of the telemetry runner delayed by 0-9 logic ticks (the same delay on both trees): in 6 of 10 phases the pass that carried both falls by 2 671, and the worst of them from 11 101 to 8 430; in the other 4 the first decode now lands on a read pass, which rises by 179-470. `pass.startup.worst` is unchanged in the reference run and within 7 in every phase.
  - `measure.lua` warmed the background function script for a fixed two runs before counting `pass.function`; the second run now only loads the table, which left the CRSF multiplexer's load and the first publication for the counted run. It now warms until a run takes a frame off the queue, as `warmTelemetryBg` does for the widget's rows, and the row stays 5 867. `--check`: 51 rows, 0 failures. `unit.telemetry.drain` 2 739 -> 2 730 and `unit.telemetry.handoff` 322 -> 233: their warm-up takes one wakeup more, which moves `smart.lua`'s own one-module-per-wakeup chain a step on in the counted call (#409).
- **Offline accounting: a free-form theme settles, and a theme build that raises says so (`bin/accounting/measure.lua`, `bin/accounting/stubs/edgetx.lua`, `bin/accounting/README.md`) (#461)**:
  - A free-form theme -- one whose module has a `build` function -- builds its whole tree in one prepare pass and never swaps, but the run counted its settle tail from the first swap, so it ended in "the dashboard never settled in 4000 passes" even where the build had succeeded. For a theme with a `build` function the tail now starts at that prepare pass; every other theme settles on its swap as before.
  - A build that raises produced the same sentence, because the widget's job step catches the raise and builds again on the next pass. The run now reads the widget's log ring after each settle pass, outside the count, and a run whose job step raised fails with how often it raised and the last error.
  - The EdgeTX stub answers `lcd.sizeText`, which a theme that picks the largest font a label fits in calls while it builds. Without it such a build raised on every pass. It answers from the same per-font table `bin/themes/validate.lua` uses, carried in each file for now.
  - **No shipped theme builds free-form or calls `lcd.sizeText`, so no row moves**: the report, `--self-test` and `--check` are byte-identical to the ones before this change.
- **The initial fuel announcement is no longer tried again on every audio pass when the sound pack has no file for it (`lib/audio.lua`, `docs/audio/events.md`) (fixes #441)**:
  - The announcement is meant once per connection, but it counted as made only after a successful play. A pack without `evt/battery.wav` (electric) or `stat/alerts/fuel.wav` (nitro) never plays it, so the whole block ran again on every audio pass for the rest of the connection and logged two warnings each time.
  - Warnings are kept at every debug level, `off` included: they go into the log history of the script running the announcement, which is what *Session Logs* shows for the tool, and into the card log while one is recording. Run on the accounting stubs, all 60 entries of the dashboard widget's history were these two lines.
  - The file is now resolved first, as the *Main power lost* announcement does. With none, once the choice of file can no longer change (model type set explicitly, battery configuration read, or neither file in the pack), one warning says so and the announcement counts as made for that connection; `resolveEventPath` caches its misses, so there would be nothing to play later either. Until then a model whose type is not yet known keeps trying, as before, because it may still need the file the pack does carry. A file that exists and fails to play is still retried.
  - `bin/accounting/measure.lua`, whose stub world has no sound pack, reports `pass.state.armed` 9162 -> 8578, `pass.startup.worst` 12222 -> 12034, `pass.tuning.prime` 14326 -> 14213, and the worst pass of each `@` theme 575 lower (`theme.@aerc` 13034 -> 12459); no other row moves and `--check` passes. With the file present nothing changes.
- **The MSP runtime's second queue turn no longer publishes when it finds the queue idle (`tasks/msp/runtime.lua`) (#445)**:
  - Every host ticks the MSP runtime, wakes the event runtime and then gives the queue a second turn (`pump()`), and the dashboard also gives it a turn on each scene-build pass; `tick()` and `pump()` both ended by publishing the runtime's state into `rfsuite.session` and `rfsuite.diagnostics` -- about two dozen field copies each. Once the connect chain is done the queue is empty on nearly every pass, `processQueue` has nothing to do, and the publish copied what the last one had already copied.
  - `pump()` now ends the turn without a publish when it finds the queue idle, and returns `true` as before: it is not a refusal. While the link is up, everything in the runtime that changes a published value publishes itself, writes the session itself, or runs before `tick()`'s own publish, so there is nothing new for it to copy; with the link down `pump()` refuses before this, as it did. A pump that finds work on the queue processes it and publishes as before.
  - Offline, the same run on master and on this change, reading every published field after every pass and after every pump: identical over 2 560 passes -- the connect chain, idle with and without telemetry frames, a rerun of the connect chain, a link loss and reconnect, an arm and a disarm. Every pass whose pump found the queue idle costs 179 instructions less; a pass whose pump found work costs 13 or 22 more, the idle test. The two passes on which the flight controller's id arrives count 188 less and 4 more.
  - `measure.lua --check`: 52 rows, 0 failures. `pass.state` 7 912 -> 7 733, and `pass.job.prepare`, `pass.job.build`, `pass.swap`, `pass.tuning.state`, `pass.service`, the eight theme rows and `unit.msp.pump` -179 each; `unit.msp.pump` 227 -> 48, because the queue in its world is idle, so the row now prices the idle turn. Three rows rise by the idle test: `pass.splash` and `pass.tuning.prime` +22, `pass.startup.worst` +13.
- **Offline accounting: the host's `/SOUNDS` cannot reach the run either (`bin/accounting/stubs/edgetx.lua`, `bin/accounting/budgets.lua`, `bin/accounting/README.md`)**:
  - `lib/audio.lua` finds out whether an announcement file is there by opening it under `/SOUNDS/` before it plays it, and the stubs remapped `/SCRIPTS` onto the run's own card but left `/SOUNDS` alone. That open therefore went to the host's root filesystem: a machine with a sound pack at `/SOUNDS` measured the branch that finds the file and every other machine the branch that does not, so two hosts measuring the same tree disagreed, and on a host with a pack a change to what happens when a file is missing could not be seen at all.
  - Every `/SOUNDS/` open is now answered the way the host answers a file that is not there, so every host measures a radio without a sound pack -- the case a host with no `/SOUNDS`, the CI runner among them, already measured. With a pack installed at `/SOUNDS` and without this change, `pass.startup.worst`, `pass.state.armed`, `pass.tuning.prime` and the five `@`-theme rows read 113 to 584 lower than CI's figures; with it, the report is byte-identical with and without the pack.
  - **No measured source changed, and four rows move by the cost of the test itself**: it is one more comparison on every open outside the card, counted under the hook like the rest of the wrapper. `pass.startup.worst` +41, `pass.tuning.prime`, `unit.telemetry.drain` and `unit.telemetry.handoff` +13 each, all inside their targets -- the same four rows the card's wrapper moved. Their `measured` figures in `budgets.lua` are recorded from this run.
- **The configuration tool can leave compiling to first use (`ui/home.lua`, `lib/preferences.lua`, `docs/reference/configuration-files.md`)**:
  - `precompile = false` in the hand-written `developer` section of `preferences.lua` skips the compile pass the tool runs behind its start screen. The start screen then waits for the connection only, and each file is compiled by the loader the first time a page needs it.
  - Meant for automated test runs on a card that starts without compiled files every time, where the pass would otherwise run in full on every start. An absent key keeps the pass, and nothing in the suite writes the key.
- **A check of what a theme's settings page reports when it saves (`bin/themes/verify_save_scope.lua`, `bin/themes/README.md`, `docs/developer/dashboard-themes.md`)**:
  - `lua5.3 bin/themes/verify_save_scope.lua` loads the settings module of every shipped theme with a settings page (`default`, `rfstatus`, `@rt-rc`, `@rt-rc-n`, `@srb-rc`, `@aerc`, `@aerc-n`, `urban`) with the real `app/pages/settings/dashboard/lib.lua`, sets the edit scope as the settings page does, and saves through `onSave` in both scopes, in English and German, with the model's write succeeding, its module missing, or the write refused with each answer the real stores give (`io`, `write`, `delete`, `rename`, `io.open`'s error text with its path, `unavailable`, `missing_mcu_id`), and the radio's write succeeding or refused with each of its answers (the same four tokens and error text, `unavailable`, the missing-module sentence of `ui/preferences.lua`, a Lua error), with and without a flight controller: 640 cases, each with its expected title, message, writes and the store the values stand in. It is red on any other answer, on a message that names a module file, shows an untranslated key, or contains what a store answered, a path separator or `file error`, and on a key a locale's bundle lacks.
  - `--self-test` runs the same cases against four copies of every module broken in memory -- the model scope answering *Saved* after its file was refused, a model store that will not load answering a file name, and the model's and the radio's answer each reaching the screen as it came -- and fails unless each turns every theme red where it breaks, the last two through the check on what a store answered, or if a copy could not be broken.
  - Offline and self-contained, with its own stubs; it is not part of the CI workflow.
- **Urban spends less on every frame (`widgets/dashboard/themes/urban/common.lua`, `layout.lua`, `linkview.lua`)**:
  - The firmware calls every reactive field of the scene on each refresh, on what is left of the widget's instruction budget. Urban's fields now keep what they worked out: a number printed at a fixed precision remembers the raw reading as well as the rounded one, the link bars work out their percentage, length and colour once per change of the reading instead of twice per frame, the battery gauge's rows read the fuel value without two nested calls, and the statistics view joins its record-key names once at build time instead of on every call.
  - One call of every reactive field with no value moved, counted under the same hook `bin/accounting` uses: flight view 1124 -> 817 instructions, statistics view 1560 -> 1147, at all three screen sizes and in the widget zone alike. With every value moving: 1536 -> 1307 and 2102 -> 1785. A rebuild costs 79 to 134 more, once per render-key change.
  - The link view, which a switch can hold open in flight, gets the same treatment for its bars and readings: 547 -> 243 with no value moved, 670 -> 496 with every value moving; its build costs 67 more. The quick menu, the battery picker and the tools view have no reactive fields and are unchanged.
  - Nothing on screen changes: the node list and every reactive value were compared between master and this change, in English and German, at 800x480, 480x320 and 480x272, in the widget zone and at full screen, through seventeen states (values held, moved, moved below the printed precision, missing, no fuel reading yet, empty and low fuel, main power lost, disarmed, arming blocked, no flight controller, disconnected, no flight record) -- identical.
  - `measure.lua --check` passes; only `theme.urban` moves, 15508 -> 15445 (worst pass 13875 -> 14022, first sweep 1633 -> 1423).
- **The tool builds three tables once instead of on every pass while it idles (`ui/home.lua`, `tasks/events/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/telemetry_bg/drain.lua`)**:
  - With nothing to do, the tool still built new tables on every pass, on every page: the list of ESC protocols the ESC tools menu entries are matched against (`updateRuntimeMenuConditions`, on every pass whether or not a flight controller is connected), the argument of the connect runner's wakeup (every pass while the link is up), and the drain's list of frames to decode (every drain pass, whether or not a frame was waiting). None of them outlived its pass, so each was garbage for the collector to find. With no flight controller connected, desktop Lua shows the first one as the only allocation of the tool's idle loop; on the EdgeTX simulator it is about half of what the tool allocates there.
  - The protocol list is built once, at load. The runner's argument and the drain's frame list are one table each, reused: the runner and its tasks read `context` during the call and keep nothing, and the frame list is emptied once its frames are decoded, so no frame is held from one pass to the next, and again before it is filled, so a pass that raised part-way through decoding a malformed frame does not hand its frames to the next one. The event runtime and the drain are the same code under the dashboard widget, and the drain under the background function script, so those make the same saving.
  - This trades garbage for a little memory that stays allocated: each of the three is now one table kept for as long as its file is loaded, in place of a new one on every pass. The protocol list never changes; the other two are refilled on every pass, and the frame list keeps the room its largest pass needed after it is emptied, because Lua does not shrink a table whose entries are set to nil. The collector has less to find on every pass; three small tables stay allocated instead.
  - Counted with `collectgarbage("count")` under desktop Lua 5.3, collector stopped, over 400 passes of the tool idle on its home menu: 823.9 -> 486.7 bytes per pass with a flight controller connected and telemetry frames arriving, 184.7 -> 0.7 with none connected. Most of what remains with a link is the frame tables `crossfireTelemetryPop()` returns, which are the firmware's. Desktop Lua's tables are larger than those of the radio's 32-bit build, so the saving on the radio is smaller in bytes and the same in kind. On the EdgeTX 2.12.2 simulator (TX15), with the tool idle on its home menu, the rate at which the tool's own memory line climbed between collections went from 9.96 to 5.27 kB/s with a flight controller connected and from 4.43 to 2.02 kB/s with none; what remains without a link is outside these three sites.
  - Fed the same frame sequence -- bursts above both per-pass caps, and malformed frames that make the decode raise -- the drain publishes the same values in the same order before and after this change.
  - `bin/accounting/measure.lua --check`: 52 rows, 0 failures. Emptying the frame list costs a few instructions where the table cost an allocation: `unit.telemetry.drain` 2 743 -> 2 761, `unit.events.wakeup` 4 086 -> 4 104 and every row that runs one events wakeup +18 (`pass.state` 7 735 -> 7 753), and `pass.function` 5 867 -> 5 907, which decodes all 15 queued frames.
- **The Logs page no longer stands still while it searches the card, and builds its list 25 logs at a time (`app/pages/logs/page.lua`, `i18n/en.lua`, `i18n/de.lua`, `docs/pages/logs.md`)**:
  - The search of the three log folders ran to its end in one call of the page's wakeup. A colour radio runs a tool's `run()` as one plain call that nothing interrupts, so with a few hundred logs on the card the screen was not repainted and no key or touch was read until the search was over, and the bar in front of it stood at a constant. The search is now a small state machine that goes on in small units for up to 40 ms per wakeup, keeping EdgeTX's `dir()` iterator between calls, and always does at least one unit. It walks the folders in the order it always did, so every file is reached by the same folder first and listed exactly as before. The notice counts the folder entries read and the logs found, from the first wakeup on; it has no bar, because how many entries a folder holds is not known before they are read.
  - The list built four LVGL objects and a measured, wrapped label for every log on the card, with no window and no cap, so a build grew with the card until LVGL could not hold it. It now builds 25 logs at a time, newest first as before, each row as before with its *View* button: the rotary encoder steps from one row to the next and the page scrolls along, and a touch drags it. *Previous* above the 25 and *Next* below them build the 25 before or after, with which logs are shown out of how many beside them. Back from a log's summary finds the list on the same 25.
  - The list was thrown away when the page closed, so every visit searched the card again. It is now kept, on the 25 it was left on, until *Reload*, or until the tool drops the page from its cache of recently opened pages. A search still in progress when the page closes is dropped and starts again on the next visit. Nothing on the list path reads the content of a log named the way EdgeTX names its logs, as before.
  - What keeping it costs: one table of six short strings per log. On the EdgeTX simulator, a 64-bit build, the list holds 273-286 KB for 587 logs and 2.3 MB for 5011, which counts against the 6 MB EdgeTX allows all Lua scripts and widgets together on a colour radio. The page held the same list before for as long as it was open, with four LVGL objects per log beside it; what is new is that it stays after the page closes. A radio's 32-bit build has smaller table and string headers; it was not measured there.
- **The servo pages build their screen twice on opening instead of five times (`app/pages/setup/servos/pwm/page.lua`, `app/pages/setup/servos/bus/page.lua`, `ui/loading_overlay.lua`)**:
  - Opening *PWM Output* or *BUS Output* reads four things from the flight controller one after another, and each of the first three replies asked for a rebuild of the whole scene -- `lvgl.clear()`, the header and the loading box again -- only to move the loading bar a quarter further.
  - `LoadingOverlay.append` now takes `progress` as a number or as a function. Given a function, the bar's fill is a rectangle with a reactive `size` that the firmware reads on its own refresh pass, the way the header's connection button and Urban's bars follow their state; given a number it draws exactly what it drew before, so none of the other callers changes. Both pages pass their progress as a function and no longer rebuild for the first three replies. The build when the tile is pressed, the build of the finished page and every rebuild on a failed read stay.
  - Counted offline under desktop Lua 5.3 on the packaged tool, from the tile press until the page has settled, with the flight-controller stub of `bin/accounting` answering: scene builds 5 -> 2 on both pages; 67 869 -> 62 621 instructions and 383.5 -> 350.2 KB allocated on *PWM Output*, 68 072 -> 62 824 and 379.8 -> 346.6 KB on *BUS Output*. The same four reads go out in the same order, the page settles on the same pass, and the finished page's node tree is identical.
  - What can be seen to differ: the bar moves on the pass that processes a reply instead of on the rebuild one pass later, and it reaches its end for one refresh before the finished page replaces it.

- **Diagnostics → Info reads the telemetry link ratio once when it opens, and after that every 45 seconds instead of every 1.2 (`app/pages/tools/diagnostics/info/page.lua`, `docs/pages/tools/diagnostics/info.md`)**:
  - *Packet Ratio* is `crsf_telemetry_link_ratio` out of `MSP_TELEMETRY_CONFIG`, a setting the firmware reads when it sets up CRSF telemetry at boot, not a statistic of the live link. The page read it together with Variant, Board Info and Build Info when it opened, and its re-read asked for the same message on its own as soon as it found its timestamp still at 0 -- on the wakeup before that load started, or, where the load ran first, on the first one after it -- and then every 1.2 seconds for as long as the page stayed open: two reads of one message on every open, and about fifty a minute while the page stood.
  - The re-read now waits for the page's own load, counts from the read made there and runs at the page's 45-second refresh interval, so a change made by another client, such as a configurator on USB, still reaches the row while the page stays open. *Reload* in the header re-reads it at once, as before.
  - `bin/accounting` does not run the tool's pages. Counted offline instead, under desktop Lua 5.3 with a per-instruction hook on the packaged tree, from the press to the settled page: 101 737 -> 90 731 instructions, 405 -> 367 KB allocated, 8 -> 7 builds, 5 -> 4 MSP reads; standing on the page for 50 passes afterwards, 4 -> 0 reads and 4 117 -> 3 706 instructions per pass. Identical over three runs of each.
- **The tool opened from the dashboard loads its files the way the tool script does (`widgets/dashboard/tool_host.lua`, `docs/developer/dashboard-views.md`)**:
  - The tool script puts a loader over `loadScript` (`src/main.lua`) that uses the suite's load mode in place of the one a caller passes, so a current `.luac` beside a file is read instead of its source, and keeps every chunk but a page's own module until the tool closes. The pages, and most of the files they load, call `loadScript(path, "t")` and rely on that loader. Opened inside the dashboard widget, the tool ran in the widget's Lua state, which has no such loader: every page visit compiled the page and the shared files it loads that way from source again, and the bytecode EdgeTX writes beside each file was never read back, because `"t"` always loads the text.
  - The tool host now puts the same loader in place when it starts loading the tool and puts the replaced function back when the tool closes. Only paths under the suite's own folder go through it, because the widget's global table is shared with every other widget on the radio; any other path reaches the radio's loader as it was asked for. The kept chunks are let go on close. The tool script and the dashboard's own loading outside the tool are unchanged.
  - Counted offline under desktop Lua 5.3 with a stand-in for the radio's loader that records every call, opening *PIDs*, *Info*, *Ports* and *Modes* in the tool hosted by the dashboard: a second or third open compiled 5 to 10 files from source and now compiles none; on a card where every `.luac` is current, a first open compiled 8, 9, 11 and 11 files from source and now reads 6, 5, 4 and 7 as bytecode.
  - What it costs: Lua memory while the tool is open, because the kept chunks stay until it closes, as they do in the tool script's own state. On the EdgeTX simulator (TX15) the peak while the tool was open rose by about 140 kB, from about 2 335 kB to about 2 478 kB; after the tool closed, the heap was where it is on master.
- **Offline accounting: the tool's pages, as the dashboard hosts them (`bin/accounting/measure.lua`, `bin/accounting/stubs/edgetx.lua`, `bin/accounting/budgets.lua`, `bin/accounting/README.md`)**:
  - The dashboard runs the tool inside its own widget call (`widgets/dashboard/tool_host.lua`), so a page opened from the dashboard is built inside a call that is stopped at 20 000 instructions, and no row priced it. Every page in the tool's registry is now opened that way -- the tool opened through the host, the page reached by the tool's own tile presses along the manifest -- and the instructions inside its `build` get a row, `page.<menuId>.build`: 83 rows. The 18 pages that cannot be opened in the run's world are named in a note each: no menu leads to six of them, the ten ESC tool pages have their tiles disabled, and two are drawn as menus.
  - Three pages need more than 20 000 instructions for their build alone, on `src/` and on the English package both: *Ports* (27 401 on `src/`, 20 507 packaged), the setup wizard (43 805 / 38 109) and its radio section (32 957 / 28 754). Their targets hold today's figure with the margin `--emit` suggests and carry 20 000 as `proposed`, so the check is green and every run prints that they sit above it.
  - The rows are measured on `src/` like every other row, where a page resolves its strings at run time, so a row is a regression figure for the source as written rather than what the page costs on a radio: it reads higher than the packaged tool, by 0 to 225 % per page (median 15 %). The worst pass of an open is reported by `--pages` and not gated.
  - The stubs gain what the tool asks the radio and the dashboard rows never do (`getRSSI`, `getGeneralSettings`, `getDateTime`, `getSourceValue`, `dir`, `mkdir`, the model's inputs, outputs and modules, the lvgl layout figures, the telemetry unit numbers), installed for the page scenarios only. The 52 rows that were there are unchanged.
  - `--pages` opens every page at twenty phases of the cadence grid and prints the build's range, the worst pass and the open's total; a report, like `--phases`.
  - The self-test also poisons a page row and removes another page row's budget, and breaks the page driver three ways -- replies held off the link, a connect that is never answered, a press that never lands -- and fails unless each one stops the run.
- **Dashboard → Design lists the themes once instead of on every visit, and builds its debug lines only when the log level writes them (`app/pages/settings/dashboard/theme/page.lua`, `app/pages/settings/dashboard/lib.lua`, `docs/pages/settings/dashboard/theme.md`)**:
  - Listing the dashboard themes loads every theme folder's `init.lua` and looks up its icon. Closing *Design* dropped the list together with the library that cached it, so every visit listed the folders again. The list is now kept with the page until the tool drops the page from its cache of recently opened pages, or the tool is closed. The page has no *Reload*, so a theme copied onto the card while the tool is open is offered once the tool has been opened again; the page's documentation says so.
  - Every debug line of the page and of the dashboard settings library was concatenated before `Log.emit` dropped it, at every log level. Both now ask `Log.wanted("debug")` once where a function starts and format a line only when it will be written; the callers hand over the pieces. At `debug` the same lines are written as before, except the listing's own lines on a visit that lists nothing.
  - Opened from the dashboard, the page is built inside one widget call, which EdgeTX stops at its instruction limit of about 20 200; a stop inside the build shows the build failure page. Counted offline under desktop Lua 5.3 with a per-instruction hook on the packaged tree, with the flight-controller stub of `bin/accounting` answering, the page's build on the first visit and on a revisit: 9 691 -> 5 265 and 9 467 -> 2 805 with no preferences; 12 211 -> 6 305 and 11 987 -> 3 808 with the log at `info` and both of the page's switches on; 15 439 -> 15 265 and 15 215 -> 5 707 with the log at `debug` as well. The worst pass of a revisit at `debug`, over 20 phases of the tool's background work: 25 384 -> 15 878.
  - Not reached by this: with the log at `debug`, the first visit after the tool opens still lists the folders and writes every line, and is still stopped when opened from the dashboard.
  - Nothing on screen changes: the page builds the same nodes as before with each of those preferences.
- **A pass of the tool that builds a screen no longer also runs the periodic flight-controller and audio work (`ui/home.lua`)**:
  - A pass that builds the screen is the longest pass the tool runs, and the MSP tick -- with the event wakeup and the queue pump behind it -- and the audio tick could land on that same pass, depending only on where their timers happened to stand. On a pass that builds, they now wait for the next pass. Their timers are not advanced, so the work runs one pass late and none of it is dropped.
  - At most once in a row: on a second build in succession they run, so a screen that rebuilds on every pass still gets them on every other pass. The start screen is left out, because it rebuilds on every step of the connection and holding the MSP tick back there would slow the chain it is waiting for.
  - It matters most in the tool opened from the dashboard, where the pass is one widget call and EdgeTX stops it at the instruction limit. Counted offline under desktop Lua 5.3 with a per-instruction hook on the packaged tree, the flight-controller stub of `bin/accounting` answering, every page opened at 20 positions of the periodic timers, the worst pass of an open: -4 238 on the median of 95 pages; *Telemetry* 25 092 -> 17 638, *Alignment* 20 377 -> 16 131, the setup assistant's *Board* 21 046 -> 13 585, *Adjustments* 14 808 -> 10 521. The pages whose build alone is over the limit stay over it.
  - What it costs: a page reaches its settled state one pass later on 48 of 95 pages, and two to four passes later on 16 that ask for a rebuild after each reply, because each such rebuild holds back the tick that sends the next request. 31 pages settle on the same pass. Each page draws the same node tree as before, a page left standing runs the same background work on the same passes as before (counted on three pages over 50 passes), and the start screen connects in as many passes as before.
- **Translation coverage counts the literal-key calls the i18n precompiler leaves as calls (`bin/i18n/check_translations.py`, `docs/developer/i18n.md`) (fixes #447)**:
  - A third class, *not rewritten*, runs the tree's own `.vscode/scripts/precompile_i18n.py` over a temporary copy of `src/rfsuite` and counts every call with a literal key that is still a call afterwards: `pageText`, `t` or a wrapper declared as `function name(i18n, key, ...)` with the key second, `t` or `tr` with the key first. Line comments are cut first. A packaged card has no locale bundle, so such a call shows its English fallback, or nothing.
  - Its baseline is 50: 16 page titles that pass a variable fallback (`pageText(i18n, "title", displayTitle)`) and 34 `tr("key", "Fallback")` calls on the developer API tester page, whose keys have no dot. Before #561 the same count was 174.
  - The self-test runs a fixture page through the precompiler and expects exactly a wrapper call, a variable fallback and a dotless `tr` key. The check takes longer, for the temporary copy.
- **Setup > Alignment's Live View moves the model in place instead of rebuilding the page for every attitude reply, holds it still within 0.3 degrees, and draws a more recognisable helicopter (`app/pages/setup/alignment/page.lua`)**:
  - Each MSP_ATTITUDE reply -- four a second for the 60 s of Live View -- asked the host for a whole page build: every control, label and divider was cleared and created again, and the model was projected into new tables each time. Only the model and the four live texts change.
  - The reply now projects the model once into tables that are kept, and the model's triangles and lines read them through function-valued `pts` and `color`, which EdgeTX re-reads on each refresh and redraws only when the points changed. The live readout, the view yaw, the nose direction and the Live button's countdown are text functions. The page is still rebuilt when Live View starts or ends, after a read or a save, and on the star.
  - The model moves only when roll or pitch has changed by more than 0.3 degrees since it was last drawn, or yaw (whole degrees in MSP_ATTITUDE) has changed at all, so a board lying still does not redraw its faces on attitude noise. The readouts still follow every reply. Over a recorded sequence of 221 replies from a still board with 0.15 degrees of noise on roll and pitch, the model moved on 50 replies instead of 114.
  - The model's layers sit in boxes placed before the page's controls. EdgeTX recreates a triangle's canvas when its points change and puts it on top of its siblings, so each layer keeps its own drawing order, and the controls stay on top and keep their touches. When any face moves, every face is handed the other of two vertex orders that EdgeTX's rasteriser fills identically, so all of them are redrawn in far-to-near order.
  - The model: a slimmer pointed canopy, a boom tapering to the tail, vertical and horizontal fins, a tail rotor, a main rotor drawn as its disc rim with two blades, and skids with upturned front ends on bent cross tubes. It has the same 21 filled faces as before (the nose face stays the accent colour); the rest are lines, which EdgeTX moves in place, and one line object carries a whole polyline. 19 line objects instead of 33.
  - Counted offline under desktop Lua 5.3 with a per-instruction hook, over 40 attitude replies: a reply cost 13 760 instructions with its rebuild and now costs 7 291, and allocated 58.9 kB of Lua heap and now 2.2 kB. The function properties add about 110 instructions to each refresh. The model's points are rotated with one matrix per reply. `page.setup_alignment_page.build` moves from 16 113 to 12 627 in `bin/accounting/measure.lua`.
  - The left panel's three readout lines are spaced by the small font's measured height, so they no longer overlap at 800x480.
  - Not changed: the faces are still recreated by EdgeTX whenever the model moves, as they were on every rebuild before, so the firmware's own allocation for them per moving reply stays.
# 0.1.6

### Features & Enhancements
- **Documentation Structure & Maintenance Rule (`docs/`)**:
  - Added a `docs/` folder as the page-by-page reference for the tool, mirroring the menu layout: a page template, an index of every reachable page with its menu path and visibility conditions, section indexes for the dashboard, audio, reference, troubleshooting and developer guides, and two pages written as examples (`setup/model`, `setup/power/smartfuel`). The folder is not part of the installation archive.
  - Added the documentation maintenance rule (`GEMINI.md`, `.agents/rules/documentation.md`): every change a pilot can observe updates its documentation in the same PR, in the same shape as the `Releases.md` rule.
- **Dynamic ESC Model Identification & Interface Unification (`setup/esc_motors/esc_tools`)**:
  - Added dynamic ESC model name detection and header display queried directly from hardware via telemetry / MSP.
  - Unified device information and capability reporting across all supported ESC manufacturers (AM32, BLHeli_S, Bluejay, Flyrotor, Hobbywing V5, OMP, Scorpion, XDFly, YGE, ZTW).
  - Isolated Hobbywing V5 parameter tables to prevent cross-talk and corrected YGE firmware version mapping.
- **ESC Firmware Version & Serial Number / Revision Subheader Display (`setup/esc_motors/esc_tools`)**:
  - Implemented a compact subheader info line directly beneath the section header across all 10 ESC manufacturer configuration pages (AM32, BLHeli_S, Bluejay, Flyrotor, Hobbywing V5, OMP, Scorpion, XDFly, YGE, ZTW).
  - Provides persistent, at-a-glance visibility into firmware versions (`FW`), device serial numbers (`S/N`), and layout revisions (`Rev`) across all parameter tabs with minimal screen footprint (~18px).
  - Corrected YGE serial number extraction (`getEscVersion`) to parse the 32-bit uint serial number from protocol buffers.
- **Active Profile & Rate Profile Live Resolution (`flight_tuning`)**:
  - Centralized live profile resolution (`getLiveProfile()`) to read active PID and rate profiles directly from telemetry sensors and session state.
  - Profile adjustments made via transmitter switches or telemetry are instantly reflected across all flight tuning pages without desync.
- **Motor Override & ESC Motor Test Tool (`setup/esc_motors/motor_override`)**:
  - Added dedicated Motor Override tool allowing direct spool-up and functional testing of main and tail motors from the radio.
  - Multi-tier safety architecture: requires explicit disarm confirmation, hardware arming switch check, deadman timeout watchdog, manual throttle override sliders, and automatic shutoff upon page navigation or telemetry link loss.
- **SD Card Session Logging & Trace Layer (`rfsuite.log`)**:
  - Implemented structured multi-level logging (`trace`, `debug`, `info`, `warn`, `error`) with ring buffer sink writing session logs directly to SD card.
  - Added single-writer arbitration between configuration tool and background widgets to avoid file contention.
  - Emits timestamps, MSP transport traces, UI lifecycle events, and save refusal explanations.
- **MSP Live Telemetry & Accessors (`tasks/msp/api`)**:
  - Added modular MSP API endpoints for live analog sensor readings (`MSP_ANALOG`) and receiver channel inputs (`MSP_RC`).
  - Added self-identification tags for simulator responses in debug traces.
- **Startup Progress & Visual Preparation (`ui/home.lua`)**:
  - Added smooth animated preparation progress bar on the home start screen.
- **Flight Controller Reboot Policy Documentation (`tasks/msp/save_pipeline.lua`)**:
  - Embedded explicit Rotorflight 2 flight controller reboot policy guidelines directly into save pipeline headers and architecture documentation.
  - Formulated clear boundaries for mandatory reboots (hardware drivers, DMA, UART ports, radio config, ESC protocols, sensor alignment), conditional reboots (swash/tail geometry changes), and non-reboot live tuning (PIDs, rates, governor, filters).
- **Reactive LVGL Telemetry Bindings & Zero-Rebuild Dashboard Engine (`widgets/dashboard`)**:
  - Migrated telemetry labels, gauges, arcs, duration timers, governor indicators, and stats to reactive getter closures evaluated directly by EdgeTX's native `callRefs` loop in C++.
  - Pruned dynamic sensor data (`voltage`, `rpm`, `flightSeconds`, `fuel`, `lq`, etc.) from `Engine.renderKey`, eliminating continuous 2-Hz full-scene teardown and rebuild churn (`lvgl.clear()` + `lvgl.build()`).
  - Scene rebuilds are now strictly confined to layout mutations (flight mode changes `preflight` $\rightarrow$ `inflight` $\rightarrow$ `postflight`, theme switches, and zone resizing), resulting in zero memory allocations and minimal CPU overhead during steady-state flights.
- **MSP API Return Format Normalization (`tasks/msp/api`)**:
  - Standardized all 102 MSP API modules (`Api.parse`) to return a clean, flat table (`return { ... }`) rather than mixed wrapped tables (`{ parsed = ... }`), eliminating duplicate unnesting logic across servo, esc, and setup pages and improving API tester introspection.

### Bug Fixes & Improvements
- **Dashboard Model Preferences Cache Invalidation & Reload Decoupling (`widgets/dashboard`, `lib/model_preferences.lua`, `tasks/msp/runtime.lua`)** (fixes #186):
  - Fixed an issue where changing theme selection or model preference overrides in the suite did not update the active dashboard widget until a transmitter power cycle due to stale in-memory preferences and RAM cache lookups.
  - Decoupled disk I/O from theme module loading during preference reloads (`reloadPreferencesIfNeeded`) so reading INI files and building the theme occur on separate passes, eliminating EdgeTX CPU limit instruction budget faults.
  - Hardened reload lifecycle against mid-load faults: pre-cleared theme and memo state before disk reads, deferred baseline stamp/sequence adoption and pending flag clearance until after successful I/O, and protected active session preferences against stale MSP runtime republishes.
- **Initial Fuel Announcement Preference Authority & Redundant Flag Cleanup (`lib/audio.lua`, `app/pages/setup/model/page.lua`) — ref #191, ref #187**:
  - Established the radio preference (`Audio / Events -> Battery -> Initial Fuel Announcement`, default: enabled) as the single source of truth for the initial fuel announcement upon connection.
  - Fixed `initialFuelWanted()` in `lib/audio.lua`, which previously consulted `FLAG_TELL_CAPACITY` from `model_flags` and silenced announcements for all pilots on default flight controller configurations (`model_flags = 0`).
  - Removed the redundant and conflicting `FLAG_TELL_CAPACITY` switch from `app/pages/setup/model/page.lua`, preventing dual-switch ambiguity where neither control could reliably silence the callout independently.
- **Dashboard Postflight Stats: Missing Handlers and Live-Value Fallthrough (`widgets/dashboard`) — fixes [#133](https://github.com/rotorflight/rotorflight-lua-edgetx-suite/issues/133)**:
  - Added per-flight tracking for `currentFlightMaxVoltage`/`lastFlightMaxVoltage`, `currentFlightMaxLq`/`lastFlightMaxLq`, `currentFlightMinBecVoltage`/`lastFlightMinBecVoltage`, and `lastFlightEndingVoltage` in `runtime.lua` (`updateDerivedFlightState`).
  - Added stat handlers in `objects/text/stats.lua` for `max`+`voltage` (MAX VOLTAGE tile in `rfstatus` theme), `max`+`link` (LINK MAX in `@rt-rc-n`), `min`+`link` (LINK MIN in `@srb-rc`), and `last`+`voltage` (ENDING VOLTAGE in `@srb-rc`).
  - Fixed `min`+`bec_voltage` handler to use the dedicated BEC minimum accumulator (`lastFlightMinBecVoltage`) instead of incorrectly returning the main pack minimum voltage.
  - Removed the `/2` transform workaround from `@aerc-n/postflight.lua`'s MIN BEC VOLTAGE tile; the accumulator now tracks the actual BEC minimum directly.
  - Fixed stats handlers to prioritize current in-flight values over previous flights (`currentFlight… or last…`), and removed live sensor fallbacks so tiles render `--` before the first flight instead of leaking live readings.
  - Made any unresolvable `(stattype, source)` pair render `--` instead of silently falling through to the live telemetry value, turning future theme typos into immediately visible blank values.
- **Dashboard Fullscreen Menu EEPROM Commit Write Flag (`widgets/dashboard/fullscreen_menu.lua`)** (fixes #135):
  - Added explicit `isWrite = true` flag to the queued EEPROM commit after a battery profile change, ensuring the message with an empty payload is recognized as a write rather than defaulting to a read request. Dispatches as an MSP write frame (`CRSF_FRAMETYPE_MSP_WRITE`) on CRSF and properly triggers MSP response cache invalidation.
- **Dashboard ESC Temperature Gauge Arc Coloring (`widgets/dashboard/objects/gauge.lua`)** (fixes #132):
  - Fixed `getArcValueColor` falling through to the LiPo cell-voltage branch for temperature sources (`esc_temp`, `mcu_temp`), which divided the temperature value by the estimated battery cell count and applied ascending (low = bad) threshold logic — causing cold ESC temperatures to display as critical (red) and hot temperatures as OK (green).
  - Added explicit temperature source detection (`isTempSource`) or temperature unit fallback (`°C`/`°F`) with inverted threshold coloring: low/cool = green, high/hot = orange/red.
  - Temperature thresholds resolve from box properties (`warntemp`/`alerttemp`), theme configuration (`esctemp_warn`/`esctemp_alert`), or sensible defaults (90 °C warn, `max(warn + 15, 105)` °C alert; when alert threshold defaults, it is clamped to gauge maximum to remain reachable on custom scales).
  - Preserved valid cold / freezing temperatures (<= 0 °C) in OK green and properly distinguish missing telemetry (`curHasValue == false`) for arc background coloring.
  - Passed temperature, unit, and scale flags directly from `renderArc` to avoid redundant lookups and eliminated unit/source mismatches under Fahrenheit mode.
  - Eliminated dependency on battery cell count for temperature arc coloring.
- **Dashboard Stats Precedence, BEC Voltage Correction & Governor Optimization (`widgets/dashboard`)**:
  - **Stats Box Flight Precedence & BEC Voltage Correction**: Corrected stat value precedence in `widgets/dashboard/objects/text/stats.lua` so current flight extremes (`currentFlight...`) take precedence over previous flight aggregates (`lastFlight...`) while in flight, cleanly falling back to previous flight values once disarmed. Resolved a copy-paste error in the `bec_voltage` branch where main battery pack voltage (`lastMinVoltage`) was displayed instead of BEC voltage, and implemented in-flight minimum BEC voltage tracking (`currentFlightMinBecVoltage` / `lastMinBecVoltage`) in `runtime.lua`. Note: On setups without a dedicated BEC voltage sensor, this box now correctly reports `0` instead of falling back to the main flight battery pack voltage.
  - **Governor Disable-Flag Translation Deferral**: Deferred `translate()` resolution in `widgets/dashboard/objects/text/governor.lua` until after verifying the respective bit is actually set in `armDisableFlags`, eliminating up to 26 string lookups per rebuild.
- **Dashboard Memory & Loader Cleanup (`widgets/dashboard`, `lib/sensors`, `setup/esc_motors/esc_tools/escmfg/am32`)**:
  - Removed uncalled legacy builder and card functions (~330 lines) from `widgets/dashboard/themes/default/common.lua`, significantly reducing RAM footprint on radios.
  - Implemented missing negative caches (`missingRenders`) in `objects/dial.lua`, `objects/time.lua`, and `objects/image.lua` to eliminate redundant disk I/O / `loadScript` lookups on every scene rebuild for absent subrenderers.
  - Removed duplicate and shadowed `SIM_SENSOR_PATHS` definition in `lib/sensors.lua`.
  - Removed obsolete and unreachable `am32/pages.lua` file containing non-functional require statements.
- **Dashboard Widget i18n Status Line & Fullscreen Menu Fallback (`widgets/dashboard`)**:
  - Localized the soft timeout connection splash status line (`"Connected with partial telemetry"`) via `widgets.dashboard.connected_partial_telemetry`.
  - Corrected the degenerate fallback translator function signature in `fullscreen_menu.lua` from `function(_, k, f)` to `function(k, f)` so fallback label strings are properly returned instead of `nil` when i18n context is unavailable.
- **Telemetry Sensor Status Initialization (`tasks/events/onconnect`, `app/pages/tools/diagnostics`)**:
  - Load telemetry configuration during the connection chain and distinguish an unloaded configuration from a validation failure, preventing a false `ERROR` before the first sensor read.
- **Housekeeping Queue Starvation & Dependency Loading Order (`tasks/msp/runtime.lua`, `queue.lua`)**:
  - Eliminated the staggered module-loading race condition where `UID` dependencies finished loading before `API_VERSION` dependencies, inadvertently seizing the transmit slot and starving the connect chain.
  - Held `UID` read back until `API_VERSION` is successfully received and verified, ensuring primary flight controller compatibility is established before secondary model preference lookups.
  - Bounded housekeeping reads with tighter timeouts (1.5s) and retry budgets (max 2–3 retries) with backoff re-arming upon failures.
  - Enhanced `Queue:clear()` to supply distinct `"cleared"` error reason to callbacks, allowing callers to differentiate queue drops from transport timeouts.
- **Adjustments & Modes AUX Channel Bounds (`setup/controls/adjustments`, `setup/controls/modes`)**:
  - Capped AUX channel selection pickers and auto-detection loops at the firmware limit `AUX 1` .. `AUX 13` (`MAX_AUX_CHANNEL_COUNT = 13` = `MAX_SUPPORTED_RC_CHANNEL_COUNT - CONTROL_CHANNEL_COUNT`), removing unreachable `AUX 14` .. `AUX 20` entries.
  - Added strict write payload and sanitization clamping against `AUX_CHANNEL_COUNT - 1` (indices 0..12) to prevent out-of-bounds array indexing in flight controller receiver inputs.
- **Onconnect API Version Task Disconnect Sentinel Handling (`tasks/events/onconnect/tasks/apiversion.lua`)**:
  - Fixed an issue where the `apiversion` onconnect task immediately completed on reconnect due to the disconnect sentinel `"0"` being truthy and non-empty.
  - Added explicit check rejecting `"0"` alongside `nil` and `""` so the connect chain properly waits for a verified MSP API version read.
  - Implemented `M.reset()` on the `apiversion` task to ensure clean state resets across connect/disconnect cycles.
- **Unified Visual Language & Save/Notice Modal Consolidation (`ui/home.lua`, `controls/modes`, `controls/adjustments`, `dashboard/theme`, `splash.lua`)**:
  - Eliminated conflicting visual vocabularies during save operations where native EdgeTX blue message dialogs (`lvgl.message`) were raised over the suite's flat in-progress save overlay, which previously blocked the Lua `run()` loop and required keyboard dismissal.
  - Standardized all save outcome reporting (success and failure) to route through `ctx.reportSave()` and `reportSaveOutcome()`, displaying unified non-blocking notice overlays with touch- and key-navigable dismissal.
  - Replaced native modal alerts in `modes`, `adjustments`, and `theme` settings with in-suite notices (`ui.notice` / `LoadingOverlay.appendNotice`), ensuring the run loop advances smoothly and UI rebuilds take place immediately.
  - Harmonized the dashboard widget connection splash (`widgets/dashboard/splash.lua`) color palette with the core tool start screen using semantic theme tokens (`COLOR_THEME_PRIMARY3`, `COLOR_THEME_PRIMARY2`), creating a unified visual identity across standalone tools and telemetry widgets.
- **Decoupled FBL Connection State & Menu Tile Gating (`ui/home.lua`, `tasks/msp/runtime.lua`, `tasks/events/runtime.lua`, `app/pages/tools/diagnostics/rfstatus`)**:
  - Decoupled `fblConnected` (true Flight Controller MSP handshake) from `rfConnected` / `session.isConnected` (pure RF telemetry link presence).
  - Resolved an issue where main menu tiles (`enabledWhen = "fblConnected"`) unlocked 0.6s after RF link connection based on RSSI alone before any MSP reply arrived from the flight controller.
  - Required verified API version handshake (`versionReadCompleted == true` and valid `apiVersion`) before unlocking FC-dependent configuration tiles, keeping tiles safely locked when an FC is unpowered, booting, or silent.
- **Unresponsive Flight Controller Diagnosis & Connect Timeout (`ui/home.lua`, `tasks/msp`, `widgets/service`)**:
  - Unblocked the initial startup screen when a flight controller is silent (e.g. freshly flashed board with `FEATURE_TELEMETRY` disabled, or misconfigured MSP port), dismissing the loading spinner after ~6.0s instead of hanging indefinitely.
  - Automatically displays an actionable diagnostic notice explaining the root cause (*"No MSP reply from flight controller. Check that FEATURE_TELEMETRY is enabled and the MSP serial port is configured."*) and providing ELRS packet rate/ratio hints.
  - Reduced initial `API_VERSION` query timeout from 5.0s to 1.5s with 3 retries for rapid failure detection (~6s total) and exposed `session.mspLastError` and `session.mspErrorKind` across session and diagnostics state.
  - Updated the service widget to clearly report `"No MSP reply"` rather than `"Loading data... (0/5)"` when telemetry link is active but the flight controller remains silent.
- **Start Screen Bounded Wait & Startup Race Elimination (`ui/home.lua`)**:
  - Enforced an upper bound (max 2.0s offline, max 3.5s online) on the initial startup screen, preventing indefinite hangs when connecting to powered receivers with unresponsive flight controllers or wedged MSP links.
  - Eliminated the 0.6s timer race (0.45s vs 8.75s) by resolving the start screen as soon as core MSP identity (API version + MCU UID) is established, allowing onconnect tasks to run asynchronously in the background.
  - Replaced input-sensitive `state.lastInputTick` with dedicated `state.initialLoadStartTick` so key presses or touch gestures during boot do not restart the timeout timer.
  - Kept FBL-dependent menu tiles locked if startup times out without flight controller response, allowing pilots to access diagnostic tools instead of a frozen screen.
- **Diagnostics & Info Stalled Read Handling (`app/pages/tools/diagnostics/info`, `fblstatus`)**:
  - Added proactive timeout monitoring in `M.wakeup()` using `AsyncLoadUi.isTimedOut()` to abort stalled reads after 12s with clear modal feedback.
  - Added automatic link-loss detection and offline reload feedback notices.
  - Standardized per-request timeouts (3.5s) across diagnostic endpoints and fixed require path in `fblstatus/page.lua`.
- **ESC Configuration Save Timeout & Give-Up Notice (`app/pages/setup/esc_motors/esc_tools`)**:
  - Reduced ESC write timeout from 5.0s to 2.5s and added user-facing failure notice overlay on give-up so pilots are immediately informed when an ESC fails to acknowledge parameter writes.
- **Flight Log Selector Row Overlap & Sort Order (`app/pages/logs/page.lua`)**:
  - Fixed row layout height calculations in the flight log chooser, eliminating vertical text overlapping and clipping on compact displays.
  - Sorted flight logs in descending chronological order (newest files listed first).
- **Settings & Preferences Surface Consolidation (`lib/preferences.lua`, `settings/general`)**:
  - Consolidated `CONFIG_SCHEMA` in `settings/general` as single source of truth for save confirmations and disarm warnings.
  - Pruned orphaned preference keys (`txbatt_type`, `theme_loader`, `hs_loader`, `toolbar_timeout`, `iconsize`, `syncname`, `audio_switches`, `audio_timer`) and removed unreferenced audio stubs.
  - Fixed temperature unit conversion (`useFahrenheit()`) in dashboard telemetry text and flight log summaries.
  - Applied `sag_gain` in SmartFuel load calculation.
- **UI Layout & Display Profile Metrics (`ui/controls.lua`, `ui/display_profile.lua`)**:
  - Harmonized row metrics, reduced excessive vertical spacing, and widened multi-column numeric input fields across standard and high-resolution color radios (such as TX15 and TX16S).
  - Stripped UTF-8 BOM encoding from Lua files to avoid syntax errors on EdgeTX Lua interpreters.
- **Navigation Exit Debounce (`ui/home.lua`)**:
  - Debounced back-button navigation (`onBack`) to prevent rapid or repeated key events from causing accidental double-exit jumps from menus.
  - Stamped `lastBackTick` strictly on actual navigation transitions.
- **Battery Reserve Overwrite Protection (`setup/power/battery/page.lua`)**:
  - Fixed an issue where saving battery configuration without modifications could overwrite `cbat_alert_percent` (battery capacity reserve).
  - Removed unsafe preference seeding and ensured `batteryConfig` is safely populated and preserved on save.
- **Theme Color Token Normalization (`ui/controls.lua`, widgets)**:
  - Replaced hardcoded `GREY_DEFAULT` / nil color tokens with semantic theme color tokens across UI controls and widgets, fixing invisible text and rendering artifacts under custom color themes.
- **Help View Paragraph Spacing & Height Estimation (`ui/help_view.lua`)**:
  - Removed duplicate paragraph spacing in `estimateWrappedTextHeight`, aligning text container height estimation with native EdgeTX LVGL label rendering (48 px for 2 paragraphs instead of 80 px) and eliminating unnecessary scrollable whitespace.
- **FC Version Simulator Fixture Sync (`tasks/msp/api/fc_version.lua`)**:
  - Updated `fc_version` mock fixture from `{ 4, 5, 1 }` (RF 2.2.1) to `{ 4, 6, 0 }` (Rotorflight 2.3.0 / FC 4.6.0), synchronizing mock telemetry with `build_info.lua`.
- **UI Controls, Heights & Display Spacing (`ui/controls.lua`)**:
  - Unified native LVGL widget heights (standard 36 px), font rounding, and vertical alignments across all pages (PIDs, Rates, Mixer, Modes, Failsafe, Governor, Trims).
  - Resolved wide-display layout spacing and boundary clipping on large color touchscreens (e.g. 800x480).
- **Settings Lifecycle & Unsaved Changes Tracking (`settings/common.lua`)**:
  - Integrated all six settings form pages (`audio/events`, `audio/switches`, `audio/timer`, `dashboard/theme`, `general`, `localization`) into the suite's `ui.dirty` unsaved changes lifecycle.
  - Introduced non-rebuilding `runtime.markValueChanged()` for inline numeric controls to prevent focus loss during scrollwheel edits, alongside `runtime.markDirty()` for structure-altering toggles.
  - Added reusable `getValueSetter(key)` helper in form runtime.
- **Combo Selector Unknown Value Fallback (`ui/controls.lua`)**:
  - Replaced misleading fallback to the first option when an unknown value is returned from hardware with a dynamic "Unknown (%s)" / "Unbekannt (%s)" placeholder.
  - Added defensive guard in `set()` to ensure selecting or confirming the placeholder is a safe no-op.
- **Notice Dialog Bounded Dimensions (`ui/loading_overlay.lua`)**:
  - Fixed word-wrap line calculation (`countMessageLines`) against `innerW` matching LVGL layout behavior.
  - Added hard geometric bounds (`roomForLines`) and UTF-8 safe bisection truncation with ellipsis (`...`), guaranteeing dialog action buttons (OK/Dismiss) never run off the screen.
- **ESC Configuration (YGE, Hardware Protection)**:
  - Consolidated YGE model names and capabilities into a single source of truth (`escModels`) and added support for **YGE Saphir 125 V2** (ID `4691`) with 12V BEC capability.
  - Fixed silent UI display and save clamping on 10.0V BEC settings for 12V-capable YGE ESCs.
  - Restored `Keep mAh` (flags bit 2) and `Startup Response` (`acceleration` U16) controls on the YGE ESC configuration page.
  - Prevented full-page scene teardown and preserved active inline editors when adjusting ESC parameters.
- **SmartFuel Reserve Consolidation (`lib/smartfuel_reserve.lua`)**:
  - Consolidated SmartFuel capacity reserve arithmetic into a single centralized module, eliminating divergent calculations between telemetry widgets and diagnostics.
- **Model Name & Preference Persistence Across Updates**:
  - Prevented installer packages and deploy tasks from overwriting user `preferences.ini`.
  - Moved Model Sync settings directly onto the Model page (`setup/model`).
  - Fixed cold-start restore to prevent temporary model names from restoring after cold boots or telemetry dropouts.
  - Restricted model writes strictly to widget runtimes.
- **Save Callback Error Handling (`tasks/msp/save_pipeline.lua`)**:
  - Wrapped page save callbacks in `pcall` guards to capture errors and report them cleanly in the notice box.
- **Dialog Fallback Handling (`ui/controls.lua`)**:
  - Properly recognized asynchronous `lvgl.confirm` dialogs as active modals, preventing premature fallback invocation.
- **Session Logs Text Overlap & Contrast Fix (`app/pages/tools/diagnostics/session_logs/page.lua`)** (fixes #91):
  - Fixed text overlap / overdrawing: log entries are now truncated with an ellipsis (`…`) via binary-search `fitToWidth` to guarantee each line occupies exactly one `rowH` row, regardless of message length.
  - Fixed invisible / low-contrast text on light EdgeTX themes: added a solid `COLOR_THEME_PRIMARY3` background panel for the log view and replaced `GREY_DEFAULT` (white/near-white on light themes) with `COLOR_THEME_DISABLED` for `debug` and `trace` log levels, guaranteeing readable contrast on all EdgeTX themes (including light blue and unstyled page backgrounds).
  - Dynamically derived `rowH` and `maxVisible` from font metrics via `textSize("Ag", SMLSIZE)` instead of assuming a fixed height, correctly adapting across 320x240, standard, and 800x480 radio display classes without overdrawing or boundary clipping.
- **Runtime Locale Resolution & Language Selector (`lib/system_locale.lua`, `settings/localization/page.lua`, `lib/preferences.lua`) – fixes #103**:
  - `system_locale.lua:resolveSystemLanguage()` previously returned the build-time template placeholder `@i18n_language@` verbatim at runtime in uncompiled / simulator mode. `I18n.new("@i18n_language@")` failed to load any bundle and silently fell back to English, causing all Settings › General labels and confirmation dialogs to appear in English regardless of the selected language.
  - Introduced a three-step runtime fallback: ① read `language` from `[localizations]` in `preferences.ini` (set via Settings › Localization); ② read the EdgeTX `LANGUAGE` global (where exposed by the firmware); ③ caller-supplied default (`"en"`). The `@i18n_language@` marker is retained as a precompile token only – packaged release builds still get the locale baked in at build time, while source/simulator runs now correctly pick up the user's chosen language.
  - Added a **Language** dropdown (English / German) to Settings › Localization so users can set their preferred UI language; the choice is persisted in `preferences.ini` under `[localizations] language` and takes effect on the next tool open.
  - Added `language = "en"` to `lib/preferences.lua` defaults so the key is always present in newly created `preferences.ini` files.
- **Save & Reload Interlock, Non-Blocking Feedback & Local Settings While Armed (`ui/home.lua`, `i18n`, fixes #136)**:
  - Header Save and Reload actions remain interactive on pages that declare them instead of forcing them disabled in the header layout, ensuring user input reliably routes to `onSave` / `onReload`.
  - When the modal warning preference is disabled (`save_armed_warning = false`), tapping Save or Reload provides immediate non-blocking feedback by temporarily updating the top armed banner to `"Save blocked: Model is ARMED!"` / `"Reload blocked: Model is ARMED!"` for 2.5 seconds before auto-reverting, keeping the page visible and navigable.
  - Relabeled the safety switch in Settings › General to "Show Disarm Warning on Save/Reload" to reflect that it governs armed notices for both operations.
  - Unblocked saving on local tool settings pages (`settings_*` such as Preferences, Audio, Localization) while armed, as these pages do not communicate with the FC via MSP.
  - Saving FC parameters while armed remains strictly blocked in all cases.

### Performance, Memory & Build System
- **Tile & Theme Icon Pre-Scaling to 40x40 (`app/pages`, `widgets/dashboard/themes`)**:
  - Pre-scaled all 148 page/tile icons and 7 theme icons to exact 40x40 px using Lanczos resampling.
  - Reduced C-heap decode buffer allocation from 19.6 kB to 6.4 kB per PNG decode (3.1x reduction), reducing LVGL image cache footprint from ~78 kB to ~26 kB and eliminating LVGL heap exhaustion (`ta` error tiles) on image-heavy menus.
  - Cut total asset disk footprint by 51.3% and eliminated software zoom/scaling overhead on 800x480 displays.
- **Codebase GC & Memory Cleanup (`allowMemAutoRefresh`)**:
  - Removed 60 obsolete `allowMemAutoRefresh()` function stubs across all page modules and dashboard themes to reduce garbage collection churn and memory overhead.
- **Incremental Bytecode Compilation & Build Identity (`lib/precompile.lua`, `bin/package/build_package.py`)**:
  - Implemented incremental startup bytecode compilation checking file modification timestamps (`fstat`) with 2-second FAT tolerance against bytecode timestamps, only recompiling modified or stale Lua sources.
  - Integrated deterministic SHA-256 build identity hashing (`build.txt`), ensuring complete cache invalidation between releases.
- **Early Link-Ready Dashboard Rendering (`widgets/dashboard`)**:
  - Render telemetry dashboard immediately upon establishing the RF/MSP telemetry link without waiting for the full multi-step initialization chain.
- **Startup Pacing & Queue Optimization**:
  - MSP queue runner takes a second pass immediately after callers populate it, reducing round-trip latency.
  - Background task ticks twice as fast during initial connection startup.
  - Eliminated redundant readiness holds on initial connect and validated battery readiness using explicit status flags.
- **Bytecode & Loader Caching**:
  - Precompiled Lua bytecode cache at start and eliminated obsolete unread bytecode writes.
- **Build & Deployment Tooling**:
  - Fixed Python runtime detection across Windows and POSIX environments.
  - Added robust language discovery supporting VS Code JSONC user settings, profiles, and workspace settings.

# 0.1.5

### Features & Enhancements
- **Save Pipeline & Reboot Synchronization (`tasks/msp/save_pipeline.lua`)**:
  - Implemented a unified asynchronous save pipeline with automatic post-save reboot synchronization (`MSP_SET_REBOOT`) and response verification.
  - Replaced uncoordinated native dialog popups with integrated status reporting directly inside the `LoadingOverlay` notice box.
  - Added direct error and save refusal reporting, notifying the user when and why a write was rejected.
- **Dedicated Background Service Widget (`widgets/rfsuitesvc`) & Settings**:
  - Added standalone `rfsuitesvc` background service widget running MSP communication and telemetry tasks independently of the main configuration tool.
  - Added dedicated Service settings page (`settings/service`) for background link configuration.
- **Model Configuration & On-Connect Synchronization (`setup/model`, `tasks/events/onconnect`)**:
  - Added Model Configuration page (`setup/model`) to inspect and edit flight controller model name and pilot configuration parameters.
  - Implemented background synchronization tasks (`model_name_sync`, `model_params_sync`) to mirror FC model parameters onto the radio.
  - Added support for signed pilot config parameters (`MSP_PILOT_CONFIG`).
- **In-Flight Live Adjustment Announcements (`telemetry_bg/adjustments`)**:
  - Added real-time voice feedback when adjustments (e.g. gains, rates, governor trims) are changed via transmitter switches or rotary knobs during flight.
- **Interactive Telemetry Log Plotting (`diagnostics/flight_logs`)**:
  - Added graphical plotting of telemetry log channels against flight time.
  - Sized the plot view accurately against the active page body rather than the full display height.
- **Armed-State Safety & Visual Feedback (`ui/home.lua`, `ui/tiles.lua`)**:
  - Replaced blocking full-screen armed warning with an intuitive status strip across the home menu and locked tile badges.
  - Prevented MSP write and save actions while the craft is armed while maintaining passive telemetry draining.

### Bug Fixes & Improvements
- **Servos Configuration (`setup/servos/bus`, `setup/servos/pwm`)**:
  - Corrected bus servo addressing across three distinct index spaces (hardware ID, logical servo index, protocol slot).
  - Switched from full-table queries to individual servo queries (`get_servo_config`) to prevent telemetry RX buffer overflow.
  - Extracted `servo_count` directly from status reply payloads.
- **Adjustment Ranges Paging (`setup/controls/adjustments`)**:
  - Implemented paged reading of adjustment tables (`MSP_ADJUSTMENT_RANGE`) on compatible firmware versions to prevent unread configurations from displaying as empty tables.
- **ESC Configuration Hardening (YGE, Hobbywing V5, Scorpion, FlyRotor)**:
  - Fixed 8-byte parameter payload alignment for YGE ESCs.
  - Preserved byte-exact Hobbywing V5 device info blocks during save operations.
  - Respected FlyRotor byte-order endianness flag and prevented unintentional serial number overwrites.
